# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

gBASIC is a modern BASIC-family language implemented as a tree-walking
interpreter in C11. The single binary `gbasic` lexes, parses, and evaluates
`.bas`/`.gb` source files (both extensions are used interchangeably). Version is
`0.10.0`. See `README.md` for the language feature surface and `docs/` for
design/reference documents.

## Build & run

```sh
make                       # build ./gbasic (runs bison, then cc)
make clean && make         # full rebuild
./gbasic program.bas       # run a program
./gbasic --tokens program.bas   # dump lexer tokens
./gbasic --ast program.bas      # dump parsed AST
./gbasic --add-loads program.bas # print source with suggested `load` lines
./gbasic --json-diagnostics program.bas # emit parse/runtime diagnostics as JSON on stderr
./gbasic --line-buffered program.bas # flush stdout per completed line (pipes block-buffer by default)
./gbasic --version
sudo make install          # install to /usr/local (binary + stdlib); PREFIX overridable
```

Build requires a C11 compiler, `make`, and `bison`. Optional native modules are
detected at build time via `pkg-config` and gated behind `HAVE_*` macros in the
`Makefile` (`HAVE_LIBPQ`, `HAVE_SQLITE3`, `HAVE_ODBC`, `HAVE_LDAP`, `HAVE_LIBCURL`,
`HAVE_LIBXCRYPT`, `HAVE_LIBCRYPTO`, `HAVE_LIBXML2`, `HAVE_QT`). The interpreter always builds; a missing dependency turns the
affected feature into a clean runtime error rather than a build failure. When
adding code that touches an optional library, guard it with the matching
`#if HAVE_*` block (the existing module code in `src/eval.c` is the pattern).

`GBASIC_PATH=stdlib` tells the interpreter where to resolve `load`ed standard
libraries (`stdlib/*.bas`); set it when running from the source tree, e.g.
`GBASIC_PATH=stdlib ./gbasic examples/gui/demo.bas`. After `make install` this is
unnecessary: the install path (`$(PREFIX)/share/gbasic/stdlib`, default
`/usr/local/share/gbasic/stdlib`) is compiled in as `GBASIC_DEFAULT_STDLIB` and
searched as a fallback when `GBASIC_PATH` is unset or doesn't resolve a library.
`GBASIC_PATH` still wins when set, so it remains the dev override.

## Architecture

The pipeline is the classic lex → parse → AST → walk, but note where the weight
sits:

- `src/lexer.c` (`include/lexer.h`) — hand-written lexer. Has stateful modes
  (`modifier_content_mode`, `lens_content_mode`, `consider_depth`) because
  modifier `(...)=` clauses and `consider` blocks need context-sensitive
  tokenization. Token list lives in `lexer.h`.
- `src/parser.y` — Bison grammar (`-d` generates `parser.tab.c`/`.h`). Builds the
  AST. Regenerated automatically by `make` when `parser.y` changes.
- `src/ast.c` (`include/ast.h`) — AST node definitions and constructors.
  `AstStmtKind`/`AstExprKind` enumerate every node type.
- `src/eval.c` — **the entire runtime, ~18k lines.** This is the file you will
  most often edit. It contains the `Value` model, environment/scope handling,
  watchers, locks, error handling, the date/time/money/file value types, AND the
  full implementation of every optional module — `sqlite`, `pg` (PostgreSQL),
  `odbc` (any ODBC-reachable database), `ldap` (bind and search against a directory),
  `webclient`, `http` (the same requests non-blocking, over libcurl's multi
  interface, with readiness delivered by the webserver's event loop),
  `webserver`, `xml` (libxml2), the Qt `window` backend (`src/modules/qt.c`
  over `qt_backend.cpp`), and the libcrypto crypto builtins — each
  inside `#if HAVE_*` guards. Module dispatch (`sqlite.query`, `pg.exec`,
  `qt.open`, etc.) is resolved here, not in separate files.
- `src/builtins.c` (`include/builtins.h`) — **only a name registry.** A flat list
  of builtin function names so the parser/evaluator can recognize them. The
  actual implementations live in `src/eval.c`. To add a builtin: register the
  name here, implement the behavior in `eval.c`.
- `src/main.c` — CLI entry point and flag handling.

## Tests

Tests are golden-file based: a source file plus a sibling `.out` holding the
expected stdout. Output is compared verbatim (string equality), so update the
`.out` when intended output changes. Negative tests pair a `.bas` with a `.err`.

```sh
./tests/run_all.sh          # EVERY suite, discovered by GLOB. Use this as the gate; the per-suite lines below are for running one thing while working on it. Discovery is the whole point: run_ari, run_nap_fs and run_render sat BROKEN from rc6 to rc7 because the brace-modifier migration drove off `.bas` files and those three embed gBASIC in a shell heredoc -- and every gate anyone ran was a hand-maintained list that did not happen to name them, so nothing went red. run_datagrid was red for the same span for an unrelated reason (PLAT-WARN's unused-result firing on fixtures that discarded a returned handle) and was likewise unlisted. A gate you have to remember to extend is a gate that silently shrinks. **RUNS IN PARALLEL SINCE 2026-10-18 AND THE COVERAGE ARGUMENT IS UNCHANGED**: what this file forbids is CHOOSING suites by judgment, not running the same ones at once. Measured on a 22-core host, ~50 minutes serial against **16m 46s**, with 183 of 185 suites reporting IDENTICALLY to a serial baseline and the two that differ explained. It was serial for a CONCRETE reason -- every suite calls `make` and two gates relink ./gbasic underneath each other, observed 2026-08-25 as `timeout: failed to execute process` and `./gbasic: Permission denied`, failures that look exactly like regressions and are not -- and that is an argument about RELINKING: the gate builds once up front, so for all but a handful `make` is a NO-OP. THREE LANES, each suite DECLARING ITS OWN with a `# GATE-LANE:` line carrying a REASON after `--`, so the knowledge lives beside the thing it describes rather than in a table a new suite silently stays out of (the `.nolineno` sidecar arrangement). `exclusive` REBUILDS the tree and runs LAST, alone, tree restored afterwards; `serial` asserts an ABSOLUTE wall-clock bound and runs FIRST on an idle machine; everything else is PARALLEL across nproc/2 workers capped at 12. **WHY THE SERIAL LANE IS NARROW IS THE MEASUREMENT WORTH KEEPING: A RATIO IS SELF-NORMALISING** -- a shape tier comparing a 4x input against a baseline measures both halves under whatever load the machine carries, so it is unchanged when everything is uniformly slower, which is why run_arridx, run_recidx, run_compile and run_http's 2x concurrency gate are all parallel; an ABSOLUTE bound has no such defence (run_xlsx's 1200ms ceiling, run_persist's 5s, run_timer's known 0.80s schedule, run_repl's pty, run_web_hardening/run_web_stream's 1s budgets). TWO OF MY OWN MARKINGS WERE WRONG AND THE SUITES SAID SO: run_ari_discover's bound is `timeout 900` against a measured 356s under a comment reading "A HANG-GUARD, NOT A SPEED MEASUREMENT" -- the very distinction the lanes rest on, and it was the serial lane's entire 8-minute floor -- and run_core asserts `sleep(0.30)` took AT LEAST that, a LOWER bound load cannot break. **THE FIRST ATTEMPT WAS A NEGATIVE RESULT AND IS WHY THIS SHAPE IS TRUSTED**: ten suites that pass serially FAILED, with the 2026-08-25 messages verbatim plus a concurrent `src/parser.tab.o` compile. The premise was wrong -- only `make clean` suites were treated as hazardous, and run_packaging runs packaging/build-deb.sh which does `make -C "$here" clean` and a LEAN rebuild IN THIS TREE -- and the mechanism missed was that **once one suite changes the switch signature, every OTHER suite's plain `make` relinks the tree back**, so one undetected rebuilder turns all 170 no-op makes into relinkers. THERE ARE THEREFORE THREE DEFENCES AND THE SCAN IS THE WEAKEST: it grew through three generations (a literal `make clean`; then ONE HOP into an executed `packaging/*.sh` or `tools/*.sh`, which caught run_packaging; then WRITES TO A BUILD INPUT, which caught run_precedence doing `touch src/parser.y` and a plain `make` to count bison conflicts) and **each generation was found by the BEHAVIOURAL GUARD, not by reasoning** -- the gate checksums ./gbasic around every parallel suite and fails naming the lane violation, reported by EVERY suite that saw it since the culprit is among them and a victim's silent failure is indistinguishable from a regression. A scan here is necessary and never sufficient. The third generation's own first draft falsely flagged run_regex, which copies `src include Makefile` into a SCRATCH tree and edits `"$fb/src/eval.c"`, so it requires a RELATIVE path now. **EVIDENCE IS A DIFF, NOT A GREEN LINE**: verdicts print in GLOB order rather than completion order so a parallel run is comparable line-for-line against a serial one, and the comparison flags a new SKIP as loudly as a new FAIL. `GATE_JOBS=1` is the old behaviour exactly; a bash without `wait -n` runs serially and SAYS SO, the call failing instantly rather than waiting so a pool built on it would launch all 185 at once. Two bugs caught in the draft before it ran: `wait -n || wait` would have SERIALISED the rest of the gate the moment one suite exited nonzero, and the tally must be kept in the PARENT since a subshell's increments are lost. **`--changed` IS THE LESSER GATE AND IT IS DERIVED, NEVER A LIST** -- a second gate built from a list would reintroduce the exact defect this file exists to prevent. The SUBSTRATE RULE IS ABSOLUTE: touch Makefile, src/, include/, third_party/ or a shared harness and it runs EVERYTHING, naming the files that forced it, because src/eval.c IS the runtime and every suite runs ./gbasic. Otherwise it selects from three derived sources -- the suite's own file changed, the suite NAMES a changed path, or it declares `# GATE-SCOPE: whole-tree` (eight suites whose subject is a property of the TREE; run_absence's stdlib-wide loop census qualifies, a suite that merely loads libraries does not) -- plus one hop through `load` for a stdlib change. CALIBRATED ON A REAL MISS: a change that looked entirely Qt-local broke run_regex and run_packaging through the MAKEFILE, and a scan for "which suites NAME a changed path" selects both, which is why selection is by CONTENT rather than directory. Measured: a docs-only change runs 12 suites of 183 in under a minute. IT SAYS IT IS NOT THE GATE with the count it skipped, because the risk is SOCIAL -- a two-tier gate becomes one-tier the moment nobody notices the lesser one is partial -- and `GATE_BASE=HEAD` exists so the narrowing path can be EXERCISED at all, since against the upstream branch a session's own work includes the substrate and the escalation fires. WHAT IT CANNOT DO is on the page: it is a heuristic about BLAST RADIUS, and run_regex's dependency was "it copies a partial tree and runs make", which no file-name reasoning derives -- so it narrows the gate for ITERATION and the full gate is what gates a push. Reports a suite that SKIPPED ENTIRELY separately from one that passed -- a green line that ran no assertions is the other way a gate shrinks -- and NAMES the manual tier it excludes rather than dropping it quietly. Takes a substring filter (`tests/run_all.sh web`); SUITE_TIMEOUT and RUN_MANUAL override. THE PER-SUITE CAP IS A HANG-GUARD, NOT A SPEED MEASUREMENT, and its old 1800 was an idle-machine number -- measured 2026-09-23 with several gBASIC sessions sharing this host at load average 45, run_ari_discover takes 35 minutes AND PASSES, five past that cap, so a default invocation reported a suite that had not failed. It is 3600 now. Two tiers were calibrated the same way and fixed with it: run_ari_discover's fixture bound (180s against a MEASURED 356s, while reporting 192 checks and 0 mismatches) and tests/repl_pty.py, which waited for 0.2s of SILENCE to decide a chunk had finished -- a guess about how fast the machine is, where a scheduling stall reads as "done" and the driver types into the window that wait exists to avoid. It waits for the PROMPT to come back now, a positive signal that asks nothing about the machine, with the quiet window kept only as the fallback for a line that prints no prompt; its separate hard 5s wait for the FIRST prompt is gone, bounded by TOTAL_TIMEOUT like everything else. A gate that goes red because a colleague is building is a gate people learn to turn off, which is the failure this file exists to prevent
./tests/run_examples.sh     # baseline positive suite (runs `make clean && make` first)
./tests/run_library_depth.sh # WHERE A LIBRARY MAY COME FROM: beside the loading file, never below it -- a stray `chart.bas` three directories down used to replace the standard library, which for gdash meant `crypto` and `web`. The CONTROL is that a file BESIDE the loader still overrides, or this suite would be satisfied by a change that stopped local libraries working, and a deep match is still FOUND and REPORTED with the remedy rather than dropped in silence. PLUS THE UNPARSABLE-NEIGHBOUR TIER since 2026-09-23 (DOGFOOD 42): the walk parses every `.bas` beside the loader looking for a `library` block with the name, and a file it could not parse ABORTED the walk and became the message -- so with any half-written program in the directory `load money` answered `could not parse library file: ./broken.bas`, naming a file with nothing to do with money and losing the real answer. Hit for real in a scratch directory of sixty throwaway files, which is exactly a beginner's directory. A FILE THAT DOES NOT PARSE DOES NOT DEFINE THE LIBRARY BEING LOOKED FOR, which is all a name search needs to know, and its diagnostics go to a sink of their own so the innocent file is not named on stderr either. TWO CONTROLS, because "skip what does not parse" must not become either of its neighbours: a real library BESIDE the broken file is still found (the walk continues rather than giving up at the first failure), and a file the caller NAMED still fails loudly, because there the author said which file they meant. A THIRD CONTROL THE GATE FOUND RATHER THAN THE DESIGN: a file named AFTER THE LIBRARY is not an innocent bystander. run_warning_model's collision tier writes a library file it deliberately makes unparsable, and the first version of this quieted it -- so `watchers.bas` failing to parse while `load watchers` was what asked went from a parse error naming the file to `library not found`, hiding the author's own syntax error behind a sentence about something else. Naming a file `<library>.bas` is how you say which file you meant, the same as `from "..."`, so it fails loudly too.
./tests/run_negative.sh     # error/diagnostic suite. CARRIES THE RESERVED-WORD TIER since 2026-09-23 (DOGFOOD 43): a syntax error names the word, in the spelling the AUTHOR typed rather than the one the grammar uses -- nobody should have to know that `EACH` is their own word. `on = 0` reported `unexpected OP_EQ, expecting IDENT or ERROR_VALUE`, which is about the `=`, the one token on that line that is NOT the problem. THREE SHAPES AND EACH NEEDS ITS OWN RULE, because the word is in a different place each time and only one of the three has an expected list to work from: it IS the unexpected token (`function f(a, each)`); it is the token BEFORE a `=` (`on = 0`); or it is STATEMENT-INITIAL (`each = 5`, `to = 1`, `step = 3`), where bison offers no expected list at all and the SOURCE is asked whether an `=` follows -- read-only, one character, since the `=` has not been lexed yet and the reserved word IS the lookahead. THE CONTROLS OUTNUMBER THE POSITIVES ON PURPOSE: a note firing on every syntax error near a keyword would be wrong more often than right, and a diagnostic that is sometimes a lie is worse than a terse one. `for i = 1 to` catches generalising the assignment rule (`to` is correct there and nobody tried to name anything) and `then x = 1` catches dropping the `=` requirement -- MEASURED, since the first draft of that comment credited the wrong control, and the pre-existing negative_do_loop_until golden catches the second too. ONE GOLDEN MOVED and it is the best endorsement available: negative_until_as_name exists to say `until` cannot be a variable name, and the message now says so.
./tests/run_repl.sh        # THE PROMPT (`gbasic` with no arguments, or `--repl`; src/repl.c over a session split in src/eval.c). A prompt is NOT a smaller interpreter -- it is the same one with its LIFETIME TURNED INSIDE OUT: `eval_program` opens an environment, runs one root and releases everything, so calling it per line makes every variable vanish between lines. The split is `gb_session_open` / `gb_session_run` / `gb_session_close`, and the teardown is EXTRACTED rather than copied (`runtime_teardown`), because a second copy in another file is a second thing that can forget a module -- and a forgotten one here does not leak, it leaves a LISTENER BOUND after the session ended. The session OWNS every chunk's AST for its lifetime (`function_register` stores the AstStmt*, not a copy) and its `active_root` spans EVERY line typed so far, which is what lets `load util` on one line find a `library util` declared on an earlier one. SELF-CHECKING NOT GOLDEN AND FORCED: every defect a prompt can have is an ORDINARY-LOOKING TRANSCRIPT -- a session that forgets a variable reads as a user who mistyped a name, a `run` that reuses the session prints a number one too large, and a resident program that recorded the questions you asked still lists as a program and still runs. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS QUESTION_NOT_PROGRAM: `sq(3)` at a prompt is an ENQUIRY and `setup()` is an INSTRUCTION, so the recording decision is made AFTER the chunk runs. WHAT SEPARATES THEM IS NOT WHETHER AN ANSWER CAME BACK, and believing it was cost the prompt half of what a session did, from the day it shipped until the book found it four days later (DOGFOOD 22, fixed 2026-09-23): the documented rule is that a line which ACTS is kept and one which MERELY ANSWERS is not, and `append(nodes, x)` and `write(f, text)` act AND answer -- so the array grew, the file was written, and neither line was in `list`, in `save`, or in what `run` replayed. A session that typed `nodes = []` and three appends reported `saved 1 line`. The runtime raises the other half now (`gb_session_acted`), and asking the EVALUATION rather than a table of mutating builtins is the whole design: a table rots silently and its rot is indistinguishable from this bug, while following the run buys the two cases a syntactic rule gets wrong in OPPOSITE directions -- a function of yours that writes a file is KEPT though it answers, and one building its result in a local `out = []` (most of this stdlib) is NOT, though it called `append` to do it. That second one is decided by WHOSE FRAME THE NAME LIVES IN and not by call depth, because gBASIC has no closures and yet `append(g, x)` still reaches the global through the parent chain and mutates it in place -- measured, and the perturbation that counts every in-place mutation is caught by that control and by nothing else. TWO PLACES TAKE A COARSER ANSWER AND EACH DEFAULTS THE VISIBLE WAY: in the FILE family an unnamed verb ACTS (the nine that plainly ask are the exceptions), and a NATIVE MODULE call acts whether it read or wrote, since nothing at the call site tells `sqlite.exec` from `sqlite.query`. A question kept is a line with a number beside it; an act dropped is work that vanishes. `?` is the spelling for the other answer. Asserted with its CONTROL (`print sq(4)`, which acts, MUST be in the listing) or "questions are not recorded" is satisfied by recording nothing -- AND THAT CONTROL'S NEIGHBOUR HAD NEVER BEEN ABLE TO FAIL: the `lacks` looked for a bare "\nsq(3)" where `list` renders `  N  text`, so the needle could not match on any binary. Found by perturbing gb_session_acted to claim every line acted; the needles are pinned WITH THEIR NUMBERS now, which is stronger, since a question recorded renumbers the line after it. THREE DECISIONS THE IMPLEMENTATION TURNS ON. (1) WHEN IS A LINE FINISHED is asked of the PARSER, not a keyword table -- gBASIC reports exactly THREE shapes for running out of input ("unexpected end of file" for a block, PLAT-CONT's "unclosed '(' opened on line N" for an expression, and the LEXER's "unterminated string" for a literal) and a keyword table would have to know eleven block forms, their `end` spellings and their inline forms, and would go wrong the first time the grammar grew one. THE THIRD ARRIVED LATE BECAUSE IT COMES FROM A DIFFERENT STAGE (DOGFOOD 21, 2026-09-23): a multi-line string is legal in a FILE and the prompt would not wait for one, so `print("hello"` put up `...` while `m = "hello` answered an error and threw the line away -- line-oriented for one token and not the other, and the one construct a beginner is likeliest to try at the bench. Reading the lexer's message as "give me the rest" is sound because it is emitted at exactly one place, having run off the end of the buffer with a string still open. THE COST IS STATED: the blank-line escape is the one blocks and brackets already have, so a blank line INSIDE a multi-line string submits the chunk rather than becoming a paragraph break, and the CONTROL that keeps it from meaning "the lexer stopped refusing" is a dangling backslash, which is not running out of input and is still an error. CONTINUE_MESSAGES is the TRIPWIRE under all three, asserted against what the BINARY emits, because a rewording would cost the prompt its continuation SILENTLY. (2) WHEN IS A LINE A QUESTION: a chunk that PARSES as one expression statement is echoed by the evaluator (which is how a call returning `nothing` stays quiet, where a `print` rewrite would announce it); a chunk that does NOT parse is retried wrapped in `print (\n...\n)` -- on its own lines, so a trailing comment survives -- and if that also fails THE ORIGINAL diagnostic is reported. THE DISCRIMINATOR FOR THAT LAST RULE IS THE POSITION, NOT THE WORDS, and the first draft of the tier got it wrong: the wrapper and the original BOTH say "unexpected STRING", so only `<prompt>:1:7` against `<prompt>:2:7` separates them. (3) REDEFINING A FUNCTION IS AN EDIT, not the paste-gone-wrong run_scope.sh refuses -- relaxed for LOCAL definitions while a session is open, with the CONTROL that a FILE with two definitions is still refused, and the listing replaces IN PLACE so fixing a function does not move it to the bottom of a program you are reading. WHAT THIS SUITE CANNOT PROVE, AND SAYS SO: `run` starting a fresh session is the right semantics and is UNOBSERVABLE here -- measured, removing the close leaves every check green, because a line is recorded exactly when it ACTED, so every line that makes state is also in the program that remakes it. Asserted STRUCTURALLY against the source with the reason written beside it, rather than by a check that would pass either way. BUILDING IT FOUND A PRE-EXISTING PARSER LEAK: a failed parse dropped the statements it had already built (no `%destructor`), so `gbasic bad.bas` leaked its prefix -- a few hundred bytes per process and UNBOUNDED at a prompt, where a failed parse is an ordinary event since every question goes through one. Fixed with a destructor on the stmt_list nonterminals EXCEPT `program`, whose value is the one handed to the caller. PLUS CTRL-C, which is why the prompt is usable at all: a beginner's first `while true` must not take the session -- and the unsaved resident program -- with it. The handler sets ONE `volatile sig_atomic_t` and returns; the evaluator unwinds through the ORDINARY RAISE PATH, so locks are released and `with` blocks unwound exactly as a real failure unwinds them, and it is catchable for the same reason. Checked at the ENTRY of eval_stmt_list, which is once per loop ITERATION (every loop body is a statement list) and once per call, so a runaway loop, a runaway recursion AND a loop with an EMPTY BODY are all interruptible -- a per-statement check would never fire on the last of those. Armed ONLY by a session, with the CONTROL that a script's Ctrl-C still ends the process, or "Ctrl-C is handled" would be satisfied by making every gBASIC program ignore it. THE LOAD-BEARING HALF IS NOT SURVIVAL BUT MEMORY: a prompt that recovered by restarting itself would print SURVIVED and have forgotten everything, so the tier asserts the variable set before the loop is still there. DRIVEN DETERMINISTICALLY -- the loop PRINTS once it is running, so the signal is sent when the loop is provably executing rather than after a guessed sleep -- and the tier needs `set -m`, which is the subtle part: a NON-INTERACTIVE shell sets SIGINT to IGNORED for every background job it starts, so without job control the CONTROL would signal a process that cannot receive it while the prompt (which installs a handler, replacing the inherited ignore) passed anyway -- a tier measuring nothing beside one that works. Thirteen perturbations proven red. AND THIS SUITE'S OWN VALGRIND TIER PRODUCED THE REPORTS-THE-WRONG-CAUSE CLASS ONE MORE TIME: its fixture DELIBERATELY fails (an undefined name, a typo) so the session exits 1 by design, and a bare `if vg_run ...` read that as a valgrind objection -- printing FAIL valgrind over a report saying 0 errors, 0 leaks. Compared against `$VG_EXIT` now, which is what the shared policy exposes it for. PLUS THE EXPLORATION SURFACE, added the same day because a book's readers do things the tutorials do not: `? expr` (BASIC's shorthand, and a QUESTION rather than sugar for `print`, because it is how you read back a name a COMMAND has taken -- `? list`), `list [name]` NUMBERED so `delete n|name` has something to name without putting line numbers back in the language, and `cls`. A COMMAND NAME IS ONLY A COMMAND AS A WHOLE WORD and that rule needed TWO parts: a delimiter (`run()` is the author's function) and an ARGUMENT SHAPE (`list = 5` is an assignment to a variable somebody called `list`). The delimiter's own tier had to be FOUND -- `run()` does not test it, since the parenthesis already makes the argument non-empty, so only a command that TAKES an argument can be fooled and the discriminator is a variable named `listing`, read as `list ing` and answered with a puzzle about a program the author never mentioned. AND LINE EDITING AND HISTORY, WRITTEN NOT LINKED: GNU readline is GPL-3 against gBASIC's Apache-2.0, so linking it would relicense the binary a beginner downloads, and libedit is one more thing a distribution may not have -- "no arrow keys on your system" is exactly the note a first chapter cannot carry, the same argument the hand-written ZIP container and base32 made. src/lineedit.c: arrows/Home/End/Ctrl-A/E/K/U/W/L, history across sessions in a 0600 file (a session can hold a connection string), UTF-8 cursor movement so one backspace removes one CHARACTER, horizontal scrolling rather than wrapping because a wrapped redraw has no idea how many rows it used. THE CTRL-C SPLIT IS THE PART TO READ: raw mode holds ISIG OFF and only while a LINE IS BEING EDITED, so Ctrl-C there is a byte meaning "drop this line", while during a RUNNING chunk the terminal is back in cooked mode and the same key is a signal that interrupts the program -- one key, two correct meanings, decided by which of the two the prompt is doing, and BOTH are asserted. TESTED THROUGH A REAL PTY (tests/repl_pty.py), which is the only way: the editor runs only when stdin AND stdout are terminals, so a pipe cannot exercise one key of it. The driver types ONE BYTE AT A TIME and WAITS FOR THE PROMPT after each newline, because raw mode is held only while reading -- a byte arriving while the chunk runs is echoed by the line discipline and flushed when raw mode resumes, a window valgrind makes wide enough that a driver typing blind loses its escape sequences and reports a puzzle about undefined variables. HISTORY IS ASSERTED AS A DIFFERENCE (Up makes `1` appear TWICE, against a control running the same keys without it), and `quit` is deliberately NOT recorded, since finding it under your finger on the first Up is the one recall nobody wants. THE PTY VALGRIND TIER'S CONTROL EARNED ITS PLACE IMMEDIATELY: the first draft ran `/bin/sh -c "... exec vg_run ..."` and dash cannot exec a shell FUNCTION, so the prompt never started and the check reported `ok` over `exec: vg_run: not found` -- the silence of a clean run and the silence of no run at all. PLUS THE SESSION CACHE, which is the increment that DISSOLVED a refusal rather than working around it. What you type is written to a cache file as you go, so there is ALWAYS a file holding the program whether or not it was saved. TWO THINGS FOLLOW AND THE SECOND IS WHAT MOTIVATED IT: a killed session loses nothing, and `spawn` WORKS AT THE PROMPT -- a spawned actor is fork+exec and the child re-parses the SOURCE FILE, which a session did not have. docs/multiprocessing_design.md §3 predicted that needed "a clear error at spawn" and nothing could REACH the case until the prompt existed; what it actually produced was the CHILD's own complaint, a bare unlocated `<prompt>: No such file or directory` followed by "child actor failed to start", naming neither the cause nor anything to do. The refusal shipped, and the cache then made it the FALLBACK -- both halves asserted, so the error cannot become unreachable and rot. THE RE-EXEC PATH IS SEPARATE FROM THE SOURCE PATH (`gb_set_reexec_path`) because that one name has FOUR jobs and they diverged: it is the DIAGNOSTIC name (`<prompt>` reads better than a cache path in every message) and the base a relative `load` resolves against -- MEASURED, `load rel from "./rel.bas"` at the prompt resolves against the CWD, and repointing the one variable would have silently resolved it against the cache directory. THE CACHE HOLDS THE PROGRAM, NOT THE TRANSCRIPT, the same rule `list` and `save` follow; history holds the transcript, so between them nothing typed is lost, filed under what it was. RECOVERY NEVER RUNS ANYTHING -- executing yesterday's half-finished work on someone's behalf is the one behaviour here that could destroy something -- asserted as a DIFFERENCE with the control that `run` DOES produce the answer, or "does not run" is satisfied by recovering nothing. KEPT ON A DELIBERATE QUIT TOO, since leaving with unsaved work loses it as completely as a crash and is the commoner way; removed once saved, so the notice is never noise. THREE WEAK ASSERTIONS THIS INCREMENT EXPOSED, all the same shape and all found only by perturbation: `contains "spawn works"` passed on the REFUSAL, because the session survives an error and runs the next line either way (it asserts a real actor ROUND TRIP now, `A got: hello`, which only a live child can send); the not-a-question check used `1 + 1`, which does not PARSE and so takes the wrapper path that records nothing either way, making it vacuous (it uses a bare CALL now, the rule's real subject); and a `lacks` on raw text missed a `4` printed as the FIRST line, where a line-exact `grep -x` catches it. AND THE SPAWN TIER HAD TO BE BOUNDED: it WAITS FOR A REPLY, so a child that never starts makes the suite HANG rather than fail -- the third way this gate can go quiet, which run_string_nul.sh already had to defend against. PLUS TYPEAHEAD, the one tier whose subject is the window every OTHER pty tier exists to stay out of (DOGFOOD 25): between the Enter that ends one line and the prompt that begins the next the terminal is in COOKED mode and echoing, so a line typed while a command ran appeared on screen exactly as a line that had been taken -- and `raw_on`'s TCSAFLUSH destroyed it as the next prompt was drawn. It never ran and nothing was said. THE ECHO IS THE WHOLE PROBLEM; a prompt that ignored type-ahead in silence would be merely unhelpful. TCSADRAIN keeps it (raw_off's TCSAFLUSH stays, deliberately: bytes typed at the EDITOR reaching a program about to read in cooked mode is a different question and loses nothing the user watched being accepted). The driver needed `GBASIC_PTY_TYPEAHEAD=1`, which nothing else turns on, and the tier's non-vacuity check is a COUNT rather than a presence: on a working prompt the typed line appears TWICE (the line discipline while the command runs, then the editor's own redraw) and once on a broken one -- without which "42 appeared" is equally satisfied by a driver that quietly waited like all the others. AND THE SUITE NOW EXPORTS ITS OWN GBASIC_SESSION_DIR, because a session that quits with unsaved work KEEPS its cache file -- which is the feature these tiers assert -- and pointed at the developer's real state directory they accumulate there: measured 5,721, after which the product greeted a real person with "5721 previous sessions ended without saving". Thirty-one perturbations proven red. Headless bar the pty and interrupt tiers; skips the editing tier without python3
./tests/run_core.sh        # WP-CORE-1: the checks a golden CANNOT express. `sleep(0.30)` is asserted against the WALL CLOCK (nanosleep guarantees the full interval, so any shortfall is real) and `env` against a variable this script sets and unsets -- both are facts about the world, and a golden can only record what the binary said about it. The third tier is the read-then-shadow warning, and it is the interesting one: gBASIC has NO CLOSURES, so a function that READS an enclosing variable and then assigns the same name gets a silent function-local instead of writing back, which is almost never what was meant. The fixture's own stdout is the proof of that semantics (bump() runs twice, both calls see 6, the global stays 5), and the warning is required to (a) appear on stderr EXACTLY ONCE -- deduplicated across calls, not once per call, or a loop would bury the program in its own advice -- (b) carry file:line:col, and (c) change NOTHING: same stdout, same exit 0. Then the control that keeps it from being noise: a local that merely SHARES a global's name and is never read first must be SILENT, which is the case that separates "you meant to write that" from "you happened to pick the same letter". Headless, never skips
./tests/run_sqlite.sh       # skips cleanly if sqlite3 dev files absent. CARRIES THE STATEMENT-NOTE TIER since 2026-09-23 (DOGFOOD 41): a database error names the statement that failed. `SQLite prepare failed: no such column: player` said nothing about WHICH of the five statements going through a `one(db, sql)` helper was running, and the position it carried was the helper -- leaving the word `player` as the only handle and grep as the only tool, in the shape everybody writes after their third count. ONE FORMATTER FOR sqlite, pg AND odbc, because the three have the identical shape and three copies of a format are three things that drift; a TRIPWIRE asserts the suffix is built in exactly one place. COLLAPSED TO ONE LINE (SQL is written across several and a diagnostic is one line by construction, `--json-diagnostics` included) and TRUNCATED (the job is to IDENTIFY the statement, not reprint it). THE CONTROL is a failure that is not ABOUT a statement -- connecting, a catalog call -- which adds nothing, or the note would name SQL that was never the problem. TWO THINGS CAME OUT OF DOING `pg` THAT NOBODY HAD FILED: a Postgres error was FOUR LINES of stderr, since its text carries its own `LINE n:` and caret on separate lines and only the TRAILING newlines were trimmed (odbc already flattened its driver's text; pg was the module that had not); and Postgres OFTEN NAMES THE STATEMENT ITSELF, so the note is added only when libpq reports no statement position -- asked of libpq rather than guessed from the text. A BUG IN THE HELPER FOUND BY RUNNING IT: written as "emit the pending space only if it fits", the space was dropped at the truncation edge while the character after it still fitted, so `... from games where` came out as `from gamesw...`, two words glued into one that looks like a real identifier. AND THE CHECK FOR IT WAS VACUOUS ON THE FIRST ATTEMPT -- it looked for the two words joined (`masterwhere`) and the bug produces `masterw`, one character past the lost space, so it passed against the defect. It asserts a PROPERTY now: the excerpt, minus any `...`, is an exact PREFIX of the whitespace-collapsed statement, which no glued word, dropped character or reordering can satisfy.
./tests/run_insight.sh    # insight.explain_change + reasoning.bas -- the FIRST INCREMENT of Business Automation Reasoning (docs/automation_reasoning_design.md §13, validated per §14). ONE function, deliberately: Recipe 1 measured that a drill-down WITHOUT a null model produces the same confident three-level causal chain from a real planted collapse and from PURE NOISE, so a decision layer built on a Finding like that would be built on sand. SELF-CHECKING AND FORCED -- every defect here is a confident ORDINARY-LOOKING CAUSAL STORY, a chain of plausible percentages naming a place and a category, and a golden would record one as expected and defend it. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS THE PLANTED/NULL PAIR: the same decomposition over a population with a real 55% collapse in a known cell and over one with NOTHING planted must reach OPPOSITE verdicts; everything else checks a component. TWO CORRECTIONS CAME FROM BUILDING IT, both invisible to reading. (1) THE THRESHOLD WAS WRONG: Recipe 1 used sqrt(2 ln n), which is where the largest of n draws lands ON AVERAGE, so ~half of pure-noise populations clear it -- MEASURED at 6 of 13 seeds with nothing planted, a coin flip with a formula in front of it. It is now the two-sided BONFERRONI quantile for a declared alpha, recorded in the Finding; the same 13 seeds clear 0 of 13. The design doc's own §4.3 said sqrt(2 ln n) and is corrected. (2) THE SHARE REFUSAL BITES HARDER THAN EXPECTED AND CORRECTLY: R2 asks whether the NET change is distinguishable from zero before reporting any share of it, and in Recipe 1's own data it is NOT (t = -1.03 over 60 cells) -- so "82.6% of the decline is Northeast" was a share of a decline that had NEVER BEEN ESTABLISHED, in the run where a cell really had collapsed. That tier carries a CONTROL (a population where every cell really fell must report shares, summing to 1) or a library that never reported one would pass. reasoning.finding's refusals are where the design's corrections became structural rather than prose: no `materiality` (needs objectives, which belong to the decision layer), no `cause` (associations only, R3), no `assurance` (decision's), and `search.width` REQUIRED. Four perturbations proven red, each caught by the tier written for it. AND A REFUSAL R18 EXPOSED: a permuted threshold is an ESTIMATED QUANTILE at rank floor((1-alpha/repetitions)*draws), and once that rank reaches the top of the sample the answer is SIMPLY THE LARGEST DRAW -- a random variable with no stated coverage, returned silently and looking exactly like a threshold. Reachable before only through an unusual alpha; `repetitions: 12` reaches it at the DEFAULT 200 draws (rank 199 of 200). At least ten draws must lie beyond the quantile, and the refusal names the count it would need; the library's own default (alpha .05, 200 draws) sits EXACTLY on the rule, which is why it does not forbid its own default. COST: four fixtures used `draws: 8` deliberately ("this asks what the STATISTIC does") and the rule cannot make an exception for a caller who promises not to look at the answer -- they are 200 now and insight_test went 19s -> 62s. R18 IS ASSERTED HERE TOO and it is a DIFFERENT AXIS from everything else in this suite: every tier here measures ONE search, and a monitoring process asks the same question EVERY MONTH. The correction has always been family-wise over the CELLS OF ONE SEARCH and NOTHING ANYWHERE SAID SO -- twelve searches are twelve families, paid for once. MEASURED over a population with nothing wrong in it (run_insight_calibration, manual): 0.725 that some month raises a finding within the year, against 0.175 once `repetitions: 12` is declared. THIS IS NOT THE TAIL-WEIGHT PROBLEM the calibration suite already records: even a PERFECTLY CALIBRATED 0.05 per run is 1-0.95^12 = 0.46 over a year, so it is arithmetic about repetition and applies to a correct library too. THE DEFAULT IS 1 AND THAT IS A NO-OP, asserted, because 1 is what the library always silently assumed. AND IT IS PRICED, like max_causes: at 20 cells the bar goes 3.51 -> 4.63 monthly, 5.31 weekly, 6.25 daily, and by R15 THE BAR IS THE SMALLEST CHANGE THE SEARCH CAN FIND, so a year of monitoring costs about a third more change before anything can be reported -- the tier asserts the higher bar AND the larger detectable change, not just the formula. `search.correction` no longer says the bare word "bonferroni" leaving a reader to imagine the family; it NAMES it ("over the cells of this one search" / "times 12 runs"). Four perturbations proven red including the CAMPAIGN TIER ITSELF, proven red and then green on the same reduced fixture. HONEST GAP RECORDED: 0.175 is not 0.05, and whether siblings_permuted closes that at campaign level is NOT MEASURED (~1.4s a call, ~6 min for a usable estimate). RECIPE 3 IS ASSERTED HERE TOO and it is the depth recipe that attacks the cross-sectional null itself -- every null this library offers compares cells to each other AT ONE MOMENT and none looks at time, an assumption every Finding's provenance has RECORDED IN WORDS ("ordinary movement is stationary across cells") and nothing ever checked. THE RESULT IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT WAS EXPECTED: seasonality does not cause false alarms, it causes BLINDNESS. Compared December to January over 24 cells with ordinary seasonal profiles, the run with a real 45% collapse and the run with NOTHING wrong give the same leader, the same z to two decimals and nothing clearing either time -- and the collapsed cell went from z +0.52 to **-0.09**, the collapse making it look MORE ordinary. The mechanism is general and has nothing to do with seasons: a shift shared by every cell inflates the cross-sectional spread each cell is judged against IN PROPORTION TO ITSELF, so it raises the detection floor for everything; isolated in the simplest population (every cell down 40%) the same planted collapse that clears on its own no longer clears, asserted as a DIFFERENCE between the two runs since a number there passes on a library that stopped detecting anything. PRODUCED R13: a share is refused when the movement is COMMON TO THE POPULATION. Without it run A establishes a 50.1% decline at t = -4.49 and reports shares -- a confident attribution of an ordinary January to whichever cell sells the most toys, which is §11's headline claim produced from a population with nothing wrong in it. The signature costs nothing to look for (22 of 24 cells moved the same way, one-in-thirty-thousand under a null where a cell is as likely to rise as fall) and a SIGN TEST decides it, assuming LESS than the t test beside it already assumes -- direction only, never the shape of how far. IT IS NOT A SEASONALITY DETECTOR and the message says so: a real company-wide collapse and a broken feed both move 22 of 24 cells down, and what is established is R2's claim one level up -- the decomposition has not located this. AND IT FOUND A DECLARED CHOICE THAT WAS NOT A CHOICE: `versus_last_year` had been in design §7 for two months while `reasoning.comparisons()` returned ONE value and the error message beside it named all three in prose, so the remedy for a seasonal measure could not be spelled; with it, the SAME data, library and threshold recover the planted cell at z -4.91 ranked first. THE FIX IS THE COMPARISON, NOT THE NULL, which is why no third temporal null was added. R13 INVALIDATED THE CONTROL R2's TIER RESTED ON -- a population where EVERY cell really fell, which is now exactly the case a share may not be reported for -- so the control is a decline that is really SOMEWHERE (six of twenty cells, scattered across regions so no branch owns them, measured across five seeds at t -2.76 to -3.17 and sign p 0.12 to 0.50). Finding that band was informative: R2 and R13 together admit a NARROWER range than either suggests alone, since the concentration that makes a share meaningful is the same concentration that inflates the sd the aggregate test divides by. A third result arrived unasked -- run D plants nothing and a cell clears anyway at z 3.59, the t threshold's known miscalibration seen LIVE rather than in a Monte Carlo, and repeating under `siblings_permuted` removes the false one AND keeps the true one, which is the strongest evidence for that null so far because both halves are asserted. Two perturbations proven red (R13 never fires; R13 always fires), each caught by the tier written for it and by no other. RECIPE 2 IS ASSERTED HERE TOO and it is the SAME RESULT AS RECIPE 3 IN MINIATURE: the reference population is contaminated by exactly the thing being looked for. Leave-one-out (added by recipe 6) removes a cell from ITS OWN reference and NOTHING ELSE, so the experiment holds ONE cell literally constant -- the same collapse, the same change of -25,728 -- and varies only how many UNRELATED cells collapsed beside it, in other regions and other categories. Its z falls -5.70 -> -3.65 -> -2.83 -> -1.86 and its verdict goes from FOUND to nothing, because BOTH TERMS MOVE AT ONCE: the reference mean slides TOWARDS the anomaly and the reference spread inflates. THE TEST FINDS A PROBLEM ONLY WHILE IT IS NEARLY THE ONLY PROBLEM, and deepening the collapse does NOT help (at 90% the z still falls -6.99 -> -1.98) because the contaminating cells deepen with it. TWO REPAIRS WERE MEASURED AND BOTH FAILED, recorded because either would have passed a review: SEQUENTIAL PEELING does nothing, since the FIRST test is the most contaminated and it is the one that decides whether anything is reported (measured, the first two statistics are -3.10 and -3.05 against 3.49, so nothing is ever peeled); and a ROBUST MEDIAN/MAD SCALE fails because two extremes in twenty-four is not a small fraction of anything -- the MAD ITSELF rose 28% between one cause and two, and once its own null threshold is MEASURED rather than borrowed (4.18 against the t formula's 3.49) the robust statistic is FURTHER from clearing. PRODUCED R14 + `max_causes`: excluding the other candidates from the REFERENCE -- not blessing them as findings, merely declining to let them define ordinary -- restores the statistic to a property of the cell, trimmed z flat at -5.59/-5.46/-5.48 where the untrimmed fell to -1.86. AND IT IS NOT FREE: the bar rises 4.35 -> 4.84 -> 6.46, so by five causes in twenty-four cells it has OUTRUN THE EVIDENCE, and THAT FAILING ROW IS IN THE GOLDEN AND ASSERTED -- a recipe showing only successful recoveries would teach that max_causes REPAIRS the problem when what it does is MOVE it, from a silent failure to a priced choice. Default is 1 because that is what the library always silently assumed, and a tier asserts naming it changes NO existing answer. Above 1 REQUIRES `siblings_permuted`, because trimming changes the STATISTIC and the t quantile is a formula for the untrimmed one -- not approximately right but a threshold for a different quantity, erring towards reporting causes that are not there. Three perturbations proven red: max_causes ignored (caught by the restores-the-statistic tier and the recipe), an off-by-one trim (caught by EVERY existing answer moving, which is the proof the default is a genuine no-op), and the refusal removed. NOTE the permuted threshold is ESTIMATED and noisy -- at 100 draws the same population reported thresholds varying by half a unit, enough to flip a verdict; 400 draws is stable to ~0.08 and the recipe uses 200. RECIPE 4 IS ASSERTED HERE TOO and it was written to CONFIRM the depth plan's prediction -- "search width dominates; almost nothing should survive" -- and REFUTED it, which is the whole reason for running rather than reasoning. ONE BUSINESS OF FIXED TOTAL SIZE (same money, same 12,000 transactions per period) cut 24 / 240 / 1200 ways: the threshold moves only 3.49 -> 3.77 -> 4.11, because sqrt(2 ln n) grows about as slowly as anything in statistics, so A FIFTYFOLD WIDER SEARCH BUYS AN 18% HIGHER BAR and paying for multiplicity is nearly free. WHAT DOMINATES IS SUPPORT: the same planted 75% collapse falls from z -14.64 to -3.34 and stops being reported, and at the finest cut A DIFFERENT CELL (S838) CLEARS INSTEAD -- the real cause missed and a false one named. The smallest detectable change goes 18% -> 53% -> **147% of a typical cell**, so at 1200 cells NO DECLINE HOWEVER COMPLETE COULD BE REPORTED: a cell could go to zero and the answer would still be `within ordinary variation`, IN EXACTLY THE WORDS USED WHEN A BUSINESS IS HEALTHY. That indistinguishability is a REPORTING defect, not a statistical one -- every verdict was correct. PRODUCED R15 + `search.detectable` (change, typical_cell, share), reported WHETHER OR NOT anything cleared, since "nothing cleared" is precisely when a reader cannot tell an incapable search from a healthy business. ASSERTED AS FALSIFIABLE rather than as a formula echoing itself: at a cut whose stated bar is 94% of a cell, a 50% collapse must NOT clear, a total one MUST, and THE STATED BAR MUST LIE BETWEEN THEM -- without that last check the first two are facts about the verdicts with nothing tying them to the number. Proven red by dropping the threshold factor (bar reported ~4x too small), which fails both the difference tier and the bracket. THE THREE DEPTH RECIPES TOGETHER GIVE A PICTURE THE DESIGN DID NOT HAVE: three separate things degrade the cross-sectional test -- multiplicity (R1, priced into the threshold), contamination of the reference by the thing being sought (R13/R14, refused or declared), and lack of support (R15, only reportable) -- and ONLY MULTIPLICITY WAS IN THE ORIGINAL ARCHITECTURE, and it is the mild one. Fixture ~19s, three recipes ~70s, suite ~90s total. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_hypothesis.sh # insight.weigh + reasoning.hypothesis (recipe 8) -- §4's THIRD RUNG of the causal ladder, and built so it CANNOT reach the fifth: every hypothesis carries `explains: false` for its whole life, and only a recorded test could promote one (R3). It produces a QUESTION, not a cause -- what each candidate predicts, how well that matches what happened, and the observation that would separate the survivors. SELF-CHECKING AND FORCED: every defect produces a RANKED LIST OF PLAUSIBLE EXPLANATIONS (names, numbers, an order) and a golden would record whichever story came top as expected. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS R11 -- two hypotheses predicting THE SAME CELLS are not separated by that pattern, so they are reported TIED with both discriminators as the next test; its CONTROL is a set predicting differently that IS ranked, or "refuses to rank" would be satisfied by refusing to rank anything. NO PROBABILITY IS CLAIMED: the charter's §11 showed "confidence .91" for a cause and nothing in the data supports that, so what is computed is SET AGREEMENT (hits/union) reported WITH ITS DEFINITION ATTACHED, and a tier asserts the definition travels with it. PARSIMONY IS NOT IMPOSED, it falls out -- a hypothesis predicting 15 cells and explaining 1 has over-predicted 14 -- proven by the perturbation that scores on HITS ALONE, which makes "everything" the winning explanation. A `discriminator` is REQUIRED (a hypothesis nobody can imagine a test for is a story, and a story scores against a pattern just as well); `predicts` is DECLARATIVE rather than a function value, because a prediction that cannot be written into provenance cannot be audited. Weighing against a finding where NOTHING cleared is refused. Five perturbations proven red. NOTE a real gBASIC trap caught here: `find` returns `nothing` on a miss and `is_unknown(nothing)` is FALSE, so an is_unknown guard reads as "found at index nothing" -- use `contains`. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_decision.sh   # decision.evaluate -- the SECOND increment of Business Automation Reasoning, from Recipe 5 (docs/automation_recipe_05_what_to_do.md), the first recipe to cross this boundary at all: recipes 1-4 all stop at the finding, leaving two thirds of the architecture unexamined. SELF-CHECKING AND FORCED, more sharply than run_insight: every defect here produces a DEFENSIBLE-LOOKING RECOMMENDATION -- an option, an expected value, a rationale -- and a golden would record "restock and promote" as expected and defend it whether or not the number underneath had ever been established. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS R9, the refusal the recipe PRODUCED rather than one reasoned to: the same intervention over the same data gives EV +7,497 sized off the aggregate decline and -4,899 sized off the cell that actually cleared -- ACT against DO NOT ACT -- and the Finding had ALREADY said the aggregate was not established. Refused at the boundary, and again structurally in reasoning.decision so a hand-built Decision cannot smuggle one past; with a CONTROL (the SAME finding sized off the cell that DID clear is accepted, and a finding whose aggregate IS established may use it) or R9 would be indistinguishable from refusing everything. R6 IS THE OTHER: the recommendation is the best alternative, NOT the best AFFORDABLE one -- a layer returning the affordable option would hide the only choice a human needs to make and would pass every other check, hence a tier asserting a cheaper affordable option existed and was NOT chosen. Materiality is computed HERE from the Context (design §4.6) and answers `unknown`, never `false`, when no threshold is declared. A BUG THIS SUITE DID NOT CATCH, recorded because the lesson is the ASSERTION and not the bug: the first sensitivity sweep cancelled each alternative's own recovery, so a cheap intervention and an expensive one got identical benefit and the cheap one always won -- it reported assurance 0 for a recommendation it NEVER ONCE PICKED, and `assurance < 1` plus non-empty `sensitivities`, which is what the fixture asserted, are both satisfied by exactly that. Found by printing values, not by the suite. Fixed twice over: the library now RAISES if the sweep disagrees with the point estimate at the nominal assumption (scale 1 computes what the point estimate computed, so they must agree), and the fixture asserts assurance as a DIFFERENCE between a robust recommendation and a marginal one (0.95 against 0.43). Four perturbations proven red including that one. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind) RECIPE 9 IS ASSERTED HERE TOO and TURNS THE LOOP: controlled evidence becomes the assumption a later decision rests on. "How much evidence is enough" has NO answer in the abstract and a precise one relative to A DECISION -- sending a manager costs 2000 against a 16835 loss so it breaks even at a recovery of 0.119, and enough is when the interval STOPS STRADDLING that point. MEASURED: with the truth well clear of break-even, TWO observations settle it; with the truth a hair above it, THIRTY do not. Sufficiency is DISTANCE FROM THE DECISION BOUNDARY, not sample size, and any rule of thumb ("at least thirty") is wrong in both directions at once. Both halves asserted, since either verdict alone proves nothing. IT RETIRES SOMETHING INVENTED: recipe 5's `sensitivity_range: [0,2]` was a span I chose, and a calibration supplies the range the EVIDENCE supports. calibrate takes reasoning.as_evidence results and NOTHING ELSE, so R10 has already refused anything uncontrolled -- a calibration cannot be assembled from what merely happened next, which is exactly how the guessed 0.6 would have been produced. One observation is refused. A WEAK ASSERTION NEARLY LET A REAL DEFECT THROUGH: the fixture asserted the interval NARROWS with more evidence, and a standard error that forgot to divide by sqrt(n) still narrows a little because the t factor falls with df -- so the check passed on a broken implementation and only the golden caught it. It now asserts the width falls by MORE THAN THE t FACTOR ALONE accounts for (~5x from n=3 to n=20, vs ~2x without the sqrt). Same lesson as the sensitivity sweep: assert a DIFFERENCE BETWEEN TWO CASES, not a direction. THE CONTEXT FIELD CHECK IS ASSERTED HERE TOO (B5, the 0.1 reconciliation pass): design §5 claimed reasoning.bas owns the value shapes AND THEIR VALIDATION for all six, and the Context was the one it never checked. MEASURED: a context written with the SINGULAR `objective` and `threshold` -- the easiest mistake available -- produced a decision with `direction: "unstated"` and `materiality: unknown` and RAISED NOTHING, and `materiality: unknown` is the DESIGNED honest answer when no threshold was declared (§4.6), so a typo was INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A DELIBERATE OMISSION. `reasoning.check_context` now refuses an unrecognised field BY NAME -- the rule webserver.listen already follows for its options -- and BOTH layers that read a context call the SAME one, so they cannot disagree about what a field is called. TWO CONTROLS, not one: a well-formed context is accepted, AND so is one carrying `approval` which only the automation layer reads, because ONE context serves both and the decision layer must not refuse a field it does not itself use. Three perturbations proven red, and the pair is INDEPENDENT -- removing the automation half leaves decision_test at 0 mismatches and vice versa, so each layer's tier catches its own. NOTE the constructor was deliberately NOT built: a Context is ordinary business data, loaded and versioned like any other record, so what is shared is the FIELD LIST and the check, not construction -- and §5's invented `policies[]` and `null_policy` were struck rather than implemented, since nothing has needed a policy separate from the authority envelope and the null is declared per search where the width that sets its threshold lives. R17 IS ASSERTED HERE TOO and it came out of the 0.1 AUDIT rather than a lab recipe: `insight.weigh` reports agreement with `agreement_is` and `decision.quantity` reports amplification with `amplification_is`, and ASSURANCE -- the number most likely to be misread, since a bare scalar in [0,1] printed beside a recommendation reads as the probability that the recommendation is right -- carried NO definition at all. WRITING THE DEFINITION DOWN IS WHAT FOUND THE LIMITATION: assurance is the share of the swept range of the RECOVERY assumption over which the recommendation does not change, and EVERYTHING ELSE IS HELD AT A POINT ESTIMATE. Measured with the recovery calibrated to [0.13,0.17] from 40 controlled outcomes: at the cell's own change of -16835 the answer is ACT with assurance 1 and NO sensitivity reported, and at -12000 it is DO NOT ACT. R9 established that WHICH quantity you size off turns +7,497 into -4,899; this is the same knife one turn along and assurance 1 is silent about it BY CONSTRUCTION. `assurance_is` now carries the definition, what was swept, the range, WHERE THE RANGE CAME FROM (a calibration naming how much evidence it rests on, or a span the caller declared -- assurance 1 over those two is not the same claim and nothing distinguished them), the things held fixed WITH THE SIZED-OFF QUANTITY'S VALUE, and that it is NOT a probability. THE REMEDY IS DISCLOSURE NOT A WIDER SWEEP, deliberately: the cell's change is OBSERVED rather than estimated, and what is genuinely uncertain -- how much of an ongoing loss an action recovers -- is what the calibration already covers, so inventing an interval for the loss would be inventing a statistic. THE DEMONSTRATION IS A DIFFERENCE BETWEEN TWO RUNS with one thing varying, and it carries a CONTROL (widen the calibration on the SAME loss and the sweep DOES catch the flip it covers) without which "assurance is blind" is satisfied by an assurance blind to everything. Four perturbations proven red including the sizing pinned to a constant, which is what proves the tier tests the pathway rather than restating library output. NOTE `amplification_is` has the same gap one library along -- it names what it is but not what it holds fixed -- and that is RECORDED in the design as needing its own measurement rather than quietly fixed. RECIPE 11 IS ASSERTED HERE TOO and it is the recipe that CLOSED §9's PROVENANCE GAP, which had sat open since the charter while the document said "this is not a logging feature" and nothing said what it was for. Recipe 9 turned the loop over ONE intervention; a real organisation runs several at once and stores every measured action in one place. Two campaigns, both gated, both holdout-controlled, both read back through as_evidence -- so R10 is satisfied and every observation is real evidence -- with TRUE recoveries 0.35 and 0.05 against a break-even of 0.119. ASKED ONE AT A TIME the library gets both right (ACT, DO NOT ACT, assurance 1 each). POOLED it recommends BOTH, and the sharp half is that MORE EVIDENCE MAKES IT WORSE: the standard error falls with the size of the pool, so at 120 observations the estimate is 0.198 with a 95% interval of [0.161, 0.235] -- containing NEITHER truth -- and the decision sized off it reports `assurance 1`, DECISIVE, for an intervention that loses money every time it is taken. That is precisely the shape recipe 9 taught us to trust. NOTHING IN THE DATA OBJECTS: all 24 outcomes are revenue, all from gated actions, all measured the same way, and the pooled mean is a REAL QUANTITY that is simply not the answer to the question asked -- no property of the arithmetic can notice that. PRODUCED R16 (a calibration may not pool evidence about different questions; every item must agree on the MEASURE and the INTERVENTION, and the calibration carries both). CELLS ARE DELIBERATELY NOT PART OF THE RULE, because pooling across the PLACES a thing was tried is the entire purpose of a calibration -- so the CONTROL is asserted beside the two refusals in both the recipe and the unit fixture, or R16 would be indistinguishable from having no calibration at all. AND THE REFUSAL IS ONLY STATABLE BECAUSE THE CHAIN NOW CARRIES IT: `reasoning.decision` requires the `finding` it came from (a Finding satisfies it by being one; `decision.evaluate` used to record a `finding_subject` LABEL), `automation.execute` records the `context` -- §9's "policy permitting it", absent entirely until now, so an action could not say which regime allowed it -- and `as_evidence` therefore reports what it was ABOUT. The finding reaches the Action THROUGH the Decision rather than beside it, since a second copy is a second thing that can disagree; what `reasoning.action` checks is that the link is THERE, refusing a hand-built Decision that names no finding, the same structural treatment R9 gets. Three perturbations proven red. HONEST LIMIT stated in the write-up: agreement on measure and intervention is necessary and not obviously sufficient -- two runs a year apart under a different organisation still pool, and saying anything about that needs a population with a real regime change in it. RECIPE 10 IS ASSERTED HERE TOO and is THE FIRST RECIPE OF A DIFFERENT SHAPE -- every other executable recipe is "a measure moved, where, which of these actions", and that sameness was the largest untested assumption in the design. Here the answer is a CONTINUOUS QUANTITY, there is no decomposition, the cost of INACTION dominates, and a MODEL carries the parameter's uncertainty into the answer. p* = cost*b/(1+b) divides by (1+b), so an elasticity interval a shade over 1.5x wide becomes a price interval 13.5x WIDE (amplification 8.85), and an interval reaching b = -1 has NO ANSWER while a point estimate names 60.7 with a straight face. R12 refuses that; decision.quantity WALKS the interval rather than checking endpoints, since a break anywhere is a break, and reports point_estimate_would_say because that number is the most confident-looking wrong answer available. THE ARCHITECTURAL RESULT: the three-layer split SURVIVED (pricing is still `decide` and still refuses to execute), but the assumption that ONE ENTRY POINT covers the layer did not -- evaluate chooses among alternatives, quantity answers from a model. NOTE `map` cannot capture anything (no closures), so a caller needs one function per model INSTANCE. AND A STANDING LESSON ABOUT MY OWN TESTING, now three for three (recipe 5's sweep, recipe 9's interval, this amplification): each time I first wrote an assertion that PASSES ON THE BROKEN IMPLEMENTATION and only the golden caught it. The pattern is always asserting a value is LARGE or SMALLER THAN BEFORE, when what separates right from plausible-wrong is a SPECIFIC RELATIONSHIP -- here, that far from the edge the model DAMPENS (amplification below 1) where the raw spread is above it.
./tests/run_automation.sh # automation.execute/would/rehearsal/observe -- the THIRD increment of Business Automation Reasoning (Recipe 6), which CLOSES THE ARCHITECTURE: until something could act, R5 (simulation as a precondition) and the ENFORCEMENT half of R6 had never been tested at all. THIS IS THE LAYER WHERE A DEFECT IS NOT A WRONG NUMBER -- it is something HAPPENING that should not have -- so the refusal tiers assert an ABSENCE and prove it with a SIDE EFFECT ON DISK (the executor writes a marker file; "nothing happened" means the file is not there), since a return value proves nothing about whether the executor ran. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS THE SHARED GATE: `would` and `execute` both call one `_gate` and neither holds a copy of the rules, because R5's entire argument is that a rehearsal tells you what the LIVE run will do -- if they could disagree the rehearsal would describe a DIFFERENT PROGRAM. Asserted over a six-case matrix with a CONTROL that the matrix lands both ways, since unanimous agreement over all-refusals proves nothing. R5 IS CHECKED BEFORE AUTHORITY, deliberately, with its own tier: approval does NOT substitute for a rehearsal, because a human asked to approve an unrehearsed process has nothing to approve ON. `would` cannot act BY CONSTRUCTION (it takes no executor at all, rather than an executor plus a dry_run flag). THE RECIPE ITSELF IS ASSERTED because its demonstration is the argument for the layer: the same process at two granularities replayed over a year, where the COARSE one MISSES the collapse it exists to find and every alarm it raises is FALSE, while the fine one catches it and raises nothing else -- same code, same data, and nothing in the source says which you have. THE RECIPE ALSO FOUND A DEFECT IN `insight` THAT insight's OWN SUITE COULD NOT: a cell was standardised against a spread INCLUDING ITSELF, so max|z| = (n-1)/sqrt(n) -- at 8 cells that ceiling is 2.47, BELOW the threshold, meaning the test could never fire however completely a cell had collapsed and would report "within ordinary variation" for a cell that had gone to zero. Fixed with leave-one-out standardisation and a t threshold (n-2 df, since a LOO residual is not normal) plus a refusal below 4 cells. Five perturbations proven red. ~10s. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind) RECIPE 12 IS ASSERTED HERE TOO and it is THE RECIPE THAT BUILDS NOTHING: fourteen months through the real libraries IN ONE PROGRAM, because the claim that the architecture is closed end to end had been made since recipe 6 and NEVER RUN. IT FOUND THAT NO ACTION IN THIS TREE HAD EVER BEEN BUILT FROM A DECISION `decision.evaluate` PRODUCED -- recipes 9 and 11 print evaluate's answer and then execute a HAND-WRITTEN one -- so R9's sizing check and §9's provenance chain had only ever been enforced against records written by hand. `assurance_is` is the proof the Decision came from evaluate, since nothing else puts one there, and that is what the suite greps. R5 refuses month 2 with a real finding (z -5.01) and a sound decision (EV 9308) behind it; R18's declared `repetitions: 12` raises the bar 3.51 -> 4.63 so several REAL collapses correctly go unacted; automation.assign holds 2 of 5 back so a counterfactual exists at all; and R9 still refuses month 13 where nothing cleared. THE LOAD-BEARING ASSERTION IS NOT THE GOLDEN: the loop must RECOVER the true incremental effect of 0.38 (treated cells close 0.62 of the gap, untreated 0.24) and it reports 0.374 -- the only check that fails if the chain is wired correctly and measuring the WRONG THING, proven red at 0.623 (the treated recovery, a perfectly reasonable number) by taking `effect` as the observed value instead of the difference from the holdout. TWO DEFECTS FOUND, BOTH IN THE RECIPE: a business whose collapse schedule was keyed off the TREATMENT record, so nothing ever went wrong and the run found nothing because there was nothing; and `assign(k,r) = "holdout"` comparing a RECORD to a STRING -- silently false by PLAT-EQ's design -- so nothing was held back, so R10 refused every outcome, so no calibration was produced, every step behaving correctly. Symptom read as a broken hash (0 of 20 at a declared 0.3); it was a missing `.arm`. See DOGFOOD. RECIPE 7 IS ASSERTED HERE TOO and is the sharpest measurement in the lab: an intervention whose true effect is EXACTLY ZERO (nothing in the fixture alters a number after the decision) shows a ~49% apparent "recovery" -- the number a learning loop would have stored, and the number recipe 5 had GUESSED at 60% and flagged as what its recommendation was most sensitive to. It is entirely REGRESSION TO THE MEAN: an action is taken precisely BECAUSE a measure is extreme, and extremes contain noise that does not repeat. The cells nobody touched recovered just as well (85.5%), and BOTH halves are asserted since the apparent recovery alone is only half the argument. That produced R10 (an uncontrolled outcome is not evidence of an EFFECT) and `automation.assign` -- the concept of deliberately NOT acting, which the architecture had no way to express, DETERMINISTIC in the key because a replay must make the same assignments as the live run (R5). A HASH BUG HID BEHIND COMFORTABLE TEST DATA: the first assignment hash left consecutive short keys consecutive, so 0 of 400 keys `c1`..`c400` were held out while the first distribution check, using longer `cell-N/x` keys, reported a clean 0.200 -- the tier now uses SHORT SEQUENTIAL keys on purpose.
./tests/run_automation_lab.sh # the Business Automation Reasoning DESIGN LABORATORY (docs/automation_reasoning_design.md §15: the cookbook is initially a design laboratory). NOT user documentation and the libraries it argues for do NOT exist -- these are EXPERIMENTS in today's gBASIC whose job is to make a design claim MEASURABLE instead of plausible. Recipe 1 answers one question about §11's automatic decomposition, the document's most attractive proposal: does "67% of the decline is attributable to Northeast -> Syracuse -> Outdoor" mean anything? IT RUNS THE SAME DECOMPOSITION TWICE -- run A over a real 45% collapse planted in one known cell, run B over a population with NOTHING planted, only lognormal noise. BOTH DECLINE 1.8%, both produce a confident three-level chain, and the output cannot tell them apart (top-region share 82.6% vs 80.3%, top-store 57.3% vs 51.2%). That is the finding: a drill-down is a SEARCH and a search always returns a winner. Run B is the entire experiment -- a decomposition only ever pointed at data with a known answer always looks like it works. THE FIX NEEDS NO NEW CAPABILITY: the other 59 leaf cells ARE the null, and against it the runs separate (z -3.45 vs -2.38); but the CUT IS NOT A CONSTANT, it is sqrt(2 ln n) -- 2.33 at 15 cells, 2.86 at 60, 3.26 at 200 -- so a decomposition that does not know its own SEARCH WIDTH cannot set it, which makes width part of the RESULT rather than an implementation detail. The golden pins the transcript; the five assertions beside it pin what makes the experiment MEAN what the write-up says, each of which could stop being true while leaving a passing golden (a golden records whatever came out): run A recovers the PLANTED cell, run B reaches a DIFFERENT confident conclusion, both headline declines are the SAME percentage (without which a reader dismisses run B as "that one barely moved"), the null clears A and rejects B, and the threshold is DERIVED from the search width. Headless, GI-independent, never skips
./tests/run_accounting_cookbook.sh # docs/accounting_cookbook.md on the shared cannot-lie harness (tools/sync_accounting_cookbook.sh wraps the parameterised sync). RUN/CODE/OUTPUT/COVER over examples/accounting_cookbook/*.bas; never skips. 4 recipes: chart and first entries, a consultancy's month end to end (the SAME worked business the suite checks, so page and validation share their figures), closing a period and why a second close is REFUSED rather than idempotent, and the refusals with the reason each matters. RECIPE 4 CARRIES A gBASIC LESSON LEARNED WRITING IT: `on error goto next` is frame-scoped and so is ACKNOWLEDGING one -- `if error then` inside a helper does NOT claim the error pending in its caller, so the first draft's `show()` helper left every error unacknowledged and the second raise escaped the frame, which is the anti-silence rule working as designed. CODE and OUTPUT proven red in isolation (comment-only .bas edit with byte-identical stdout, confirmed identical by diff before running the suite; perturbed page block with files untouched)
./tests/run_fake.sh        # stdlib/fake.bas -- fabricated but realistic data (docs/fake_data_design.md). THE API SHAPE WAS DECIDED BY THE LANGUAGE, not taste: a gBASIC record is a VALUE, so `s.n = s.n + 1` inside a function mutates a local copy and a stream object CANNOT ADVANCE ITSELF (measured -- every draw returned the same number). So every generator is a PURE FUNCTION OF (seed, index), which also buys ORDER INDEPENDENCE: row 47 is the same row asked for directly or reached by walking, so adding a field to one generator cannot shift everything produced after it and committed fixtures cannot silently rot. The RNG is gBASIC's own (SplitMix64 into xoshiro256, NOT libc), so output is identical across machines -- asserted by running the same program twice in separate processes and requiring byte-identical output, since in-process determinism is not the claim. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS THE LAST: design §7 says the four consistencies (intra-record, referential, temporal, arithmetic) are proved TOGETHER by driving `accounting` with the output, because an unbalanced entry, a phantom account or a cross-currency line are each refused where posted -- so 500 generated invoices posting cleanly with the accounting equation holding DEMONSTRATES consistency instead of claiming it. THE DISTRIBUTION TIER is what separates this from a name generator, and the comparison is measured not asserted: Faker 39.0.0's `pyint` puts 11.0% of values in each leading digit where Benford expects 30.1% for 1, so Faker data cannot exercise a Benford detector at all; fake.lognormal lands at ~30.8%, asserted as a BAND because it is a distribution and not a value. Names/emails/phones are STRUCTURALLY unable to name a real person (RFC 2606 domains, 555-01xx), which matters because fake data reaches bug reports and shared databases. Headless, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_fake_plant.sh # `fake.plant` -- a clean population with a KNOWN defect in a KNOWN place (docs/fake_data_design.md §6). SEPARATE FROM run_fake BECAUSE THE CLAIM IS A DIFFERENT KIND: the generators are judged on whether their output is realistic, planting on whether it is exactly and only what was asked for. Five kinds -- round_dollar, just_under, weekend, duplicate, sequence_gap -- returning {rows, planted} where planted names anomaly/id/index/was/now/source. THE LOAD-BEARING PROPERTY IS "NO MARKER": the rows come back carrying nothing their neighbours lack and the report is a SEPARATE value, because a marker field is a back door a detector could read and a detector tested against data that LABELS ITS OWN ANOMALIES has not been tested at all. That rule has one visible consequence, and it is a REFUSAL rather than a convenience: a planted duplicate needs an id CONTINUING the population's own sequence, so a population whose ids do not end in digits is refused rather than handed an id that stands out -- inventing a distinguishable id would quietly make the fixture worthless. Self-checking not golden, forced: every planting defect leaves a population that STILL LOOKS LIKE A POPULATION (an anomaly on the wrong row, one too many, a clean row altered in passing), so a golden would record the damaged data as expected. The three claims §6 sets are asserted separately -- exactly n rows changed, they are the rows the report NAMES, and every other row is unchanged by deep comparison -- plus that only the intended FIELD moved. Each tier proven red against its own deliberately broken copy: a report naming the wrong row, a clean row altered in passing, a marker field, a duplicate id that stands out, an unsorted sample, a weekend anomaly that misses the weekend; each caught by the tier written for it and by no other. WHAT PLANTING IS FOR IS STILL AHEAD OF IT and the design says so: there is NO transaction-level detector in the tree (`forensics` is FINANCIAL-STATEMENT forensics -- accruals, M-score, Altman -- and has no Benford test, which is a correction to this design doc's own earlier claim), so the nearer consumer is Phase 3 credit analytics. The scale tier's load-bearing figure is the THIRD one: 200 anomalies on 200 DISTINCT rows of 20 000, since a sampler that collided would report 200 anomalies on fewer rows and look perfectly fine. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_valgrind_policy.sh # the valgrind POLICY itself (tests/valgrind_tier.sh), which 35 suites now share. BEFORE 2026-09-01 those 35 ran through 12 DISTINCT INVOCATIONS -- two different --error-exitcode values, --track-fds on seven and not the other twenty-eight, -q on four, and the one that actually changes what counts as a failure: --errors-for-leak-kinds=definite on most but NOT all. Without that flag valgrind's default is `definite,possible`, so a suite that omitted it was running STRICTER than its neighbours and which strictness a suite got was historical accident. The shared policy is the one every suite already CLAIMED in its own words ("no definite leak or invalid access"); `possible` is deliberately not asserted, since an interior pointer into a live allocation is ordinary in a tree-walking interpreter. THIS SUITE EXISTS BECAUSE SHARING INTRODUCED A NEW FAILURE MODE THAT DID NOT EXIST BEFORE: if `vg_run` ever stops actually running valgrind -- a bad edit, a renamed variable, a source line that silently does nothing -- then 35 tiers go on printing "no definite leak or invalid access" while asserting NOTHING. It cannot be proved with gBASIC, which is clean as far as every other suite can tell, so the control is two three-line C programs with a KNOWN definite leak and a KNOWN invalid read: if valgrind is really running they fail, and if it is not they pass and this suite goes red. It also pins THE ONE AXIS on which the two policies differ, which is the whole reason there are two -- vg_run fails on a definite leak and vg_run_access_only does not, while BOTH fail on an invalid read. Without that pair, access-only would be indistinguishable from "valgrind is off", which is exactly what it must not become since run_odbc (driver-internal defects, suppressed BY NAME via VG_EXTRA), run_continuation (does going past the fixed 64-entry opener stack write out of bounds) and run_smtp (libcurl) all assert through it. Plus a TRIPWIRE that keeps the consolidation from unravelling: no suite may invoke valgrind directly, and at least 35 must go through the policy -- a new suite typing its own flags is the state this replaced. Both halves proven red (vg_run stubbed to exec its argument; one suite reverted to a raw call). Skips without valgrind or a C compiler
