./tests/run_web_configure.sh # `web.configure(sv, settings)` -- deployment settings for a declared server. A `server` block's head options are LITERALS, so a worker count could not come from configuration; reported from a real application. THE HEAD STAYS LITERAL AND THE STATED REASON WAS WRONG: the refusal said "so the block stays statically checkable", and MEASURED over src/frontend.c NONE of the seven head options has its value read by a parse-time check -- only the SITE option `host` does, to refuse two sites claiming one name. The binding reason is WHEN: `server_register` is in the pre-registration set, so a block is materialised before anything runs and `workers: n` would read an unassigned `n`. Hence a second door, not a looser head. `configure` returns ANOTHER DECLARATION, which is why it is not an argument to `serve` -- web.routes, web.dispatch and the outline keep working on it and configuring twice composes. WHICH OPTIONS ARE ADMITTED IS DERIVED, not chosen: an option may be overridden exactly when no parse-time check depends on its value, which is all seven and NOT `host`. TIERS: SEMANTICS (27 self-checking, since every defect is a plausible RECORD -- a setting dropped, an original mutated, a field lost in the copy); TABLES, THE TRIPWIRE, because the admitted options now live in TWO places (head_options[] in src/frontend.c and web._head_options() in stdlib/web.bas) and two representations of one declaration drift, which is the defect `tools` exists to prevent one library over -- it reads BOTH and requires them identical; LIVE, which is the only tier that says the configured value is ACTED ON rather than merged and ignored -- the SAME SOURCE served twice under different configuration must take DIFFERENT PATHS (workers=1 single-process, workers=2 supervising, observable because only _serve_pool announces the bound port) and both must answer on a port the source never names; and MESSAGE, because the refusal NAMED A REMEDY THAT DID NOT EXIST -- "computed configuration belongs to webserver.listen" is true for port/address/timeout/cert/key and FALSE for `workers`, since webserver.listen binds one socket and has no worker count, and one message served both option tables which is how it came to be wrong for exactly one. A CORRECTION RECORDED IN THE FIXTURE: the record could ALREADY be written directly (`app.options.port = ...`, documented in the reference), so the gap was never "there is no way" -- but that path validates NOTHING, and `app.options.workesr = 4` is accepted in silence, which is asserted as a DIFFERENCE beside configure refusing the same typo by name. Also: a worker count is checked where it is USED, so a literal `workers: 0` meets the same rule as a configured one (it previously served single-process without a word). Three perturbations proven red; the message one caught by no other tier
./tests/run_tools.sh       # `tools` -- one declaration of what a model may call, and the safe way to call it (stdlib/tools.bas, docs/tools_design.md; AI proposal step 3). THE EXTRACTION of what `llm.bas` already does, not a rival: `tools.as_llm_tools` feeds the same declaration to the existing chat loop, which is the evidence. THREE DEFECTS IN THE OLD SHAPE MOTIVATE IT AND ALL THREE ARE TESTED. (1) THE SCHEMA AND THE VALIDATOR WERE TWO HAND-WRITTEN THINGS -- `llm.tool` takes a JSON-Schema record AND a function with nothing checking they agree, so the schema can tell the model `id` is required while the function reads `customer_id`. THE ORACLE TIER IS THE LOAD-BEARING ONE: the fixture reads `tools.schema` back and drives dispatch FROM it, so every parameter the schema calls required must be one dispatch refuses without and every type it names must be one dispatch rejects a wrong value for -- a `params` declaration generating a schema nobody enforced passes every other check in the file. Proven red by a schema that marks everything required and by a dispatch that skips the type check. (2) A RAISING TOOL KILLED THE RUN: `llm.bas` documents that a tool "must not raise", which was TRUE WHEN WRITTEN because a raise could not be caught at all, and PLAT-ERR made the rule unnecessary while the rule outlived it -- MEASURED, a raising tool still ends the whole program on that path. dispatch runs the body under `on error`, and the SECOND assertion is the one that matters: THE NEXT CALL STILL WORKS. (3) A TOOL BODY ON THE EVENT LOOP FREEZES EVERY CLIENT, so bodies run in pre-spawned workers. THE POOL TIER asserts PARALLELISM as a difference -- the call with NO sleep is sent LAST and must come back FIRST, since a test that only checks all three replies arrive passes on a pool of one serving them in turn -- plus the PRINCIPAL HANDOFF, which can only reach a worker by being written into the message (a principal does not cross `spawn`), proven red by a worker that ignores it. THE HANDLER TIER IS THE WHOLE CHAIN and asserts an ORDERING, not a clock: both handlers must RETURN before either answer appears. 18 define-time refusals each beside their nearest legal neighbour, including unknown fields refused BY NAME on an entry AND on a parameter. THE CALLER SPAWNS, NOT THE LIBRARY, and that is the language: `spawn` resolves a bare function NAME, so it takes neither a library function nor a function value (DOGFOOD 2026-09-06, now an executable bullet in run_limitations). Four perturbations proven red, each caught by the tier written for it
./tests/run_inbox.sh       # `watch(inbox.messages)` -- an actor reply delivered by the event loop; the PLATFORM half of the tool pool. `receive()` BLOCKS, which is right for a sequential program and fatal in a handler, because the watcher IS the event loop: a pool whose replies are collected with `receive` has moved the tool body off the loop and then made the loop wait for it, which is no improvement at all. SMALLER THAN THE HTTP CASE because an interpreter has exactly ONE inbox -- one descriptor, and one readable event on a SOCK_SEQPACKET socket is exactly one whole frame, which is not an assumption but what the GI bridge's mailbox source has relied on since it was written. The global is bound when the WATCHER IS REGISTERED rather than at load, because send/receive/spawn are builtins with no `load` to hang it on. A MAILBOX HAS NO COMPLETION, unlike a transfer, so the program says when it is done: `unwatch` ends delivery and the loop exits -- without it the first version ran forever, and A HANG IS NOT A FAILURE, which is why that tier is bounded. TWO CONTROLS: actors with a reply in flight and NO watcher must exit at once (or "the loop runs after main" would be true of every program), and `receive()` itself must be UNCHANGED, since adding a delivery route must not disturb the blocking one. `receive()` inside a watcher WARNS in the same words as `http.wait`, with the control that an ordinary receive is silent. ONE PERTURBATION proven red -- the inbox polled but never serviced -- and it reports THE PROGRAM HUNG, which is the diagnosis. THIS SUITE IS THE FIRST TO RUN VALGRIND OVER AN ACTOR PROGRAM, and that immediately found a PRE-EXISTING fd leak: `ensure_root_mailbox` opens a socketpair the root actor owns for life and nothing ever closed it, so every program that called `self()` or `spawn` ended with two descriptors open -- an untouched examples/spawn_handle_passing_test.bas reports the same two. Fixed at teardown. The lesson is about COVERAGE not the leak: a tier present in 36 suites measures nothing about a shape none of them exercises
./tests/run_timer.sh       # `timer` -- PERIODIC WORK ON THE EVENT LOOP, and until this there was NO WAY TO DO IT AT ALL. Every source the loop polls is request-, reply- or transfer-driven (`server.requests`, `inbox.messages`, `http.events`); nothing fired because TIME PASSED, so periodic work had one spelling -- `sleep` in a loop -- which on the event loop means the handler never returns and its worker never comes back. THAT IS WHAT gdash MEASURED AND MISATTRIBUTED TO STREAMS (GDASH-11): they reported workers=N streams=N leaving the next request unanswered and asked for a stream handler that can wait, when parking already worked and what was missing was anything to do the POKING. Needs no descriptor (the loop already wakes every 50ms, so a timer is a comparison per iteration plus a shortened poll timeout) and no `load` (no optional dependency, only clock arithmetic -- the `process` precedent). SELF-CHECKING NOT GOLDEN AND FORCED: every defect is a PLAUSIBLE NUMBER OF TICKS -- twice as often, half as often, or a silent catch-up after a stall all read exactly like a working timer. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS COALESCE, and it is a DIFFERENCE: at most one tick per timer per iteration with the next due time computed from the DELIVERY, so a handler slower than the interval loses ticks instead of bursting -- a burst on the event loop is an unbounded queue, which PLAT-HTTP shipped once and whose symptom was A HANG, NOT A FAILURE. Every other tier in the file passes on a catch-up schedule, so the assertion is that 4 ticks arrive where ~18 intervals went by, NOT that ticks arrived. AND THE SHORTFALL IS REPORTED or coalescing is silent data loss: `skipped` plus `delivered` must account for the fixture's KNOWN 0.80s schedule and may not claim more time than the wall clock saw. TIME IS MEASURED FROM OUTSIDE and that is forced -- gBASIC's clock is SECOND-RESOLUTION, so `epoch(now())` cannot see a 0.25s sleep at all (DOGFOOD 2026-09-12) -- which is also the stronger oracle, the standard run_core.sh holds `sleep` to. THE MONOTONIC TIER READS THE SOURCE, because a test cannot move the system clock: a wall-clock timer stalls on an NTP step back and fires every interval it crossed on a step forward, both silently. ONE-SHOT asserts the program EXITS BY ITSELF (a one-shot removes itself, so the loop runs out of work) with the CONTROL that a repeating timer does NOT, or "it exited" would be true of a build where timers never worked. UNWATCHED pins warning 2108 AT THE TIMER'S OWN CREATION SITE -- it fires after `main` returns where there is no current line, and a warning at 0:0 names nothing, the same correction the library-shadow note needed -- with the control that a watched timer is silent. STREAM is the shape the ask asked for end to end: a parked SSE stream receiving 15 timer-driven events it never requested WHILE an ordinary request is still answered. TWO DEFECTS IN THE RUNNER ITSELF, both the "a tier that reports nothing" class: the forever CONTROL read 0 ticks because a KILLED process's block-buffered stdout never reaches the file (fixed with --line-buffered), and the monotonic tripwire's `time\(` matched inside `clock_gettime(` so it failed on the correct source. VALGRIND
./tests/run_principal.sh   # `with principal(p)` / `principal()` -- the identity on whose behalf a body acts (AI reference proposal step 2, and general beyond it). NO WORD IS RESERVED AND THE GRAMMAR DID NOT CHANGE: it rides the `with lock(...)` production, whose opener is recognised by POSITION, so a variable, a field and a parameter may all be called `principal` -- and the GRAMMAR tier asserts bison still reports ZERO conflicts, which is not decoration in a project that rejected `IDENT expression` as a statement form over 4 MEASURED conflicts. `principal()` answers NOTHING, never an empty record, when no block is open, because "nobody said" and "acting for a principal that carries no fields" are different claims and an enforcement point must be able to refuse the first -- the same rule `materiality` follows in answering `unknown` rather than `false`. Dynamically scoped like `on warning`'s mode and unlike `on error`'s frame, since "who is this being done for" is the CALLER's answer to give; nesting shadows, leaving restores, and the pop happens BEFORE the result propagates so a return, a goto and a raise all leave it. THE TWO LOAD-BEARING TIERS ARE THE PLACES IT MUST NOT REACH, both asserted as DIFFERENCES: an actor does not inherit it (spawn is fork+exec, so the isolation falls out of the process model rather than being enforced) WITH the control that a worker handed the record explicitly DOES act under it -- "the child sees nothing" alone is satisfied by a feature that never works anywhere, and "the child acts for gwen" alone by one that leaks; and a request handler fired by the EVENT LOOP inherits nothing even though the listener was created inside a block, because the loop runs after `main` outside every block the program opened, WITH the control that the same handler establishing a principal from the request header acts under it. That second one is the architecture's rule that a request is acted on for whoever SENT it. 5 refusals each beside their nearest legal neighbour, including `with lock` which shares the production and must be untouched. Three perturbations proven red; the first two (the scope never left, and `principal()` answering an empty record) are caught by THREE tiers each, which is honest overlap rather than three independent checks. Headless bar the handler tier, which needs python3 and curl. A LESSON FROM SHIPPING IT: the implementation was first written next to `http_multi`, which is INSIDE `#if HAVE_LIBCURL`, so the entire feature vanished from a lean build -- green on this machine, and caught only by run_packaging.sh, which is the one suite that builds without the optional libraries
./tests/run_http.sh        # PLAT-HTTP `http` -- requests that do NOT block the program, plus handle readiness DELIVERED by the event loop. `webclient` performs one request and returns when it is done, which is right for a script and wrong for anything that also has to answer a socket; `http` is the same request over libcurl's MULTI interface, so a request is a HANDLE -- the six verbs `process` gives a child, meaning the same things. SELF-CHECKING NOT GOLDEN, and forced: every defect here is an ORDINARY-LOOKING RESULT (a 500 reported as a failure, a `start` that secretly blocked, a body that arrived in one piece), so a golden would record it and defend it. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS TRANSPORT-VS-HTTP: `transport_ok` says the bytes arrived and `status` says what the server thought, so a 500 must be a SUCCESSFUL transfer carrying 500 while a refused connection is a failed one carrying NO status -- asserted as a DIFFERENCE, since either half alone passes on a module that conflates them (proven: the perturbation making transport_ok mean `status < 400` is caught by exactly ONE check, the one asserting a 500 completed). CONCURRENCY is the tier without which every other one passes on a `start` that performs the request: four 400ms requests started together against the same four one at a time, asserted as a RATIO (gate 2x, measured 33/10x -- 515ms against 1717ms), never an absolute time. PARITY WITH webclient IS THE ORACLE -- the same URL through the blocking EASY interface must give the same status, headers and body, and the header parser is deliberately SHARED so a divergence means a multi-interface option changed the semantics. DELIVERY asserts the waited-or-watched rule as a difference INSIDE ONE PROGRAM (a handle the program blocked on must NOT also be reported; one it merely started MUST), in a program with NO SERVER BOUND -- which is the other half, since the loop now runs after `main` while there is anything to deliver and a program holding only handles used to exit. Its CONTROL is NO_WATCH: the same shape with the watcher removed must return at once and abandon a 3s request, or "the loop runs after main" would be true of every program. IGNORED is the HANG tier and it came from a real defect: the first delivery loop re-reported undrained bytes EVERY iteration, so a watcher that never read the body gave an unbounded event queue and a loop that never went idle -- it did not fail, it HUNG, which is why that tier is bounded by `timeout`. RAISE asserts something PRE-EXISTING that this module's second caller exposed: a raise inside a watcher exited 1 with NOTHING on stderr, because the fatal report runs when `main` returns and a watcher fires after that -- asserted for `http.events` AND `server.requests`, since a fix proven only on the new caller says nothing about the old one. DEFERRED is the tier the AI architecture actually rests on and it FOUND A DEFECT IN THIS MODULE: a handler runs on the event loop's thread, so a request needing a model call must START it and RETURN, with the answer appended later from a DIFFERENT watcher -- which nothing in the tree had ever done. Written the natural way (`h = http.start(...)` rebinding one global per request, remembering `pending[id] = req.id`) the SECOND request CANCELLED THE FIRST, because dropping the last reference to a handle cancels its transfer: two handlers ran, ONE answer arrived, nothing was reported. Right in WAITED mode (nobody could read the answer again), wrong in WATCHED mode (the loop will deliver it and the event carries the handle back), so the loop now holds a reference of its own from start until `done` is delivered. THE ASSERTION IS AN ORDERING, NOT A CLOCK: two requests at once, each upstream call 800ms, and BOTH handlers must have STARTED before EITHER answer appears -- a tier that only checked both clients got their bodies passes on a handler that blocked and served them one at a time. Six perturbations proven red, each caught by the tier written for it and (for the claim tier, the raise tier and DEFERRED) by no other. TWO LESSONS FROM THE TIER ITSELF, both the "a red tier that reports nothing" failure class: under `pipefail` an `order=$(grep ... | awk ...)` whose grep matches nothing FAILS THE ASSIGNMENT and `set -e` ends the suite SILENTLY -- the perturbation was red and printed no FAIL line for two runs; and the fixture's stdout must be `--line-buffered`, because the tier reads it after killing the process and block-buffered lines that prove the ordering may never reach the file. PLUS THE REQUEST-BODY-IS-BYTES CHECK (2026-09-25): the body was copied with `copy_string` and measured with `strlen`, so `"a" + chr(0) + "b"` went on the wire as ONE byte with POSTFIELDSIZE saying 1, and nothing raised. Asserted against what THE SERVER reports (`/length`) rather than by echoing the body back, because THIS MODULE'S READER WAS ALREADY CORRECT (`value_string_n`) -- so an echo comparison would have passed while the write side was broken, and is exactly the shape that let `webclient` and `webserver` hide the same defect. Its CONTROL is an ordinary two-byte body, so the fix cannot be something appended to every request. Client is a python3 loopback fixture on an OS-ASSIGNED port so no golden carries a number this machine could bind; skips without python3 or without libcurl in the build, the latter detected by the module's own refusal text. VALGRIND, since this is a new refcounted value kind sharing one multi handle torn down before curl_global_cleanup -- the handle STRUCTS outlive that point, being owned by values in the global env, so their easy handles must be freed first
./tests/run_webserver.sh    # CARRIES THE BODY-IS-BYTES TIER since 2026-09-25, one for each direction. OUT: a handler returning `"a" + chr(0) + "b"` sent `Content-Length: 1` and one byte -- the count and the payload AGREEING WITH EACH OTHER, so a client checking only that they matched saw nothing amiss, which is why the tier asserts Content-Length BESIDE the bytes. IN: a request body carrying the byte was refused with 415 Unsupported Media Type, a refusal naming the one thing that is NOT the problem, since changing the media type is the only remedy a 415 suggests and it cannot help -- docs/webserver_design.md had offered 415 or 400 and left the choice to implementation, and the implementation took the less defensible one. Both are asserted through `tests/webserver_client.py`'s `request_raw`, which keeps the body as BYTES on both legs, since a golden cannot carry a raw NUL and a shell reading the output would strip it; each perturbation reddens only its own assertion
./tests/run_scope.sh       # WHAT MAY SHARE A NAME WITH WHAT -- two core-language changes, and the CONTROLS outnumber the refusals because both changes are about ONE scope. (1) DEFINING THE SAME FUNCTION TWICE IN ONE SCOPE IS REFUSED. It was silent: the second registration overwrote the first's stmt and returned, so the first became unreachable with nothing said, and the program ran the definition the author is less likely to be looking at. Scope is the FILE for a local and the LIBRARY for an imported one; three registration paths each need their own case (top-level walk, program-block hoisting, library import) and the library one is why `function_find_in_library` exists at all -- `function_find_local` matches only NON-imported entries so it cannot see two definitions inside one library. NOTHING IN THE TREE HAD A DUPLICATE: the gate was green before any fixture changed, which is the evidence it is a pure tightening. (2) A FUNCTION FROM ANOTHER LIBRARY MUST BE QUALIFIED. An unqualified name now reaches exactly two things -- the library whose code is RUNNING, and the root program's own functions. MEASURED BEFORE REMOVING THE THIRD STEP by instrumenting function_resolve over the whole gate: 4,311 resolutions went through the backward scan, 3,931 of them ONE LIBRARY REACHING ANOTHER (stats.bas -> matrix, five names), and of the 177 distinct library.function pairs root programs reached, EXACTLY ONE name was defined by more than one library -- so the scan was almost never choosing between candidates, it was resolving a unique name by a rule that could have chosen wrongly by LOAD ORDER, which the author of the call neither controls nor can see. THE ERROR TEACHES THE FIX and that is what makes the refusal affordable: it names the library and spells `stats.ols(...)`, and where several libraries define the name it offers each rather than choosing. Asserted with a CONTROL that a name NO library defines still gets the plain message, or the hint would be manufactured rather than looked up. THE EXEMPTION HELD IN ONLY TWO OF THREE SHAPES: a library in its own file worked, a library declared in the SAME FILE as its program NEVER DID (the own-first rule was keyed on the source PATH and a same-file library has none) and a MODIFIER BODY never did (invoked by a different path than invoke_function, so `dates`'s `end of month` could not call `_end_of_month`). Both were invisible while the cross-library scan caught the call on the way past; removing it exposed them, and the fix keys on the LIBRARY and threads it through invoke_function and both modifier sites. TWO THINGS FELL OUT: UNLEARN's `x(mod) = v` clause ambiguity CAN NO LONGER OCCUR (an unqualified library call with an identifier argument used to parse as a clause and fail with `compare modifier not found: x`, naming your own argument), and the library-override warning is GONE because there is no override left to warn about. Migration was ~400 call sites, both statistics cookbooks, and the server block's serve/emit/finish. Four perturbations proven red. NOTE the auto-qualifier that did the migration damaged two DECLARATIONS a call-shaped regex cannot tell from a call -- `function serve(` in stdlib/web.bas and `server sse( port: 0 )`, a server BLOCK NAME -- both caught by the gate, and both the reason the sweep was audited with `grep "function web\."` afterwards
./tests/run_alias.sh       # `load NAME as ALIAS` -- the name a FILE calls a library by. A library used to be reachable by exactly ONE name, the one on its own `library` block, claimed by whichever file happened to declare it -- so two libraries that picked the same name could not coexist in one program AT ALL. MEASURED with two throwaway files each declaring `library util`: the second import met the duplicate-function refusal and stopped with "function 'hello' is defined twice in library 'util'", which names a function NEITHER FILE DEFINED TWICE and never mentions that there are two files. AN ALIAS IS AN IMPORT IDENTITY, NOT A LOOKUP-TIME RENAME, and that choice is the whole feature: registration keys on the EFFECTIVE name, which is what lets one declared name arrive from two paths at once -- a scoped rename would have bought the typing convenience and left the collision exactly where it was, since both imports would still register under the one declared name (PERTURBATION 1 IS EXACTLY THAT and reproduces the original error verbatim). THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS THE PAIR OF VENDORS AND IT IS ASSERTED AS A DIFFERENCE: "both aliases resolved" is satisfied by an implementation that merged the two imports and answered both from whichever won, so the two libraries DISAGREE about what their shared functions return and the fixture says which answer it wants from which name. THE CONTROLS OUTNUMBER THE REFUSALS deliberately, because aliasing touches the path every qualified call in the language goes down and what must be shown is mostly that NOTHING ELSE MOVED -- an unaliased load unchanged, an aliased library still reaching its own functions unqualified, its DEPENDENCIES keeping their own names (an alias is the importing file's word for one library, not a renaming that reaches into what that library loads), and its exported MODIFIERS still resolving, three different resolvers none of which knows about the other two. AN ALIAS REPLACES THE DECLARED NAME rather than adding to it, since two names for one import is the ambiguity this removes; the call to the replaced name names the alias in force, because a rename that MISSED A CALL SITE is the mistake this feature makes easiest to write. 7 refusals: effective names are a flat namespace, so an alias may not be another library's name (both orders -- a rule that holds one way around is a rule about statement order), another alias, or a BUILT-IN MODULE, and a built-in may not be aliased, since `sqlite.`/`gi.` are intercepted by string BEFORE user-function resolution and an alias could never reach one. The two-libraries-one-name collision is now ONE message naming both files and spelling the `as` that fixes it. Diagnostic tiers read what the binary SAID rather than the committed golden, or they would pass on a build whose messages had gone. Four perturbations proven red, each caught by the tier written for it -- and perturbation 4 (the dependency renamed too) was caught by the collision refusal itself, which doubles as a guard on the implementation. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_namespace.sh   # `library_collisions()` and `password_hash_cost()` -- the two audit builtins the gdash session asked for (GDASH-4 step 0, 2026-08-30). WHY THE FIRST EXISTS: gBASIC already warns when one library function overrides another, but that warning lives in the UNQUALIFIED-LOOKUP path, so it is CALL-TRIGGERED -- two libraries sharing a name stay silent for as long as every call is qualified, and it fires only when a bare name has to choose. gdash built a namespace sweep whose entire assertion was "the interpreter said nothing" and hit exactly this: `persist` and its own `gdash_paths` both define `ensure_dir`, silently, while a `resolve` colliding with `web.resolve` DID warn -- same code path, different call sites. The builtin reports the LATENT state, before anyone writes the call that makes it live. IT IS NOT AN ERROR CONDITION AND A CALLER MUST NOT ASSERT IT IS EMPTY: stdlib itself has seven benign shared names (at, create, merge, offline, select, series, with_transport), so a program loading `dates` and `frame` legitimately reports `select` -- which is also why a LOAD-TIME warning was rejected, since it would fire on every such program. The useful shape is an allowlist or a baseline, and the reference documents it that way. THE CONTROL TIER is what keeps the fixture honest twice over: a builtin returning an EMPTY array always would be indistinguishable from a clean namespace (the exact false reassurance this removes), and one returning EVERY imported name would pass the positive checks -- so the fixture plants a collision AND asserts that names unique to one library are absent. A live tier runs against real stdlib, asserting the dates/frame `select` collision is found and that one library alone reports none. password_hash_cost is MEASURED not declared -- it performs one real hash and times it, because parameters alone do not say what they cost on the machine in front of you, which is the number an operator needs; `password_hash` takes no options, so this is how a deployment states its KDF posture instead of assuming it. Its prefix is the PARAMETER field only (`$y$j9T$`, through the third `$`): including the salt would make two postures incomparable, which is the whole use, and the round-trip check caught that off-by-one. Headless; the password tier needs libxcrypt
./tests/run_web_form.sh    # `req.form` -- the request body decoded as application/x-www-form-urlencoded. Every web application needs it and until now every one WROTE it: `req.body` was raw, so a login form was thirty lines of hand-rolled percent-decoding per application, independently wrong in a different place each time (reported by the gdash session while surveying what auth needs, 2026-08-30). IT SHARES THE QUERY PARSER because a form body and a query string are the SAME GRAMMAR -- pairs joined by `&`, percent-decoded, `+` meaning space -- so a second decoder would be a second set of bugs; `+` handling and the decoder were already there and correct. THE CONTENT-TYPE TIER IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS: a JSON body split on `&` and `=` yields a field named `{"a"` holding `1}`, which is a PLAUSIBLE-LOOKING RECORD and the wrong one, so only a form content type is decoded and everything else gives an EMPTY record -- and the tier asserts BOTH halves, since a decoder returning empty for everything would pass the negative cases alone. Also pins that an ENCODED separator does not split a pair (`p%40ss%26word` -> `p@ss&word`, the case naive split-then-decode gets wrong), that a `; charset=` parameter is tolerated, and that a longer type merely STARTING with the form type is refused. Multipart is deliberately not decoded: file uploads need a different parser and a decision about where the bytes go. CARRIES THE `%00` TIER since 2026-09-25 (PLAT-NUL's door sweep): `%00` is the RFC-correct way a NUL reaches a form field or a query parameter, so this is the REALISTIC door rather than a corner of one -- and `webserver_percent_decode` COUNTED ITS OWN OUTPUT INTERNALLY AND THEN RETURNED A BARE POINTER, so every caller re-measured with `strlen` and `a=x%00y` arrived as ONE BYTE. A handler validating `req.form.name` was therefore checking a string the client had not sent, which is the NUL-injection shape exactly and the same argument the LDAP report that started PLAT-NUL made one layer up. THE NAME SIDE WAS WORSE THAN TRUNCATION: two fields differing only after a NUL COLLAPSED INTO ONE, the second silently overwriting the first -- the record-field-name defect already fixed at the storage level, reintroduced here because that fix stopped at storage, and the tier asserts a COUNT (two fields) for the same reason the record tier does. Reported as HEX, because a NUL cannot survive a shell command substitution and a tier comparing the raw bytes would silently compare the truncations; with the CONTROL that a value without an escape is unchanged. Client is curl, loopback only; skips without curl
./tests/run_web_bind.sh    # PLAT-WEB-1 Gap A: `webserver.listen(port, {address:})`, and the default that must NOT move. `listen` bound 127.0.0.1 unconditionally, so the PLAT-WEB design's `server myapp( port: 8080 )` could not produce a server anything else could reach. Adding the option is small; the risk it carries is why this suite exists, because making a listener reachable is a security-relevant act and NO existing golden prints a bind address -- a default silently changed to 0.0.0.0 would publish every gBASIC server on the box and every test would still pass. Two devices keep the tiers from being vacuous: `server.address` is read back from getsockname() (so asserting it asks the KERNEL what happened rather than echoing our own argument), and every tier checks that string against REACHABILITY using 127.0.0.2 -- a second loopback address every Linux has and a 127.0.0.1-bound socket does not answer on. Each tier therefore probes TWO addresses and requires OPPOSITE answers, which fails a build that reports the right string while binding the wrong interface. Tiers: DEFAULT-LOOPBACK (127.0.0.1 answers, 127.0.0.2 does not -- the security property), EXPLICIT-WILDCARD (both answer), EXPLICIT-SPECIFIC (the mirror image: binding 127.0.0.2 must EXCLUDE 127.0.0.1, which no wildcard-vs-loopback test can distinguish alone), IPV6, V4-MAPPED-PEER (a dual-stack listener reports an IPv4 peer as a dotted quad, not ::ffff:127.0.0.1 -- otherwise `trust_proxy "127.0.0.1"`-style literal comparisons fail silently and treat a trusted proxy as a stranger), GRACEFUL-SHUTDOWN, REFUSAL (5 pinned messages, also wired into run_negative.sh: a hostname is refused rather than resolved, and an unknown option field is refused BY NAME since a misspelling that was ignored would leave a server the author asked to publish sitting on loopback), VALGRIND. Client is bash's own /dev/tcp -- no curl, python3 or nc -- and every address is loopback, so nothing leaves the machine. Never skips bar IPv6/valgrind. NOTE the fixture writes no trailing newline, so `read` reports EOF after assigning: under `set -e` outside an `if` that ends the run silently
./tests/run_gbasic_site.sh
./tests/run_frontend.sh    # the only suite that LINKS libgbasic.a from C instead of running ./gbasic, so it is what protects the embedding API (the LSP, and anything hosting the parser). Three programs: test_diagnostics_sink exercises the sink struct with no lexer, parser or evaluator involved -- a contract test in isolation, which is the only way to tell a sink bug from a reporting bug; test_parse_diagnostics requires three known errors to arrive as three STRUCTURED records with exact spans rather than three lines of stderr -- and how it gets three is itself the honest part: gBASIC has NO parser error recovery, a parse aborts at the first error, so it parses three ONE-ERROR buffers into ONE SHARED sink rather than pretending a single parse reports more than it can; test_two_contexts is the REENTRANCY proof -- two pthreads each parsing 500 times through gb_parse, every call required to return 0 with the right statement count. Before Phase 2 the Bison parser was global (shared yylval/yylloc/yychar/parser stack, plus file-scope active_lexer and parsed_program), so concurrent parses corrupted each other or crashed; the ctx is now stack-allocated and threaded through yyparse/yylex/yyerror. NOTE this is a structural suite -- it asserts objects and counts, never stderr text -- which is deliberate: a sink test that reads the printed form is testing the printer. Skips cleanly with no C compiler
./tests/run_json_diagnostics.sh  # --json-diagnostics golden (also asserts default byte-exact)
./tests/lsp/run_lsp.sh      # gbasic-lsp: position-transcode unit + framed JSON-RPC handshake golden
./tests/run_process.sh      # general process API: process.run (NAP-6) + PLAT-PROC live child control (process.start/poll/read/wait/stop/release). GI-independent, never skips. PLAT-PROC cases are deterministic by construction, not timing: children that must be observed mid-run block on a gate file the parent creates. Covers incremental non-blocking reads, nonzero exit, >64KB on both streams without deadlock, stop-by-escalation (SIGTERM-only vs force_after->SIGKILL, incl. a child that ignores SIGTERM), interleaved read/poll, byte fidelity across a mid-codepoint chunk boundary, use inside a spawned actor, and an abandoned-handle fd/zombie audit (SKIPs the zombie tier if `ps` is absent). PLUS WRITING TO A CHILD (`process.write`/`close_stdin`/`stdin: "pipe"`, 13 checks): `process.start` handed back a child you could only LISTEN to, which is enough for a command and not for a CONVERSATION -- and a stdio MCP transport is a conversation. Opt-in because the default is inheritance and that is what a shelled-out command wants, so the tier asserts the DEFAULT STILL INHERITS beside the two-way case, or "stdin works" would be satisfied by a change that piped every child. The write pipe is BLOCKING unlike the read pipes -- a partial write leaves the caller holding half a message with no framing to resynchronise on -- and a child that closed its stdin or exited is a SHORT WRITE, NOT A RAISE, since for a conversation that is an ordinary outcome and process.poll can say why. AND A NUL IS CONTENT AT A BRAND NEW STRING-READING SITE: PLAT-NUL's standing lesson is that a truncation defect found in one place is evidence about EVERY place that reads a string, so the tier writes `ab\0cd` and requires 5 bytes written and 5 echoed back -- a `write` measuring its argument with strlen would send three, report three and be echoed three, an ordinary-looking short write with nothing raised. The interleaved case reads in a BOUNDED LOOP rather than after a sleep, since a bare sleep asserts how fast this machine happened to be and fails in the direction that looks like the feature is broken
./tests/run_json_strict.sh # strict JSON (json_encode / json_encodable), and the suite is built around one refusal: it never validates with gBASIC's OWN `decode`. Two tiers. GOLDEN pins the type mapping, the escaping, and -- explicitly -- that the native encode/decode dialect is UNCHANGED. STANDARDS PROOF parses every document json_encode emitted with python3's json module, an INDEPENDENT implementation, because `decode` deliberately accepts the historical dialect (bare `nothing`/`unknown`, and since rc7 `inf`/`nan`), so a round trip through it would prove exactly nothing about standards compliance -- which is the trap this whole suite exists to avoid. Tier 2b re-validates every request body the llm tool path puts on the wire the same way, because that is where the defect was found: an assistant tool-call turn carries "content": null, which replayed through `encode` became `content:nothing` and would have made every tool continuation malformed (DOGFOOD 2026-07-24). Skips without python3
./tests/run_stream.sh       # PLAT-STREAM `--line-buffered`: the opt-in prompt-stdout flag. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar the valgrind tier). Gate-file determinism throughout — child fixtures publish a READY file only after the print under test has executed, so a read at that instant is provably post-print. Covers: streaming mid-run WITH the flag and provably not without it, a partial line (`input` prompt — already fflushed by the interpreter, so identical both ways) plus output that ends with no trailing newline, a 20 000-line child checked byte-identical against the unflagged run with every line verified, composition with `--json-diagnostics` in either flag order (stderr byte-identical three ways), a child killed by SIGTERM mid-output (flushed bytes survive; unflagged, nothing does), opt-in proof, and that a flag-looking argument after FILE still reaches the program
./tests/run_try_decode.sh  # PLAT-JSON `try_decode(text)`: decode that reports failure as a VALUE ({ok,value,message,offset,line,column}) instead of raising. Shares decode's parser -- no second JSON implementation -- so both accept the same dialect and diagnose an input identically; a PARITY tier asserts the reported text equals the raised text across 9 malformed classes, and a RAISE-INTACT tier asserts decode itself is unchanged. Existed originally because a raise could not be caught at all, which forced pure-gBASIC pre-validation, which is QUADRATIC (`mid` is O(i) on codepoint-indexed strings: 64KB 16s, 128KB 69s, 256KB 291s); frame-scoped `on error` (PLAT-ERR) removed that forcing, and try_decode still earns its place by reporting WHERE the JSON is malformed rather than only that it is. Also caps nesting at 10 000 levels for BOTH entry points -- unbounded parser recursion used to segfault the interpreter around 45 000 levels on an 8MB stack. Headless, no python3, valgrind tier over every malformed path
./tests/run_stderr.sh      # PLAT-STDERR `print to error`: a program's route to standard error. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar the valgrind tier); determinism from redirection, process exit and a gate file, never a clock. Covers: bytes reaching fd 2 and not fd 1 from every statement position the grammar admits (top level, function body, inline-if consequent, loop body), full rendering parity with `print` proved by emitting every value shape twice and requiring the two captures byte-identical (352 bytes, 15 value kinds), byte-level edges (empty argument, embedded newline, interior NUL, multi-byte UTF-8), interleaving at a shared destination with and without `--line-buffered` (source order vs stderr-first), stderr arriving mid-run in BOTH configurations while stdout does so only when flagged, `--json-diagnostics` JSON byte-identical whether or not the program also writes to stderr and still parseable, plus shell-level redirect/discard/pipeline cases
./tests/run_pre_registration.sh # PLAT-GUARD tripwire on the set of declarations `eval_program` pre-registers before running a `program` block. Sited with the platform, next to the code, because gBASIC Studio's STU-4B declaration-hoisting rule is defined AS that set and nothing in either file references the other. Asserts it two ways: structurally (reads the BEGIN/END PRE-REGISTRATION SET markers in `src/eval.c` — catches a kind added or removed even with no behavioural test for it) and behaviourally (a program block reaching a function, modifier, library and function value all declared below `end program`, plus the negative that a top-level statement there does NOT run). Fails with a message naming the hoisting rule as what must move with it. THE NEGATIVE GREW A DIAGNOSTIC 2026-09-07: a top-level statement beside a program block does not run, which is what that tier already asserted, and NOTHING SAID SO -- hit three times in one day building the AI stack, the worst a top-level `watch(inbox.messages)` that registered NOTHING, so every worker reply went undelivered and every conversation hung with the symptom pointing nowhere near the cause. Warning `2106`, source `dead code`, once at the first such statement, PINNED HERE BESIDE THE BEHAVIOUR it describes rather than in a suite of its own. MEASURED BEFORE SHIPPING: of 420 files with a program block, FOUR have a top-level statement and one of those is this suite's own fixture, so it costs almost nothing. A warning not a refusal because the program runs exactly as it did, and `watch` CANNOT simply be hoisted with the declarations -- a watcher FIRES on registration and would run its body before anything it reads was assigned
./tests/run_web_routes.sh  # PLAT-WEB-1 step 2: the route table as data (stdlib/web.bas) -- the routing the `server` block would eventually be SUGAR FOR, written as ordinary values so the whole scheme is testable with no socket. A router's failure mode is not a crash but a PLAUSIBLE WRONG ANSWER: "/products/new" resolving to the "/products/{id}" handler yields a perfectly ordinary page about a product whose id is the word "new", so a golden would enshrine it. Both fixtures therefore state their own expected answers and print ok or a MISMATCH naming both sides; the goldens pin that every line says ok, plus a check-count floor. THE TWO INVARIANT TIERS ARE COMPLEMENTARY AND THAT WAS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED -- each was proven red on a deliberately broken copy of the library and each caught what the other missed: ORDER-INDEPENDENCE (the same routes declared four ways must resolve every probe identically) catches first-match-wins, which the oracle does NOT because with that route set declaration order happens to agree with the rule; the ORACLE (a matcher and specificity rule REIMPLEMENTED in the fixture from the definition, reading route strings back out of web.paths) catches an inverted specificity rule, which order-independence does NOT because a consistently wrong router is still consistent across orders. Tiers: SEMANTICS+ORACLE goldens, STATIC (Gap B: `web.static` over the new `real_path`/`file_type` builtins -- canonicalize-then-check, with the tree built in the runner because it needs SYMLINKS gBASIC cannot create. Proven red TWICE: with the containment check removed, and with it applied BEFORE canonicalization, which is the ordering mistake the design warns about and which serves /etc/passwd through a symlink while looking correct. Every refusal sits beside a case that must still be SERVED -- a symlink INSIDE the root is 200 while one pointing out is 403 -- since over-blocking is as much a defect as under-blocking, plus a check that the refused file was readable all along, without which a web.static that always said 403 would pass every hostile case), REFUSAL (13 build-time rejections -- unknown verb, unrooted path, trailing slash, partial/unclosed pattern, greedy-not-last, repeated capture name, invalid capture name, empty segment, indistinguishable pair, bad handler, missing field, non-array), LIVE (the table behind a real listener on loopback: captures off the wire, handler status/headers reaching the client, 404, 405 + allow), VALGRIND. NOTE refusals are compared BY MESSAGE with the position stripped, deliberately unlike run_chart.sh: a library raise reports stdlib/web.bas:LINE:COL, so pinning whole stderr would make every edit a rebaseline of 13 files while asserting nothing -- the text is the contract, and each case carries a hand-written .msg beside it
./tests/run_web_hardening.sh # PLAT-WEB-3 §6 -- the parts of hardening that "actually bite", none of which any functional suite touches, because a correct client never sends them. Request and idle-read timeouts (a slow client must not hold a worker: an unanswered request is 504, a connection that says NOTHING is 408 on the same budget); and smuggling-resistant framing, where the rule is REFUSE, never guess -- Transfer-Encoding is 501 because this server does not speak chunked, and two Content-Lengths that DISAGREE are 400. The pair that makes those meaningful is the fourth case: two AGREEING Content-Lengths must still PARSE, proving the refusal is about the disagreement and not about duplicate headers, since an honest client with a duplicated header is not an attacker. Plus web.trust_proxy as a pure golden, pinning RIGHTMOST-UNTRUSTED -- the correction to the draft's "first hop", which is spoofable by anyone who can set a header. Client is curl and nc, never webclient. Skips without curl
./tests/run_web_stream.sh  # PLAT-WEB-4 Gap E -- streaming responses, whose whole difficulty is that a stream is NOT a slow response and must not be treated as one. Tiers: SSE_TICK (three emits from inside the handler itself); SSE_BROADCAST (a stream PARKED by one request and poked from a SECOND connection -- the shape a real event feed needs); STREAM_TIMEOUT_EXEMPT (the server runs with timeout: 1 and the suite waits 2.2s, so a plain response would have been 504'd and an idle connection 408'd -- the stream must outlive both, and this is the tier that would catch a stream accidentally sharing the request budget); STREAM_DISCONNECT (kill the listener; the next emit must return FALSE and reap it, or a dead client leaks a slot forever); FILE_DIRECT and FILE_STATIC (a 5MB file served byte-identically with Content-Length from fstat -- the point being that the bytes never become a gBASIC string, which a smaller fixture could not distinguish); STREAM_DRAIN (a TERM ends a parked stream: client EOF, worker exits 0); and the negative that keeps the two response shapes apart, since a stream carrying a body is an author who thinks they are writing one thing while writing another. Skips without curl
./tests/run_web_pool.sh    # PLAT-WEB-2 -- the process worker pool: a supervisor that HOLDS the listener, workers that inherit it over the LISTEN_FDS protocol, drain on SIGTERM (in-flight finishes, the worker exits ITSELF), the application-level `server.draining` soft stop, and the rolling reload whose rule is NEVER RETIRE A WORKER ON FAITH -- a good deploy rolls v1 to v2 with service up throughout, a deploy whose source cannot parse fails and costs nothing, and a deploy that comes up, reports ready and then DIES inside probation also fails and costs nothing. That third case is the one a naive reload passes and a correct one must not. DETERMINISM IS STRUCTURAL, not tuned: nothing steps on a timer -- in-flight requests are held by a gate FILE the driver creates, readiness is a marker the worker PRINTS, and "the request is parked in the handler" is a line waited for rather than assumed. Ports are OS-assigned (port 0) so no golden carries a number this machine chose. drain_app is driven from OUTSIDE because a single-process server only serves from the post-main event loop, and the process exiting BY ITSELF after /last IS the assertion that draining releases that loop. curl throughout: webclient would test our stack with our stack. Skips without curl
./tests/run_web_tls.sh     # PLAT-WEB-3 Gap D -- TLS termination: a default certificate, SNI selection by hostname (asserted by asking `openssl s_client` what subject it was handed, with and without -servername, rather than trusting our own report), plain HTTP to a TLS port failing FAST rather than hanging, and the POOLED shape where a plain TCP socket is inherited and TLS is terminated in the WORKER -- which is what makes certificate rotation a rolling reload and nothing more. The two negatives are about WHEN a bad pair is caught: a missing certificate and a key that does not match its certificate are both refused at LISTEN time, naming the file, rather than at the first handshake hours later when the operator has gone home. Certificates are generated per run by openssl into a scratch dir, so nothing is committed and nothing expires. Skips without curl or openssl, and separately if the build has no libssl (detected by the refusal text -- "not available in this build" rather than "could not load certificate") -- AND THAT SECOND SKIP WAS DEAD CODE until 2026-10-09, found by sweeping for run_ldap.sh's trap rather than reported. The probe fixture is a NEGATIVE that raises BY DESIGN, so gbasic exits 1, and under `pipefail` the status comes from gbasic rather than from grep: the outer `if !` was therefore ALWAYS TRUE and the inner test ALWAYS FALSE, whatever the text said. MEASURED -- grep matches and the pipeline still reports 1 -- so a build without libssl FAILED this suite (`FAIL tls_basic (no port announced)`) instead of skipping it, proven red on exactly such a build. Captured into a variable now, with a THIRD branch that FAILS LOUDLY when the probe says neither thing, since the fixture is meant to refuse for exactly one of two reasons and "something else happened" is worth naming rather than guessing at
./tests/run_web_server_block.sh # PLAT-WEB-5 -- the declarative `server` block, built LAST and only because WEB-0..4 had proved the runtime shape underneath it. The grammar adds exactly ONE reserved word, `server`, checked against the tree before it was taken; the verbs and directives are NOT reserved -- `get` stays usable as an ordinary identifier, because they are recognized by POSITION, the technique later reused for `on warning` and for keyword field names. Tiers: OFFLINE (web.routes / web.dispatch answer from the DECLARATION with no socket at all, plus the outline -- §9 introspection, and the tier that makes a route table testable without a server); HOIST (a block declared BELOW the program binds before it runs, and the declaration is inert until served); the §8 LOAD-TIME refusals, every one located and named -- unknown verb, a pattern repeating a capture name, two patterns that can NEVER be told apart, a duplicate route, a greedy capture that is not the last segment, malformed heads, and a block nested inside a function; JSON_DIAGNOSTICS (those checks reach --json-diagnostics, so an editor sees them); then the live tiers over the WEB-1..4 runtime -- minimal serve with capture/body/static-fallback/404/405, HOST DISPATCH with the server record bound nowhere but a dead frame, streams (in-handler loop, park/poke via a global record, reap), the drain hook self-exiting 0, `workers: 2` spawning the pool via process.self, and TLS configured from the head. Live tiers skip without curl; block_tls skips without openssl or libssl
./tests/run_nap_fs.sh       # NAP-10 filesystem metadata + atomic_replace; GI-independent; xdev case gated on a distinct /dev/shm, opt-in NAP_FS_STRESS=1 concurrency stress (structural coverage also in examples/nap_fs_test.gb + run_negative)
./tests/run_chart.sh        # chart.bas Phases 1+2+4 (docs/chart_design.md): line/scatter/area/bar/histogram/pie/heatmap/sparkline as DETERMINISTIC SVG text, pure gBASIC. Five truth standards, deliberately distinct: GOLDEN (byte-exact four-chart rendering: multi-series with gaps from unknown AND nan, hostile-escaped title, money at face value, date x-axis labeled as real dates, *_xy escape hatch, no-data note); ORACLE (under FIXED margins + FIXED bounds every pixel is hand-computable, so the tier asserts literals computed ON PAPER -- a golden records whatever we emit, this tier knows what we SHOULD emit; coverage floor so it cannot pass by running nothing); DETERMINISM (two runs byte-identical -- the property the SVG-as-string design exists to buy); STRUCTURE (every chart fed back through OUR OWN xml.parse: well-formedness catches escaping bugs generically -- the fixture title is R&D <script>... -- plus circles-inside-viewBox and an exact element census; SKIPs without libxml2); VALGRIND. Refusals (negative_chart_*) live in run_negative: text y-column, palette overflow, unequal columns, missing column, DUPLICATE bar category, STACKED negatives, histogram over text, pie with an UNKNOWN or NEGATIVE share (a missing share misstates the others), ragged heatmap matrix. Pie uses a pure-gBASIC Taylor sine (no trig builtins exist) that the oracle checks FROM OUTSIDE via cardinal points; heatmap colors are hand-lerped hex via hex_encode(from_bytes(...)). NOTE chart .err files pin stdlib/chart.bas LINE NUMBERS -- editing the library means regenerating all TEN -- a plausible wrong picture is worse than an error. PLUS GDASH7 (2026-09-09), the three asks from gdash after six phases of using this library on a real dashboard, and the first is the sharpest silent-failure this tree has produced: A CATEGORICAL X DREW NOTHING. A text x reached `_plottable`, which answers `unknown` for anything not a number, so every point became a gap and the renderer emitted a COMPLETE PLAUSIBLE CHART OF AN EMPTY RESULT SET from a frame that was full -- axes, gridlines, fourteen text elements and the "no data" note. `bar` handled the SAME COLUMN correctly all along, which is what made it a TRAP rather than a documented limit: moving one visual from bar to line lost the data and said nothing. "Revenue by month", whose x is almost always a period LABEL, is the commonest dashboard chart there is, so this was the DEFAULT case. GDASH'S OWN TEST ASSERTED `contains(svg, "<svg")`, WHICH AN EMPTY FRAME SATISFIES, and the blank chart survived six phases behind it -- which is why the tier here asserts a DIFFERENCE: the same series over a numeric x and a categorical x must draw THE SAME NUMBER OF MARKS. Ordinal x places points at THE SAME BAND CENTRES `bar` USES, asserted by requiring a line's markers to sit exactly on the bar's centres for one frame (with the y bound pinned so only x placement can differ), because a dashboard showing both would otherwise put January somewhere other than over the January column. A MIXED COLUMN IS REFUSED BOTH WAYS with three controls (all-numeric, all-categorical, all-dates each accepted), or "mixed is refused" is satisfied by refusing every x column there is. A REPEATED CATEGORY IS ALLOWED HERE AND STILL REFUSED BY `bar`, a deliberate difference asserted as one: a bar would have to INVENT A SUM and misstate the picture, a line invents nothing, and refusing would block a categorical scatter. AXIS LABELS gained prefix/suffix/decimals/scale -- `_scale` is DISPLAY ONLY and the tier proves it MOVES NO GEOMETRY by pinning the margin first, since a wider label legitimately reserves more room and that layout response read as the data moving in the first draft of the check; an explicit `decimals` means FIXED places, because `$4,000` from `y_decimals: 2` is a setting that appears not to work. `mark_keys` puts data-series/data-key on each DATA mark and A LEGEND SWATCH NEVER GETS ONE -- matching on DOM order is reliable only until a legend adds rects, which is the ambiguity the attribute removes, so a keyed swatch would reintroduce it (proven red). Off by default, so every existing golden is byte-identical (proven: turning it on moves 4). AND AN UNKNOWN OPTION IS REFUSED BY NAME with a near-miss hint (`ylabel` -> "did you mean 'y_label'?"), checked in `render` as well as `options` so a hand-built spec cannot smuggle a typo past. Ten perturbations proven red. A gBASIC TRAP HIT WHILE WRITING IT, and the file's own comment warns about it thirty lines up: an inline `if` immediately before an `else` line is CAPTURED BY IT. NOTE the library is chart.spec not chart.new: a library cannot DEFINE a function named new (keyword); and watch the inline-if-before-else trap plus append-mutates-a-LOCAL-COPY inside functions, both hit while building this
./tests/run_rules.sh      # `rules` -- typed business rules as DATA (stdlib/rules.bas, docs/gbasic-rules-library-proposal.md). FIRST INCREMENT AND A VERTICAL SLICE, not a layer: the proposal's own sequence puts "specify record schemas" first and the stack-versus-condensed agreement property fifth, and BOTH are here, because a schema chosen before a real rule has been encoded through it is a guess and a condensation whose agreement with its source is first checked two increments later is a condensation nobody can still change. THE FIRST RULE ENCODED IS THE PROPOSAL'S OWN WORKED CONTRACT, unchanged -- 80% of the eligible amount, capped at USD 500, the cap rising to USD 600 for service on or after 2027-01-01 -- and it reproduces that document's stated figures (gross 640, payment 500 then 600); separately, the proposal's allow/emergency/frozen example condenses to the SAME NESTED CONDITIONAL it predicted as the "exact condensed form" -- same branches, same operators, same order, differing only in rendering a binding by its label and parenthesising the inner comparison. That agreement is the evidence the representation was specified correctly, and it is why the real rule was encoded BEFORE the schemas were written down. SELF-CHECKING NOT GOLDEN AND FORCED: every defect this library can have produces A PLAUSIBLE DECISION -- a payment capped at the wrong figure, a condition that quietly became false, an "unresolved" where the rule was actually wrong -- so a golden would record the wrong payment as expected and defend it, which is how a decision engine comes to be trusted while being wrong. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS AGREEMENT over GENERATED stacks (tests/rules_agreement.bas via `fake`), and the two paths are GENUINELY DIFFERENT CODE, which is what makes agreement evidence rather than a tautology: `evaluate` walks the rules FORWARD and evaluates every condition whether or not a later rule overwrites the result, `evaluate_compiled` walks nested conditionals LATEST-FIRST and short-circuits -- opposite orders, and the compiled one visits fewer. So agreement is asserted on the TARGET RESULTS and deliberately NOT on the node counters or the trace, since the two are MEANT to do different amounts of work. AND THE CENSUS IS A TIER BECAUSE THE FIRST GENERATOR AGREED PERFECTLY WHILE EXERCISING NOTHING: measured, 200 stacks gave 9 conditions that fired against 184 that did not and 69% of all results were errors, so the property was being checked almost entirely on stacks where no override ever applied -- the one case that needs no ordering to get right -- and it reported zero disagreements, truthfully and uninformatively. Conditions come from a boolean-shaped generator now; 2000 stacks, 3947 target results, 0 disagreements, with floors on results in all three states, both absence kinds, and conditions that fired as well as conditions that did not. THREE PERTURBATIONS OF THE FOLD PROVEN RED (inverted 94, an absent condition silently becoming false 93, an absent IF taking the else branch 95) AND A FOURTH PROVEN GREEN ON PURPOSE -- the unconditional collapse replaced by an equivalent `IF true` -- which is what says the property catches semantic divergence rather than any edit. THE ABSENCE SEMANTICS DIVERGE FROM THE HOST DELIBERATELY AND THE DIVERGENCE IS MEASURED: gBASIC RAISES on `not unknown`, `true and unknown`, `false or unknown`, `unknown + 1` and `unknown > 1`, and answers `false` on `unknown = 1`; the rule language propagates and reports what it waits for, because in a negotiated agreement a missing fact is an ORDINARY BUSINESS CONDITION and a decision engine that aborts on it cannot do its job. THE SAFETY RAIL IS THAT INVALIDITY DOES NOT BECOME ABSENCE, asserted BOTH WAYS as one tier: a missing service date leaves the cap `absent` with `res.ok` still TRUE and nothing recorded as an error, while a USD cap against a EUR gross is an `error` carrying the host's own sentence with `res.ok` FALSE and nothing reported as waiting -- either half alone passes on a library that always says one thing. The static half is asserted too (two LITERAL currencies are a validation problem with a remedy naming money.convert) and so is its limit: a binding declared `money` does not say which currency, so that mismatch can only surface at evaluation. "SHORT-CIRCUIT" TURNED OUT TOO STRONG and the business reading decided it: taken literally, `unknown AND false` answers unknown, where what a reader wants is FALSE -- "we have not got the inspection date, but the account is closed, so no" -- so the logic is KLEENE'S and short-circuit applies only where the left operand DETERMINES the answer, asserted with a right operand that is a REAL ERROR so the only way those checks pass is by never looking at it. `!>` IS NEVER REWRITTEN TO `<=` and the fixture asserts the two DIFFER rather than asserting either value, because `not (nan > 1)` is true while `nan <= 1` is false -- a value check alone passes on a library that rewrote the operator. THE DEPTH CAP IS MEASURED AGAINST THE CRASH, which is the tier nobody would write from the proposal: it cited `try_decode`'s 10,000-level cap, that is a cap on a C walk, and THIS walk is gBASIC -- whose own recursion segfaults with NO DIAGNOSTIC AND NO OUTPUT between 380 and 400 levels for this evaluator, so 10,000 would be a cap that never fires in front of a crash that always does. Cap is 100 and the tier asserts the MARGIN (lift the cap and the interpreter must still survive twice the shipped cap), which goes red if anybody raises it toward the crash or if a frame gets dearer; lifting it in a perturbation makes the FIXTURE segfault, caught by the runner's nonzero-exit guard and its check-count floor rather than by any assertion, which is why both of those exist. AND THE REFUSAL LOOP STOPS AT 3,000 FOR A REASON THAT IS NOT THIS LIBRARY'S: a nested gBASIC VALUE around 22,000 deep (40,000 for arrays) segfaults the interpreter AT TEARDOWN -- the program prints its answer, succeeds, then exit 139 -- measured with no library involved and filed in DOGFOOD, and the consequence is a requirement on the unbuilt deserializer, which must bound depth WHILE PARSING because once the value exists a refusal is too late and merely letting it go out of scope is the crash. AND A TRIPWIRE THIS LIBRARY HAD NEVER HEARD OF CAUGHT IT ANYWAY, which is the argument for the gate rather than for the library: run_absence.sh counts hand-rolled set-union loops ACROSS THE WHOLE STDLIB BY SHAPE and holds the tree to a measured 10..20, the stated reason being that the survivors append a TRANSFORM which `excluding` cannot express without a `map`. The first version of rules.bas added FIVE and pushed the count to 22, all of them plain unions where the haystack and the destination were the same list -- so the tripwire was exactly right, and its message is the one to quote: re-measure, do not just move the bound. They are `unique(concat(a, b))` now and the count is back to 17. Nothing in this library references that suite and nothing in that suite references this library. TWO SOURCE TRIPWIRES for what no behavioural check can see: the registry and the hand-written dispatch must be ONE list (the direction that drifts silently is a key the dispatch honours and the registry does not, so validation would refuse what the evaluator runs, or the reverse), and the library may not contain `bound(` -- WITH the control that the scanner flags a planted one, since "no offenders" is equally satisfied by a scanner that read nothing. THAT SCANNER'S CONTROL COST A RUN AND IS THE pipefail TRAP AGAIN IN A NEW DISGUISE: `code_only FILE | grep -q PATTERN` fails THE WHOLE PIPELINE WHEN grep MATCHES, because grep -q exits on the first hit, closes the pipe, and `sed` dies of SIGPIPE at 141 -- so the control reported "the scanner did not flag a planted bound()" against a scanner that had just flagged it. Third time in this tree from the same shell option (run_ldap's skip probe and run_web_tls's, both via a deliberate nonzero; this one via SIGPIPE, the variant neither of those notes would have warned about). Captured into a variable now. Six more perturbations proven red on the fixture, each caught by the tier written for it: invalidity laundered into absence, AND short-circuiting strictly, the negated comparisons rewritten, a function value made live, equality against unknown answering false, and a missing fact becoming zero. VALIDATION REPORTS EVERY PROBLEM, NOT THE FIRST (six independent mistakes, six problems), each carrying the RULE'S OWN ID rather than an array index -- the author reordered the stack and the index moved -- plus the target, condition-or-replacement, found against wanted, THE AUTHOR'S OWN SPELLING and a remedy; `<>` is refused BY NAME and its remedy is gBASIC's own sentence verbatim, because the readers who write `<>` in a rule are the readers who will write it in gBASIC. One check the proposal did not ask for and the first authoring mistake suggested: A CONDITION THAT CANNOT POSSIBLY BE A BOOLEAN, where the common form is not a literal but writing the QUANTITY where the comparison was meant (`amount + cap` for `amount >= cap`). DELIBERATELY NARROW and that is the design: only a literal of the wrong type and an operator whose result can never be boolean, because a binding or a registered call could be anything and guessing would make it a diagnostic that is SOMETIMES A LIE. FIVE CONTROLS SIT BESIDE THE TWO POSITIVES, since the rule fires on a SHAPE and over-firing on correct rules is the failure that got the blind-shadow warning measured at 287 false positives and reverted. A MALFORMED STACK WAS A HOLE RATHER THAN A DECISION, found by trying it: a stack record with no `rules` array RAISED `unknown record field: rules` straight out of `evaluate` -- so a caller told failure is a value got an exception -- and a stack that was not a record at all came back `ok: true`, which is worse, being a successful-looking decision about a string. `validate` refused both all along, which is NECESSARY AND NOT SUFFICIENT, because nothing obliges a caller to validate before evaluating and the one who forgets is holding attacker-supplied data. All three entrances share one shape guard now, WITH the control that a well-formed stack is still accepted. NOT BUILT AND SAID SO: the textual authoring layer (the proposal's nine precedence levels stay the specification it will be built to), live variable bindings (which would open security door 3), persistence (door 4), and the controlled English templates -- the registry carries an unused `template` field waiting for them. 125 checks, ~6s plus valgrind. Headless, no network, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_set_ops.sh   # `excluding` / `intersecting` as INFIX WORD OPERATORS (0.6.0 increment 3), measured into the grammar rather than chosen: the operator form costs ZERO bison conflicts, which is why it is spelled `a excluding b` and not `excluding(a, b)` -- a project that rejected `IDENT expression` as a statement form over FOUR conflicts does not take a spelling on taste. Membership is `=`, so PLAT-EQ's deep comparison means records compare BY VALUE, and duplicates on the left are PRESERVED (`unique` is a separate question, asserted so the two cannot silently merge). THE COST TIER IS A NEGATIVE CONTROL ON THE REFERENCE: docs/reference.md states the cost is the PRODUCT of the two lengths, so a suite has to assert it -- bounded BOTH ways, above because worse than the product means something became cubic, and below because if it ever gets cheaper the REFERENCE IS WRONG, which is how this tree's performance notes went stale for six weeks. The data is DISJOINT on purpose, since membership short-circuits on a match and overlapping data measures how early the matches happen rather than the shape. **AND THAT TIER WENT RED IN THE PARALLEL GATE ON 2026-10-19 OVER THE MACHINE RATHER THAN THE CODE** -- ratio 2.02 inside the gate against 4.06 alone on the same binary -- which QUALIFIES THIS FILE'S OWN ARGUMENT that a ratio is self-normalising: it is, only if BOTH HALVES SEE THE SAME LOAD, and measured once each in sequence they need not. THE QUALIFIER IS NARROWER THAN THE FIRST DIAGNOSIS AND WAS MEASURED: steady load does NOT reproduce it (under fourteen busy loops the old shape reported 3.6, 4.3, 4.38 and 4.0, all inside the band), so what skews it is a load TRANSITION between the two samples, which is exactly what the end of a parallel gate is as its workers drain. The fixture ALTERNATES the two sizes and keeps each one's SMALLEST sample, so both halves sit in the same load window however the load moves: 4.01/4.04/4.04 idle and 4.1/4.12/4.1/3.04 under the same fourteen hogs, where the small run is 40ms and one scheduler stall can double it. The band is UNCHANGED, deliberately -- widening it would have bought the green line and thrown away what the tier measures. Headless, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_notation.sh # `notation` -- a TEXTUAL form that keeps EVERY gBASIC type (stdlib/notation.bas, docs/text_serialization_design.md). THE GAP THE OTHER THREE SERIALIZERS LEAVE: `encode` is readable and REFUSES a date, money, a duration or a file; `serialize` keeps every type and is OPAQUE BINARY -- so nothing could be opened in an editor, read and hand-edited while surviving a typed value, which is most of what a business record is made of. THE TAG GOES ON THE KEY SIDE and that is not a preference: both brace positions already mean something in gBASIC and they DIFFER -- before the separator is `x {date}= "..."`, THIS VALUE IS OF THIS TYPE; after the value is a comparison LENS, which is not merely ambiguous but LEXICALLY CLAIMED (lexer_begin_lens_content fires the moment a brace follows a value). Measured, key-side costs 0 grammar conflicts where value-side costs 1, and adding the nameless element form to gBASIC's own grammar BREAKS `{ x: 1 }` EVERYWHERE -- which is why the format has its OWN parser and its output is NOT gBASIC source. Pasteability was never reachable anyway: gBASIC refuses `\u{0}` in a literal (DOGFOOD -- the AST stores a literal as a C string so it would truncate at evaluation) while a field NAME may now hold an interior NUL. SELF-CHECKING NOT GOLDEN, forced: every defect a serializer can have is a PLAUSIBLE DOCUMENT -- money rounded to the minor unit still reads like money, a date decoded as a string still reads like a date. THE LOAD-BEARING PROPERTY IS THE ROUND TRIP AND IT NEEDS TWO CONTROLS or it is satisfied by `serialize`, which already keeps every type and is binary: the output must be LEGIBLE (newlines, indentation, the value's own text, the tag on the key side) and a defect that ROUND-TRIPS must still be caught -- money's sub-cent case, where `string` gives 3.46 for 3.459 and an encoder built on it round-trips 3.46 to 3.46, self-consistently wrong. THREE DEFECTS THE ROUND TRIP FOUND, none visible by reading: a NESTED ARRAY'S DEFAULT WAS COMPUTED AND THEN DROPPED (the encoder looked one level, the decoder cascades, so a matrix of dates came back as PLAIN STRINGS); A RE-RAISE ISSUED WHILE `on error goto next` WAS STILL ARMED VANISHED, since PLAT-ERR's frame scoping meant the frame caught its OWN raise and EVERY REFUSAL SILENTLY STOPPED REFUSING, five checks red in one edit; and A RAW NUL ROUND-TRIPS PERFECTLY so nothing forced the escape -- the tier now asserts the file contains NO RAW CONTROL BYTE rather than merely that the value survives, because a text file hostile to the editor it exists to be opened in is not a text file. Ten perturbations proven red. A HAND-EDIT TIER asserts whitespace is not load-bearing (the same document reflowed, comma-spaced and comment-prefixed decodes the same) WITH the control that editing a number actually changes it. Headless, no network, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_persist.sh      # persist (stdlib/persist.bas): crash-safe versioned persistence -- atomic temp-file+rename write, and a read that reports missing/corrupt/loaded as a VALUE via try_decode rather than raising. Four cases inherited from Studio's STU-STORE tier when Studio moved to its own project (~/development/gbasic-studio): the three states unchanged, a corrupt file reporting WHY with the parser's reason and position, every value shape round-tripping byte-identically, and a 115KB/240-record index opening in under 5s -- the pure-gBASIC validator it replaced took 92s on the same input
./tests/run_outline.sh      # PLAT-OUTLINE source_outline(text) builtin (general in-process structural outline over the reentrant gb_parse): headless, GI-independent, path-free goldens. 12 fixtures (empty/one/program/functions/modifiers/nested/consider/multiline/comments/invalid/unmatched/unicode) dump kind/name/half-open BYTE range/line:col/flags + a byte-exact slice of every node + containment self-check + diagnostics; plus large-file (exact 25000-node count + timing ceiling), 50x repeated-call stability, and a valgrind tier (SKIPs if valgrind absent)
./tests/run_stridx.sh       # PLAT-STRIDX string access that is not quadratic in the string's size. Two costs made every per-character loop O(n^2), and both are gone: reading a string VARIABLE deep-copied the buffer (env_get -> value_copy), so even the O(1) `byte_at(s,i)` cost O(n) per call (1 MB loop: 30 s); and `len`/`mid`/`left`/`right` each re-walked the whole string to count codepoints (256 000-char forward scan: 249 s). Fixed in the StringHeader that already sat in front of every string: `refs` (share, don't copy -- sound because string values are immutable, `as.string` being assigned in exactly one place), a memoized `cp_count` + forward cursor, and a lazily-built sparse cp->byte index (one sample per 64 codepoints) for multibyte strings accessed out of forward order, without which BACKWARD scans stay quadratic (256 000 units: 152 s -> 0.31 s). Costs 40 bytes per string value, measured. Tiers: CORRECTNESS (tests/stridx_test.bas -- forward/backward/random/alternating access, ASCII and multibyte, invalid UTF-8, interior NULs, rebinding between accesses, aliasing, empty/single-unit; golden captured BEFORE the change and byte-identical after, so a moved line means semantics moved), SHAPE (7 cases assert the RATIO across a 4x size step, gate 8x where quadratic is ~16x, never an absolute time), CEILING (one very generous absolute bound), VALGRIND. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_arridx.sh       # PLAT-ARRIDX arrays: aliasing + complexity class. Arrays became a shared refcounted store with copy-on-write on 2026-07-23 (docs/array_cow_design.md), so `arr[i]` in a while loop and `append` are LINEAR — the O(n^2) warnings that survived in docs until 2026-07-29 were stale. This suite exists because nothing guarded that: examples/array_cow_test.bas pins SEMANTICS, nothing pinned COST. Tiers: ALIASING (tests/arridx_test.bas — the cases array_cow_test does not reach: an element assigned FROM THE SAME ARRAY, where the RHS is read from the store the write is about to free; mutation during `for each`; capture-then-mutate both directions; plus every in-place mutator against a live alias, one per detach call site), SHAPE (7 patterns, ratio across a 4x step, gate 8x), CONTROL (a NEGATIVE control — these tiers cannot be proven red since there is no defect left, so the same harness is pointed at still-quadratic string concatenation and REQUIRED to exceed the gate; reports ~20x. If it stops failing, the shape tiers measure nothing), VALGRIND. Headless, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_recidx.sh       # PLAT-RECIDX records as a map: a record was an association list walked with `strcmp`, AND reading a record VARIABLE deep-copied the whole field array (env_get -> value_copy). Either cost alone keeps a per-key loop quadratic — the same pairing PLAT-STRIDX documents for strings, and the reason the first version of this fix measured 85x faster on the BUILD path while every READ still cost O(fields) and threw the freshly built index away (DOGFOOD 2026-08-13). Both are gone: a lazily-built hash index from name to slot, in a header riding in FRONT of the RecordField array (so `as.record.fields` still points at field 0 and the ~80 sites reading it are untouched — the StringHeader trick), built only above RECORD_INDEX_MIN_FIELDS since records are overwhelmingly small; plus the array shared and refcounted with copy-on-write (record_ensure_unique), the ArrayStorage pattern one level in. Measured: 32 000 fields 3.42s -> 0.04s, 128 000 fields 0.13s (previously not finishing usefully), and 300 000 lookups of ONE key flat at ~0.3s where they were 0.65s against 1 000 fields and 8.41s against 8 000. DETACHING IS THE DELICATE PART: a `Value` is both owner and borrow with nothing in the type to say which, so detaching inside `record_find` decremented refcounts borrows never held and double-freed (the webserver died on its third request); detaching happens only where ownership is structural — `resolve_lvalue_ref` and `record_set` — which is the rule `array_ensure_unique` already followed. Tiers: SEMANTICS (tests/recidx_test.bas, self-checking — every line states its own expected value computed from n and prints `ok` or a MISMATCH naming both sides, so the golden cannot enshrine a wrong answer), PARITY (every check runs TWICE, on a record below the index threshold and above, making the linear walk the ORACLE for the indexed path; the two blocks are diffed against each other with the label stripped — this is what catches duplicate names resolving to the last match instead of the first, which is possible since `select 1 as x, 2 as x` yields a repeated name), SHAPE-LINEAR (ops over every field, 4x step, gate 8x), SHAPE-FLAT (ops doing FIXED work against a GROWING record — gate 2.5x, ideal 1x; the tier that was failing at the half-fix and the one a linear-only suite would have let through), CONTROL (string concat, required to exceed both gates), VALGRIND (both real defects here were found by valgrind and neither produced a wrong value — for a refcounting change this is not an extra tier). Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind)
