./tests/run_render.sh      # PLAT-RENDER one renderer turns a value into text. `print` carried its OWN switch, separate from the one `string()` uses, and it understood numbers inside an array and nothing else: `print(["a","b"])` was `[?, ?]`, `print([[1],[2]])` was `[?, ?]`, and `print({a:1})` was the literal word `{record}` -- so a record could not be DISPLAYED AT ALL, which is the single most common thing wanted while debugging. Nothing failed, because two committed goldens (examples/record_helpers_test.out, examples/conversion_builtin_test.out) had captured the broken output AS expected and were defending it. The irony: the comment above value_print_to already argues that `print` and `print to error` must share one renderer so they cannot drift on some value shape -- correct reasoning, simply not applied one level out. `print` now delegates to builtin_string_value. THREE THINGS HAD TO BE TRUE for that to be safe. (1) DISPLAY MUST BE TOTAL: string() reached compounds through the JSON encoder, which legitimately refuses a date or function, so `string([aDate])` RAISED and naive delegation would have let `print` kill the program. The walker now has three modes (RENDER_JSON / RENDER_ENCODE / RENDER_DISPLAY) and display recurses into the single-value renderer, which has a text form for every kind -- no cycle, since that renderer only re-enters the walker for arrays and records. (2) ENCODE MUST BE UNCHANGED: `encode`/`json_encode` still refuse typed and live values, since a lossy token yields text `decode` cannot read back; pinned as negatives (negative_encode_typed, negative_json_strict_live) because a leak would be silent. (3) DURATIONS NEEDED A TEXT FORM: after unification, duration was the one kind a program could hold and not display, still `{duration}` in BOTH renderers; it now renders in the words the literal syntax uses (`2 days 3 hours`), largest component first, singular at 1, `0 seconds` when empty. Tiers: PARITY (the fixture runs TWICE over one value list, via `print v` and via `print string(v)`, and the captures must be BYTE-IDENTICAL -- structural rather than a transcript, so it cannot drift; this is the tier that would have caught the original defect, and it carries a coverage floor so an empty capture cannot pass by comparing nothing to nothing); GOLDEN; SHAPE-BY-SHAPE (each previously broken form asserted as an exact line, so a regression names WHICH shape went back, plus an assertion that the `?` placeholder appears nowhere); TOTALITY (typed values NESTED in arrays and records, every one of which raised before); BOUNDARY (encode/json_encode still refuse); VALGRIND (display allocates per nested typed value -- a new allocation path on every print). Proven red on every tier except BOUNDARY, which correctly passes on both binaries since encode is unchanged by design. Rebaseline: 5 goldens (3 examples + plat_stderr_parity + boxed_struct). NOTE boxed_struct was a PLAT-NUMFMT miss, not this change -- run_native_platform is display-gated and was not in that sweep; a GdkRGBA component is a C float, so 0.9 stored and widened reads back 0.8999999761581421, which %g had been rounding away. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_equality.sh    # PLAT-EQ `=` on two compound values answers a question about the VALUES. eval_comparison had no branch for arrays or records, so both sides fell through to the numeric coercion at the end of the chain where a record becomes 0 and an array becomes 0 -- and 0 = 0. ANY two compounds compared equal: `{x:1} = {y:2}`, `[1,2] = [3,4,5]`, `[] = [1,2,3]`, and `[1,2] = "hi"` (both sides 0); ordering operators silently answered a comparison between two zeros. THE DAMAGE WAS NOT THE OPERATOR -- few programs write `if recA = recB` -- but `contains`, `find` and `remove_value` route through it via values_equal and `consider` dispatches through it, so `contains(people,{name:"zed"})` was true for an ABSENT value, `find` always returned 0, and a `consider` on a record always took its FIRST branch. An array of records is how every xlsx-pipeline frame is represented, so this was live in shipped code; `unique` was the honourable exception, RAISING on non-scalar arrays rather than guessing. The fix routes `=`/`!=` on compound kinds to value_storage_equal -- which already did correct deep comparison and is what WATCHERS use to decide whether a value changed, so the operator and change-detection agree ON COMPOUNDS (a claim CORRECTED 2026-09-06: they must DIFFER on number-versus-boolean, because a variable holding 1 that is assigned `true` HAS changed and a silent watcher would leave the program holding a boolean while reporting nothing -- the watcher answers whether the STORED VALUE changed, which is a different question, and tests/equality_routes_test.bas asserts the one place the two part company plus a control that they agree everywhere else) -- and refuses the ordering operators, following the idiom eval_comparison already used for functions, regexes, gobjects and boxed values. Record comparison is order-insensitive (fields looked up BY NAME), which is right because a record is a mapping. Tiers: SEMANTICS (tests/equality_test.bas, SELF-CHECKING -- every check states its own expected answer and prints ok or a MISMATCH naming both sides. This matters more than usual: a golden records whatever the binary answers AS the expected output, so a plain golden would have enshrined `true` for `{x:1} = {y:2}`); ORACLE (the fixture compares every pair against a deep equality written IN gBASIC -- walking type/count/keys/has and bottoming out on `=` only for scalars, which were never broken -- so it is a separate implementation rather than a second call into the same function, which is the PLAT-NUMFMT lesson. Plus a COVERAGE floor, since a tier that stops running its checks otherwise passes by saying nothing); DISPATCH (tests/equality_dispatch_test.bas -- `consider` on a record and an array, and watcher firing on equal/reordered/changed assignment. The implicit uses, and the ones a caller never thinks of as comparisons; wrong outcomes are named as the word WRONG so a bad branch is visible rather than a missing line); REFUSAL (6 ordering cases with pinned messages, also wired into run_negative.sh as 4 cases); VALGRIND (value_storage_equal recurses through shared refcounted storage, where a consumed borrow is a use-after-free a correct answer would not reveal). Proven red against the pre-change binary on every tier except valgrind -- which is the point about this defect class: it produced no memory error and no crash, only a wrong answer, and NO GOLDEN CAN CATCH IT. Zero regressions and zero golden movement across the whole suite, because nothing depended on the broken behaviour -- which is exactly why it survived. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind) PLAT-EQ'S SCALAR HALF LANDED 2026-09-05 (tests/equality_scalar_test.bas, 26 checks): PLAT-EQ routed COMPOUNDS away from the numeric fallthrough and LEFT THE SCALARS IN IT, so every string was still 0 there -- `0 = "stop"` TRUE, `0 = ""` TRUE, `1 = "1"` false, and `1 > "stop"` ANSWERING. Not a coercion anyone designed: a MISSING CASE in a uniform chain, since every other rich kind has a `both` branch beside a `mixed` branch (array, record, function, watcher, regex, gobject, gboxed, money, datetime, duration, nothing/unknown -- eleven pairs) and STRING had only `both`. The fix is the twelfth, in the shape of the other eleven. INSTRUMENTED FIRST, whole gate: 1,500 mixed-kind comparisons, 1,472 number-versus-boolean (a REAL coercion, kept, and now the CONTROL that catches over-correction -- proven red by refusing it), 28 number-versus-string at two sites, and ONE WAS ALREADY WRONG: run_xlsx.sh counts corrupted cells with `c.value = "#VALUE!"` and a cell holding 0 compared EQUAL, passing only because no cell in that fixture is zero. Found measuring an actor pool for the AI proposal -- a worker whose sentinel was "stop" exited on the message 0 and the parent hung in receive(). Three perturbations red; no golden moved. PLAT-EQ'S THIRD AND LAST INSTALMENT LANDED 2026-09-06 (tests/equality_kinds_test.bas, 19 checks): the two earlier fixes each routed a family AWAY from the numeric fallthrough and NEITHER CLOSED IT, so every kind with no branch of its own was still inside -- `{file}"/etc/hostname" = {file}"/etc/passwd"` TRUE, two directories TRUE, two separately started CHILD PROCESSES TRUE, a file equal to a directory AND to the number 0, and `file > dir` ANSWERING. `file` and `dir` are ORDINARY PROGRAM VALUES, so a program comparing two paths was told every pair was equal. FOUND BY ADDING A THIRTEENTH KIND: PLAT-HTTP's own fixture asserts two transfers are two handles and it FAILED -- which is the argument for closing the door rather than adding a fourteenth branch, since a new value kind opted into the coercion silently and did. MEASURED before changing it, whole gate instrumented: exactly EIGHT comparisons reach the fallthrough with a non-numeric kind (4 http, 3 workbook, 1 actor), all identity comparisons `value_storage_equal` -- the function WATCHERS already use to decide whether a value changed, so the operator and change detection cannot disagree -- answers correctly. Nothing depended on the coercion, which is why it survived. 9 of the 19 checks proven red against the open catch-all and the SIX CONTROLS stay green (`0 = false` at 1,472 measured uses, ordinary number and string comparison), which is what separates this from "refuse everything"; three ordering refusals added, each naming BOTH kinds because which two met is what the author needs to see. AND A SWEEP FOLLOWED, which is the durable part (tests/equality_routes_test.bas, 95 checks): `=` is not the only thing that decides sameness, and each of the three fixes had corrected ONE site. SIX ROUTES ask the question -- `=`, `contains`, `find`, `remove_value`, `consider` and `unique`. The first five reach eval_comparison and agree BY CONSTRUCTION, which is the property under test and not an excuse to skip it, because `unique` HAD ITS OWN SWITCH requiring both kinds to match: `unique([0, false])` returned BOTH elements and `contains` then reported each present -- an array `unique` had just called duplicate-free holding what `contains` calls a duplicate. It delegates now. THE WATCHER IS DELIBERATELY OUTSIDE THAT SET: it answers whether the STORED VALUE changed, which on the one real coercion MUST differ from `=` (a variable holding 1 assigned `true` HAS changed, and a silent watcher would leave the program holding a boolean while reporting nothing), so the claim that the operator and change detection cannot disagree was CORRECTED rather than enforced -- the fixture asserts the one place they part company plus a control (an equal ARRAY assigned over itself is not a change) without which the exception reads as watchers firing on everything. The expected answer is STATED by the fixture, so the tier cannot pass by having every route agree on the wrong one.
./tests/run_numfmt.sh      # PLAT-NUMFMT `print` shows the number the program actually holds. `print`, `string()` and `quote()` share one formatter (format_number in src/eval.c) and it emitted `%g` -- SIX significant digits -- so a program could compute a value it had no way to display: 265550.75 printed as 265551, 23750.25 as 23750.2, the Excel date serial 46237.5674884 as 46237.6. Not cosmetic: every xlsx-pipeline test asserts by COMPARISON because of it, and run_xlsx's NOW/TODAY tier splits a serial into day and seconds-of-day for no other reason. It now emits the SHORTEST decimal that reads back as the same double, so a value renders in full and no further. Tiers: NESTING (a number inside a container goes through a DIFFERENT formatter -- the shared encode/display walker -- than a bare one, and that second path kept `%.17g` for a day after this shipped: `string(0.1)` was `0.1` while `string([0.1])` was `[0.10000000000000001]`, one value with two renderings decided by whether it sat inside a container. The PLAT-RENDER parity tier could NOT catch it, because `print` and `string()` agreed with each other -- both wrong -- which is the standing lesson that agreement is not correctness. Asserted as an INVARIANT (wrapping a value wraps its rendering and changes nothing else) over a 360-value battery rather than as digits, plus a JSON round-trip battery since shortest-round-trip is now used for `encode` too and that is only safe because it is lossless; `pg_parameter_text` and the pg JSON builder keep %.17g deliberately -- wire formats nobody reads); GOLDEN (pins SHORTNESS, which round-trip alone cannot -- %.17g also round-trips and would render 0.1 as 0.10000000000000001); ROUND-TRIP (the DEFINING property, `number(string(x)) = x` over a 1420-value generated battery, checkable in-language and unable to rot the way a list of expected digit strings can); ORACLE (an INDEPENDENT reimplementation of the rule in awk over 4070 generated values -- the tier that catches a minimality search which is self-consistently wrong, since the golden records whatever we print and round-trip accepts any faithful rendering. THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS TIER WAS VACUOUS and passed against the unfixed binary: it parsed our own printed text, and the shortest form of the double `0.333333` reads back as IS `0.333333`, so truncation was invisible. The dump now emits a RECIPE (`div 1 3`) and awk recomputes the value in its own arithmetic, never reading our number -- every op is one correctly-rounded IEEE primitive so both land on the identical double. Proven red: 2590 of 4070 disagree against the pre-change binary. Honest limit: awk shares libc printf/strtod, so it checks the SEARCH and the branch rule, not printf itself); WIDTH (the change made output LONGER into fixed char[32] buffers -- the widest possible rendering is -1.2345678901234567e-308 at 24 chars, asserted BY LENGTH so a buffer that stops fitting fails as a number rather than as a truncated string downstream); VALGRIND. Note the extremes here are built with `number("1e20")` because gBASIC had no exponent LITERAL when this suite was written -- `1e20` lexed as a duration, and did NOT raise, it printed an unlocated line and answered `0 seconds` with exit 0. Scientific notation is a literal since 2026-09-23 (run_exponent_literal.sh) and the `number(...)` calls here are left alone deliberately, so the text route stays exercised. The 2026-08-14 rebaseline moved 17 goldens (13 stats + 4 xlsx/insiders), all in the same direction: digits that were being truncated. Headless, GI-independent, no python3, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_string_nul.sh # PLAT-NUL: a gBASIC string is a COUNTED sequence of bytes and NUL is content -- which docs/reference.md had promised in as many words since the type was written ("any byte -- including NUL -- is valid content; strings are not NUL-terminated from the program's point of view") and which WAS NOT TRUE. `len`, `mid`, `contains`, `find`, `byte_at` and `hex_encode` read the string header's length; `replace`, `trim`, `join`, `split`, `repeat`, `starts_with` and `ends_with` read a C string and stopped at the first NUL. NOTHING SAID WHICH WAS WHICH, so binary-safety was a per-function fact a caller discovered ONE PROBE AT A TIME -- and `contains` and `ends_with`, two functions in the SAME FAMILY, disagreed about what the string was. REPORTED BY THE gdash SESSION (docs/gdash5_platform_report_string_nul.md) from LDAP filter and DN escaping, which is the worst possible place to find it: RFC 4515 and RFC 4514 both REQUIRE NUL to be escaped, so the one byte the code was most obliged to handle was the one the platform ate. THE SWEEP IS THE ARTEFACT, not the fix: running the WHOLE family rather than the five reported found three more (`repeat`, and `starts_with` on BOTH sides) and one CONTRADICTED the report, which had marked `starts_with` safe after testing it with a NUL-free needle only. THE NEEDLE HALF IS THE SHARPER ONE: a needle read as a C string compares only its head, and one that BEGINS with NUL compares NOTHING AT ALL and matches everything -- so `starts_with("xy", chr(0))` was TRUE and `starts_with("axy", "a"+chr(0)+"b")` was TRUE. A predicate used to VALIDATE INPUT answering true for input that does not match is the worst direction available, and no report had it. SELF-CHECKING NOT GOLDEN, and forced: nothing raised, `replace` returned a shorter PLAUSIBLE string and `ends_with` a wrong boolean, so a golden would have recorded 1 as the length of a three-byte string and defended it. Tiers: SEMANTICS (41 checks, and the already-safe builtins are asserted too -- without them a regression making `len` C-string would leave every "want 3" agreeing with a wrong answer and the file would pass while measuring nothing); CROSS-FAMILY (contains/ends_with/find/starts_with/len/mid/trim/replace must all give the SAME answer about the same bytes -- the disagreement WAS the defect, so this is the tier that states it); NEEDLE (a NUL needle is an ORDINARY needle -- `replace` can rewrite one and `split` can separate on one, both previously refused as "empty"); REFUSAL CONTROL (empty means LENGTH ZERO and is STILL refused -- without this tier the whole fix is satisfied by deleting the check); MODIFIER FORMS (`{trimmed}`, `{split ","}`, `{join "-"}` share the helpers, so a fix applied to the call form only would pass everything else); ROUND-TRIP through hex_encode as an ORACLE (it was never broken, so it is a second implementation rather than a second call into the code under test); VALGRIND. Three perturbations proven red: ends_with reverted (caught by semantics AND cross-family), starts_with reverted (caught by the needle tier), and the empty-needle refusal deleted (11 of 15). THE THIRD ONE TAUGHT SOMETHING THE OTHER TWO DID NOT: removing that refusal does not make `replace` answer wrongly, it makes it search for a ZERO-LENGTH needle, find one at every position and never advance -- so the fixture HANGS, and A HANG IS NOT A FAILURE. Without a bound the suite sits there until run_all's 1800s cap reporting nothing, which is a third way this gate can go quiet without going red (the others being a suite that SKIPS, already defended, and a discovery pass that SHRINKS, observed 2026-09-04 and not yet defended). The fixture invocation is therefore `timeout -k 5 60`, with -k because this interpreter installs a SIGTERM handler for pool drain and a bound that might not fire is not a bound. ALSO A LESSON ABOUT THE LEDGER: DOGFOOD item 13 fixed EXACTLY THIS CLASS in `read()` on 2026-08-29 -- one `strlen` where the length was already known -- and no sweep followed, so these seven went on truncating for another five days. A NUL-truncation defect found in one place is evidence about every place that reads a string. AND THAT LESSON CAME BACK FOR THIS SUITE ITSELF 2026-09-10: the sweep stopped at string VALUES and never asked about a record field NAME, which was still a `char *`. A dynamic key is the one place a program puts arbitrary bytes into a name, and THE DAMAGE WAS NOT TRUNCATION BUT COLLAPSE -- `r["a"+chr(0)+"b"]=1` then `r["a"+chr(0)+"z"]=2` left ONE field named "a" holding 2, the second silently overwriting the first, with BOTH subscripts reading back the survivor. A record used as a map keyed by anything binary LOST DATA with nothing raised, and `decode` truncated a LEGAL JSON key containing \u0000 the same way. The length rides IN FRONT OF THE POINTER (the trick this file already uses twice -- the string-value header and the record-field-array header) so `name` stays a `char *` and the ~110 sites reading it as one are untouched; a `size_t name_len` beside it is WORSE, because two construction sites `realloc` their array and set every member by hand, so a new member reads back as GARBAGE rather than zero. THE LOAD-BEARING ASSERTION IS A COUNT: `r[k1]` reading back 1 is satisfied by a record that stored k1 alone, and only "there are TWO fields" catches the collapse. Its CONTROL is that the prefix before the NUL is NOT a key, which is the half a library still carrying the bug fails. Fixed across every route -- storage, the HASH INDEX (whose hash and compare were BOTH length-blind), keys/has/remove_key/merge, the copy-on-write detach, encode/decode, and BOTH ENDS of the actor boundary, where the wire format had carried the length all along so an actor transmitted the right bytes and truncated them on arrival. AND WHERE A NAME REACHES SOMETHING THAT CANNOT HOLD A NUL IT IS REFUSED RATHER THAN TRUNCATED: an HTTP header name and a known option name were validated with strcmp/strlen, so `"host\0anything"` compared EQUAL to `"host"` and was accepted as an option nobody declared, then written to the wire by `snprintf("%s")` as just `host` -- fixing storage alone would have MOVED the truncation rather than removing it. ELEVEN SITES IN src/modules/ WERE MISSED BY THE FIRST SWEEP and found by a CRASH (`free()` on a header-prefixed pointer), which is this suite's own lesson applied to itself: a sweep that stops at src/*.c is not a sweep. Four perturbations proven red, and the length-blind HASH is caught by the 40-key index tier and by NOTHING ELSE. AND THE LESSON CAME BACK A THIRD TIME 2026-09-24, FOR THE DOORS OUT OF THE LANGUAGE: the sweep had covered the builtins and the record field names and had never asked what happens when a string leaves gBASIC for something with rules of its own. Measured across every door at once -- `encode`/`decode`, `serialize`/`deserialize`, `json_encode`, the actor wire, sqlite, pg, odbc, xlsx, xml -- and THE THREE ANSWERS ARE THREE DIFFERENT FACTS ABOUT THE DESTINATION rather than three preferences. SQLITE COULD HOLD THE BYTES AND LOST THEM TWICE IN ONE CALL: `sqlite3_bind_text(..., -1, ...)` means NUL-TERMINATED, so the writer measured the value with strlen, and the reader called `value_string`, which stops at the first NUL -- so the round trip agreed with itself perfectly, which is exactly how this survived, and the tier therefore ASKS THE DATABASE (`length(cast(v as blob))`) rather than our own reader. POSTGRES CANNOT: `text` refuses 0x00 outright (`invalid byte sequence for encoding "UTF8": 0x00`) and libpq IGNORES paramLengths for a text-format parameter, so there is nothing to repair and the answer is a refusal naming `bytea` as the remedy. ODBC's `SQL_C_CHAR` is NUL-terminated BY CONSTRUCTION, a driver-interface fact, so it is DOCUMENTED rather than fixed. XLSX AND XML ARE POSTGRES'S ANSWER ONE FORMAT ALONG, because xlsx is XML in a zip and XML 1.0's `Char` production EXCLUDES #x0: `xlsx.set` -> `save` -> `open` -> `cell` gave back ONE BYTE OF THREE in silence, which is the worst place available for a silent shortening since nothing downstream can tell a truncated cell from a short one, and it refuses at `set` rather than after the file is written. `xml.parse` already refused and BLAMED THE WRONG THING -- libxml2's "Premature end of data in tag r line 1", which reports where it stopped rather than what is wrong -- so what moved there is the SENTENCE, and the tier asserts the message for that reason: a check that only asserted "it refused" would pass on both binaries and measure nothing. `xml.parse_html` is deliberately untouched, HTML's own NUL rules differing and being unmeasured. Each refusal carries its CONTROL, and the xlsx one goes ALL THE WAY THROUGH THE DOOR (set, save, reopen, read) so a save path broken by the same edit cannot hide behind an in-memory check; four more perturbations proven red, including both over-corrections (each refusal made to fire on every string), which the controls and nothing else catch. Headless, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_regex.sh        # TEXT-0 regex in the core (docs/text_design.md §2-§3): a `regex` VALUE KIND plus OVERLOADS of contains/replace/split, not an re_* family — only `match`/`match_all` take new names, because a literal `find` returns one index and a regex match must return {text,start,length,groups}. `find` is deliberately NOT overloaded. Engine is libc POSIX ERE, always on (no HAVE_* guard). Tiers: GOLDEN (tests/regex_test.bas — every overload run in BOTH modes on input where the literal and pattern answers DIFFER, so an overload that swallowed the literal case moves the golden; plus codepoint offsets fed back through `mid`, unknown-on-miss, the unknown-vs-"" non-participating-group distinction, zero-width match termination, interior-NUL subjects); FLAG MATRIX (the only part not a direct libc mapping — POSIX couples "dot matches newline" and "^/$ per line" into the single REG_NEWLINE bit while gBASIC separates them, so 2 of the 4 combinations need `.` rewritten, and REG_NEWLINE additionally stops `[^x]` matching a newline where PCRE's /m does not; 12 assertions that pass individually under a wrong choice but cannot all pass together); NEGATIVE (8 pinned messages incl. \D inside a bracket and a NUL in a pattern — rejected, while a NUL in a SUBJECT works); ACTOR ROUND-TRIP (ships as pattern+flags and recompiles — a regex_t is meaningless in another address space); VALGRIND. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_xlsx.sh         # xlsx Stages 1-2 (docs/xlsx_design.md): ZIP container over zlib (hand-written; libzip/minizip absent on dev + RISC-V), the PART TREE, sparse cells, and write + round-trip. The design's load-bearing claim is that the reader DISCARDS NOTHING -- `libxlsxwriter` was rejected because it generates new workbooks and cannot edit an existing one, so round-trip is only possible if every part survives the read. Tiers: GOLDEN READ (shared strings incl. an XML entity and non-ASCII, a sparse sheet with no row 4 and no column C, a formula cell keeping BOTH <f> and Excel's cached <v> -- that cached value is the recalc engine's future test oracle -- a boolean, an Excel error as its own kind, style indices preserved since only the number format distinguishes a date from 45000, and a second sheet resolved via r:id because part filenames follow creation order not tab order); GOLDEN WRITE (editing ONE sheet changes that part and NOTHING else, incl. the unmodelled vendor part byte-identical; save is byte-DETERMINISTIC across runs because ZIP mod-time fields are fixed rather than clock-derived -- a clock there would look like a successful write while making byte comparison useless; and the output validates under `unzip -t`, i.e. by something other than our own reader); RETENTION (every entry in the ZIP's own central directory must appear in xlsx.parts -- compared against `unzip -Z1`, never a hardcoded list, so growing the fixture cannot leave the check narrower than the file); NEGATIVE (not-a-ZIP, truncated, missing file, overwriting a formula cell REFUSED since the cached value and formula would disagree and Excel would silently revert the edit on recalc, creating a new cell refused rather than placed wrongly); FORMULA (Phase A+B of §13.G -- the expression evaluator plus the durable-core functions, chosen because four of the nine most-used "functions" in the measured Enron corpus are arithmetic OPERATORS, so precedence/refs/ranges carry the largest share of real usage before any function library exists. `xlsx.check` is THE ORACLE: an xlsx stores both the formula and Excel's own cached result for every formula cell, so each formula is checkable IN ISOLATION with no dependency graph -- the graph is only needed once something changes. Asserts zero disagreements, not just a stable golden, since a golden would happily record a regression as the new expected output. VOLATILE functions (NOW/TODAY/RAND) are skipped rather than judged because their cached value dates from whenever the workbook last calculated; UNSUPPORTED functions are reported BY NAME rather than defaulted to a plausible zero, asserted separately. CAVEAT recorded in the fixture generator: this fixture's cached values are hand-written, so check measures self-consistency here and becomes a true oracle only against a workbook Excel actually wrote); RECALC (dependency-ordered recalculation. The ordering tier is the one that matters: on the fixture's Ledger sheet D7 = B5*2 sits ABOVE B5 = SUM(B2:B3) in sheet order, so an engine evaluating top-to-bottom hands D7 a stale B5 and prints a plausible wrong number -- changing B2 to 1000, the only correct transitive answer is 1801.5 and a stale read gives 2302.5, so the test names both. Also: recalculated values PERSIST through save while the formulas survive, only the edited sheet's part changes, and a circular reference is REPORTED rather than iterated toward a fixed point -- with healthy cells on the same sheet still evaluating, since one cycle must not sink a sheet); INDEPENDENT READER (§13.U -- the one claim nothing but ourselves had ever checked: everything else asserting our output was our own reader or `unzip -t`, and unzip only proves a valid ZIP. Demonstrated not argued: a ZIP holding one text file PASSES unzip -t and is not a spreadsheet. The tier writes a cell, has LIBREOFFICE open the result and export it, and requires every awkward shape to survive a foreign reader -- written value, XML entity, non-ASCII, sparse blank row, styled date (serial 45000 -> 03/15/2023), boolean, Excel error, formula cached value. LibreOffice is not Excel so this is strong evidence not proof, and does NOT retire whether Excel itself opens our files; it retires the weaker possibility that only we could read our own output. SKIPs when libreoffice is absent); MACRO SHEET (a sheet declared with an empty `r:id` -- a VBA module/macro sheet, genuinely in the workbook with no worksheet part behind it. `xlsx.sheets` listed it and `xlsx.cells` then rejected it as "no such sheet", which is self-contradictory AND false, and it broke the one loop every caller writes. Found by scanning the whole 15,871-workbook Enron corpus one process per file: 400 workbooks (2.5%) carry one and all 400 scan failures were this single cause -- zero ZIP, XML or cell-parsing failures in the entire corpus. Reads now answer as the empty sheet it is; a genuinely absent name still raises, and a WRITE is still refused but with the real reason rather than the false one -- both pinned as negatives, since blanket leniency would let a typo'd sheet name silently return nothing. Separate fixture, so the byte-exact read/write/retention goldens are not rebaselined for an unrelated concern; it includes the trailing space in `"VBACode "` that seven corpus files carry, which a name-trimming reader would pass on `Module1` and still fail. Corpus measurements, incl. the build order by fully-recalculable workbooks, in docs/xlsx_design.md §13.I); SHARED FORMULAS (a formula filled down a column is stored ONCE -- the anchor carries the text, every other cell in the run carries an empty `<f t="shared" si="n"/>`. Read naively those cells have "a formula whose text is empty" -> `#VALUE!`, and since recalc writes values back it CORRUPTED them, measured at 171 cells on the first corpus file tried. 61.0% of formula-bearing workbooks use them and 13.2M of the corpus's 20.7M formula cells are continuations, so two thirds of every formula cell was being read as empty -- which also means the §13.I function ranking is measured on a biased sample, since the scanner never saw them. Resolution TRANSLATES the anchor by the cell offset; the fixture is one column per rule -- relative shifts, absolute `$` does not, a ref inside a string literal is text, both range endpoints move -- because a wrong offset yields a plausible number from the wrong cells rather than an error); NOW/TODAY (the Excel date serial, epoch 1899-12-30 -- Lotus treated 1900 as a leap year and Excel kept the bug. `GBASIC_XLSX_NOW` pins the clock for this tier only; expected serials cross-checked against LibreOffice, and the day and seconds-of-day are asserted SEPARATELY because `print` rendered only ~6 significant digits when this tier was written, showing a full serial as 46237.6 (DOGFOOD 2026-08-09); PLAT-NUMFMT fixed that on 2026-08-14, and the split is kept because day and seconds-of-day are the two things that can independently go wrong. Also asserts local-time handling across two zones and that the UNPINNED clock agrees with date(1), so the tier cannot pass with only the test seam working); POST-2001 (examples/xlsx_modern_test.bas over examples/fixtures/xlsx/modern.xlsx, whose cached values LIBREOFFICE computed via tools/make_xlsx_modern_fixture.sh -- so `xlsx.check` there compares against an INDEPENDENT implementation instead of restating our own output, which is the weakness every hand-written fixture has. Evidence ranks Excel-authored corpus > LibreOffice-authored > hand-written; the arithmetic is also small enough to check by eye. The structural finding is bigger than any function: anything newer than the original ECMA-376 list is stored PREFIXED -- `_xlfn.XLOOKUP`, `_xlfn._xlws.SORT`, `_xlpm.x` for a LET parameter -- so until those are stripped every modern function is an unknown name however well implemented, while `_xll.` (add-ins) and `_xludf.` (VBA) must NOT be stripped since they are unevaluable in principle. basic.xlsx's unsupported-function case is now `_xll.HPVAL` because it used to be XLOOKUP and that test broke the moment XLOOKUP was implemented -- a fixture pinned to a gap that closed. 62 formulas, zero disagreements. Refused rather than approximated: YEARFRAC basis 1, XLOOKUP non-exact modes, AGGREGATE 14-19. Deferred: dynamic arrays, which SPILL and so are structural, and LET/LAMBDA, which need a binding environment); CROSS-SHEET (examples/xlsx_crosssheet_test.bas over crosssheet.xlsx, values again computed by LibreOffice via tools/make_xlsx_crosssheet_fixture.sh -- the sheet XML is hand-written so the shapes are guaranteed present but carries NO cached values, so LibreOffice must compute them to convert the file. Closes §13.J's largest cause: 3.09M cells, 54% of all disagreements. 42% of the corpus population is the QUOTED form ('Nymex hist.'!A:B, quoting being forced by a space in the name), 30% plain, 28% EXTERNAL ([4]Book!A1) which names a different workbook and is REPORTED UNAVAILABLE rather than read from Excel's stale cached copy -- which is why `unsupported` rises as disagreements collapse. Brought VLOOKUP/HLOOKUP/INDEX/MATCH and the IS* predicates with it, since VLOOKUP is the dominant consumer; note VLOOKUP's 4th arg and MATCH's 3rd DEFAULT TO APPROXIMATE, and guessing either way is silent. Whole-column ranges (A:B) clamp to the sheet's real extent. Three defects found building it, all recorded in §13.L: a column off-by-one that COUNTA(A:A) CONCEALED by reading the neighbouring column which happened to hold the same count; a realloc-dangling XlsxSnap* that segfaulted on a real corpus workbook; and per-lookup range materialisation that cost 116s on one file, fixed by giving the four position-addressing functions an unmaterialised descriptor -> 12s. Corpus agreement 66.1% -> 94.9%); WORKBOOK RECALC (`xlsx.recalc(wb)` with no sheet argument orders ACROSS sheets; the per-sheet form became a trap once cross-sheet refs worked, since a formula can depend on a FORMULA elsewhere and recalculating only its own sheet reads the other's stale cached value -- nothing errors, the number is just wrong. Fixture chain.xlsx declares its sheets Out, Mid, Inputs -- the REVERSE of dependency order -- so sheet-order evaluation hands Out a stale Mid: the test names both 201 (correct) and 21 (stale), like the single-sheet D7/B5 case. Cross-sheet cycles reported not iterated, with a healthy neighbour still evaluating. Walk is an EXPLICIT STACK not recursion (chain depth reaches tens of thousands); dependency edges stay RECTANGLES rather than expanding a whole-column ref to a million cells. Valgrind caught two pre-existing leaks here -- xlsx_call/xlsx_primary pre-filled their result with an allocated error every branch overwrote -- and note the naive fix (init to empty) is WORSE than the leak, since empty becomes 0 and would turn an unsupported function into a plausible zero; an unassigned SENTINEL resolved to #NAME? at the end is leak-free and keeps the refusal, proven behaviour-neutral by a byte-identical corpus re-run); TEXT+MATH (examples/xlsx_textmath_test.bas over textmath.xlsx, values again LibreOffice's. Built 2026-08-15 after a METHODOLOGY correction: `xlsx.check` counted `unsupported` cells without recording WHICH name it refused, so the roadmap was ranked by counting `NAME(` tokens -- the method §13.J had ALREADY shown to be structurally blind -- and that blind method kept steering for months under its own documented warning because it was the only one available. Notes now carry `blocked_by`; ranked by the name actually refused, the top was FIND at 240,587 blocked cells and LEFT at 207,757, against ~14,000 for the VLOOKUP/SUMIF compiler work §14 said came next. Implemented the TEXT family, the MATH family and the clock parts. The cases are chosen where a plausible implementation is wrong in a way POSITIVES DO NOT REVEAL: INT floors while TRUNC truncates (differing only on negatives), MOD takes the DIVISOR's sign unlike C fmod, Excel's TRIM COLLAPSES interior runs rather than only stripping, FIND is case-sensitive where SEARCH is not, a FIND miss is #VALUE! not 0, MID with start<1 is an ERROR not a clamp, CEILING/FLOOR round to a MULTIPLE not an integer. `_xlfn.CEILING.MATH` is NOT an alias for CEILING -- significance becomes optional and a `mode` argument decides how negatives round -- and LibreOffice emits that spelling then CANNOT EVALUATE ITS OWN OUTPUT (caches #VALUE!), so those cases are excluded rather than pinned to an artifact and the negative-mode behaviour follows Microsoft's docs with NO independent validation, which the generator states outright); SHAPE (a 40k-row sheet must evaluate under a 1200ms CEILING, not a ratio -- measured: with the pre-fix linear cell lookup the 4x-size step reports 7.7-8.0x, which an 8x ratio gate would have PASSED, while the ceiling separates 312ms indexed from ~2500ms scanned. Fixture generated in-test with awk+zip, so no python3 and nothing large committed); VALGRIND. Skips cleanly when built without zlib or libxml2. No python3 in the test path -- the fixtures are committed and tools/make_xlsx_fixture.py + tools/make_xlsx_macro_fixture.py are only how they were authored
./tests/run_xlsx_cookbook.sh # docs/xlsx_cookbook.md -- the xlsx tutorial, made unable to lie. A cookbook's failure mode is not being wrong when written but being wrong six months later while still reading as authoritative, so the page OWNS neither the code nor the output it shows: `examples/xlsx_cookbook/NN_name.bas` owns the code, its `.out` owns the output, `tools/sync_xlsx_cookbook.sh` copies both into the page at the `<!--CODE:name-->` / `<!--OUT:name-->` markers (idempotent), and this suite fails while any of them disagree. Four tiers: RUN (every recipe executes, stdout byte-identical to its committed .out -- so the page's output is a golden, not a pasted transcript), CODE (every ```basic block equals its .bas), OUTPUT (every output block equals its .out, so a behaviour change that legitimately moves a golden ALSO moves the page and the two cannot drift apart), COVER (every recipe is documented and every marker has a file, so adding recipe 13 and forgetting to write it up fails rather than passing silently). All four proven red before shipping, CODE and OUTPUT in ISOLATION -- a comment-only edit to a .bas leaves the output identical and must still fail, which is the case a run-only suite would wave through. 12 recipes over the whole surface: the 15 xlsx.* calls, then grid/consolidate/dbframe. Skips cleanly with no zlib/libxml2; recipes 11-12 skip independently without sqlite. ALL EIGHT COOKBOOK RUNNERS NOW SHARE tests/cookbook_harness.sh (2026-09-01): five of them were BYTE-IDENTICAL once the cookbook's name was substituted out and the other three differed only in WHEN they skip, so the runner is now a name, a literal glob and at most three hooks (cookbook_precheck, cookbook_recipe_skip, COOKBOOK_SKIP_PATTERN). 1240 lines to 492. The argument is not the line count -- it is that a fix to the harness used to need applying eight times, and the two runners that had ALREADY drifted (a different SKIP wording, an extra stderr pattern) are the evidence it would not have been. The literal glob stays in each thin runner because run_docs_gate.sh greps tests/*.sh for exactly that string to decide a directory is wired; a variable would read as unwired. All four tiers re-proven red on the shared harness, CODE in ISOLATION (comment-only .bas edit, stdout confirmed byte-identical first), and all eight suites report the SAME PASS/SKIP counts as before the change
./tests/run_nlq.sh        # `nlq` FIRST INCREMENT -- retrieval and grounding, NO MODEL AND NO SQL (docs/nlq_design.md). WHY THIS PART FIRST: discovery_design §2 already says schema retrieval must precede prompting because 500 tables do not fit in a context window, which makes retrieval a SEARCH -- and Recipe 1's finding applies unchanged, A SEARCH ALWAYS RETURNS A WINNER. Hand a model twenty tables chosen badly and it writes flawless SQL about the wrong ones AND THE SQL RUNS, because the tables it was given are real; the number that comes back has the right units and nobody downstream can tell. THE ORACLE IS NOT OURS AND NEEDS NO MODEL: estateforge's questions_for(demo_plan()) records the `touches[]` every question needs, computed from the plan rather than by running any SQL, and the catalog + questions are COMMITTED as tests/nlq/estate_demo.json so the gate is hermetic -- a gate you can turn off by not having a sister repo checked out is a gate that shrinks. MEASURED: 13/16 lexically, 14/16 once the estate's owner DECLARES that rpt means report. BOTH HALVES ASSERTED, since recall alone is maximised by selecting every table (which is the failure retrieval exists to prevent) and precision alone by selecting none -- so there is a recall floor AND a ceiling on table-slots selected. THE SYNONYM TIER IS A DIFFERENCE, not a level: declaring one must IMPROVE recall, or the option is decoration and the `unresolved` field it exists to close is pointless. A FIXTURE TRIPWIRE EXISTS BECAUSE OF A DEFECT I ALMOST REPORTED AS THE LIBRARY'S: the first catalog emitted sp.tables ONLY, so every `touches` naming a VIEW (warehouse.rpt_volume_gross) was unrecallable and lineage_aware scored 0/4 -- a defect in the ANSWER KEY'S INPUT that read exactly like a weakness in the tool. With views included it is 3/4, and the tripwire greps the fixture for them. THE REMAINING MISS IS ASSERTED AS HONEST rather than demanded: `w_filtered_out` asks which staged rows never reached the fact table and the answer is trading.ctp, reachable only through the ETL's own `where k.is_active = 1` -- lineage, not vocabulary, and no lexical method can find it. A tier demanding 4/4 would be demanding the next increment. THE SEMANTICS FIXTURE ASSERTS A TIE rather than an order: archive.deal_2015 scores IDENTICALLY to trading.deal (same table word, same matching column) so which comes first is decided by the tie-break and not the question, and NOTHING IN THE CATALOG SAYS WHICH IS LIVE (design R6) -- asserting "trading.deal came first" would pass on an alphabetical accident and hide that. Ranking is TOTAL (score then id) and asserted over a REVERSED catalog, so a driver's row order cannot decide an answer. A SECOND ESTATE in a different domain (estateforge's core bank -- accounts, ledgers, ACH, delinquency) that nothing here was written for and for which NO SYNONYMS ARE DECLARED: 6/6. BUT THE TIER SAYS WHAT THAT DOES NOT PROVE -- the bank is 14 objects against a limit of 8, so a selection is more than half the estate and even a poor ranking recalls a lot; the load-bearing number there is the RATIO (32 table-slots for 7 needed), which is what stops a small estate from flattering the result. Its availability came from a bug this work FILED AND DID NOT PATCH: questions_for(bank_plan()) raised, because `_trading` was the only one of five subject generators without its `_has` guard, and estateforge fixed it. PLUS THE VALUE TIER, the only thing in this suite that produces a SCORE: the recorded SQL RUN against a real estate (estateforge demo_plan() built into its own PostgreSQL database) and compared to the answer estateforge FOLDS OVER THE GENERATED ROWS -- a second implementation, not a second call into the thing under test. 10 scored, 7 agreed, 2 failed to execute. THE DISAGREEMENTS ARE ASSERTED TO EXIST, because a run where everything agreed would mean the tier had stopped discriminating: t_total_volume is R2 WITH A NUMBER (the model summed warehouse.fact_volume at 4,025,053 where the question is about trading.deal at 6,285,487 -- the fact table is built by an ETL that keeps only active counterparties, so it is a subset, and the sum of a subset is a perfectly good number 36% short of the one asked for). THE TWO THAT FAILED TO EXECUTE ARE THE BETTER OUTCOME: the invented literals hit `character varying = integer` and returned NOTHING, and a type mismatch is a LOUD failure, which is what this design exists to convert silent ones into. AND A CLAIM THIS WORK WITHDREW: t_active_deals grounding to warehouse.stg_deal instead of trading.deal was written up as R2 on the grounds that the staging copy is filtered so the counts need not agree -- MEASURED, THEY AGREE EXACTLY (315 either way). Reading an ETL is not running it. THE SKIP NAMES A CONSEQUENCE AND NOT A TIER, and says NOTHING WAS SCORED rather than that a tier was absent, because this is the only tier that turns observations into numbers and a skip that only says `skipped` is indistinguishable from a pass in scroll-back; it also shrinks the gate as little as possible, since everything else needs no database and still runs. Set NLQ_ODBC_CONNECTION to run it. Headless bar the value tier; no network (the model's answers are REPLAYED from committed fixtures), never skips otherwise (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_finio.sh      # `finio` PHASE 0 -- the financial adapter framework's VALUE MODEL, and the ONE MEASUREMENT that chose its architecture (docs/financial_adapters_design.md §21). NO ADAPTER EXISTS YET AND THAT IS THE PHASE: what Phase 0 fixes is the shape everything above it is built on, and the design says that shape is decided by a MEASUREMENT rather than by preference -- Axiom 2 makes every interpreted value traceable, a 100,000-record file at eleven fields is 1.1 MILLION source locations, and whether gBASIC carries that is NOT KNOWABLE BY READING. MEASURED over a NACHA-shaped fixed-width file (the shape whose provenance is most expensive, so the numbers are an UPPER BOUND): per-value 2.79 GB and 7.3-7.6s, per-record 482 MB and 3.8-4.6s, retained-source 54 MB and 0.4-0.5s -- **294x, 51x and 5.7x the 9.5 MB file**. THE EXPERIMENT WAS BUILT TO FIND A TRADE AND FOUND NONE: I expected on-demand to buy its memory back with a query penalty, and measured it is ~2 MICROSECONDS A QUERY against 428 MB and 3.8s of build, so a consumer needs ~2 million provenance queries before per-record's build alone pays back. Per-value is not merely expensive, it is also the SLOWEST TO ANSWER (four levels of indirection through a working set that defeats the cache). THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS THE ORACLE, not a timing: the three architectures must answer an identical query workload with an IDENTICAL CHECKSUM, or a mode could look cheap by answering a DIFFERENT QUESTION -- and timings are a property of the machine, so they are recorded in §21 and measured on demand (RUN_FINIO_COST=1) rather than asserted. THE LOCATION TIER CHECKS AGAINST THE FILE with head/tail rather than against ourselves, because the fixture's own version of it compares finio to finio and is satisfied by a reader that is self-consistently one byte out -- which the first draft WAS, since gBASIC's `mid` is 0-BASED and the library passed `offset + 1` everywhere, yielding an ordinary-looking raw field from the neighbouring column. SELF-CHECKING NOT GOLDEN, forced: every defect here is a PLAUSIBLE RECORD. Axiom 7's three answers are asserted as DIFFERENCES (a blank field is `unknown`, a present field failing its rule is `invalid`, and an unknown's value is never zero) since asserting one passes on a reader that answers it for everything. The §9 registry refuses an entry claiming `implemented` with no specification_sources and one without implementation_allowed (Axiom 12), WITH THE CONTROL that a fully evidenced entry is ACCEPTED -- without it the five states would be decoration. NOTE the fixture's own hand-built records did not match its layout in the first draft and the MISMATCHes were the FIXTURE's fault, not the library's, which is the ordinary way a hand-built fixture lies. PLUS THE AMOUNT-KIND TRIPWIRE (2026-09-18, tests/finio/amount_kind_test.bas): WHAT KIND OF THING IS AN AMOUNT, declared as a TABLE every adapter must appear in. THREE ADAPTERS ANSWERED `money` AND THE OFX ONE ANSWERED TEXT for the whole life of that adapter, and the reason it survived is the reason this tier sits ABOVE the adapters rather than in any one suite: EVERY assertion about an amount in this tree goes through `string(...)`, and `string(money)` and the decimal text it was parsed from are THE SAME CHARACTERS -- only ARITHMETIC tells them apart, and every fixture was written by the hands that wrote the reader. FOUND BY POINTING finio_ofx AT A REAL CREDIT UNION'S DOWNLOAD, the first file from that institution it had ever seen, and adding three amounts up: `"-28.00-5.00-28.00"`, type string, NO RAISE. The check is therefore the ARITHMETIC (`x + x` must not equal `string(x) + string(x)`) and not `type()`, which passes on a value that reports the right kind and cannot be used. TWO EXCEPTIONS ARE RECORDED WITH THEIR ARGUMENTS, because an exception nobody wrote down is indistinguishable from the defect: BAI2 amounts are MINOR UNITS with no currency on most record types (so there is nothing to denominate a money with; `cents` travels beside it) and pain.001's `CtrlSum` carries NO CURRENCY OF ITS OWN (a block mixing EUR and USD has a control sum money cannot represent). THE FIRST DRAFT OF THE TRIPWIRE REPORTED pain.001 AS BROKEN -- it scanned for the first field whose name contained "amount" or "sum" and found `control_sum`; the contract NAMES its record kind and field now, because a tripwire whose false positives look exactly like its true ones is not a tripwire. The gate cannot shrink: an adapter absent from the table FAILS rather than skips (proven red both ways). Headless, no network, never skips (bar valgrind and the opt-in cost tier)
./tests/run_finance_cookbook.sh # docs/finance_cookbook.md -- the finance tutorial on the shared cannot-lie harness (tools/sync_finance_cookbook.sh wraps the parameterised sync). RUN/CODE/OUTPUT/COVER over examples/finance_cookbook/*.bas; never skips, since finance is pure gBASIC over core money. 9 recipes covering the whole Phase 1 surface: the five solvers in EXCEL ARGUMENT ORDER, the one equation they all solve, nominal/effective/periodic/continuous rate conversion, amortization, NPV/IRR, dated flows (xnpv/xirr), day counts, multiple-IRR detection, and the optional fv/timing tail. EVERY FIGURE ON THE PAGE WAS COMPUTED INDEPENDENTLY IN PYTHON BEFORE BEING COMMITTED -- a cookbook golden records whatever the library said, so agreement with an outside calculation is the only thing that makes it evidence rather than a transcript. Recipe 8 is the one that earns its place: it prints the SAME flows discounted at 100% AND 200% and gets 0.00 both times, so "more than one rate can satisfy these" is DEMONSTRATED on the page rather than asserted -- and the multiple-IRR warning is shown being caught with `on warning goto next`, which is also the only way a fixture can assert on a warning at all. CODE and OUTPUT proven red IN ISOLATION: a comment-only .bas edit leaves stdout byte-identical and must still fail CODE (confirmed identical first, then confirmed failing), and a perturbed page output block with .bas and .out untouched must fail OUTPUT; COVER proven against an undocumented tenth recipe
./tests/run_money_cookbook.sh # docs/money_cookbook.md -- the money/finance tutorial on the same cannot-lie harness as the xlsx cookbook (tools/sync_money_cookbook.sh wraps the shared parameterised sync). RUN/CODE/OUTPUT/COVER tiers over examples/money_cookbook/*.bas; never skips, since money is core and finance is pure gBASIC. 9 recipes: exact construction, 178 currencies with their own minor units, guard-digit arithmetic, allocation, dated FX, loan payments, amortization, appraisal, depreciation. THIS PAGE EARNED ITS HARNESS WITHIN AN HOUR: writing recipe 7, an ORDINARY amortization schedule rather than a stress case, surfaced a SILENT WRONG ANSWER in `money * scalar` -- a double's shortest decimal can need more fractional places than a power of ten fits in int64 (0.005 * 1.005^12 is 0.0053083890593224915, NINETEEN places), and the code treated any such scalar as negligible and returned 0.00. It is not negligible: in exactly those cases the mantissa is large too. Every payment in a 12-month schedule came out zero while a 360-month one worked, because its scalar happened to land on eighteen places. The unit fixtures had all used SHORT scalars (2, 3, 1.08, 0.5), so only realistic arithmetic produced one long enough -- which is the argument for worked recipes in one example: a page of them exercises shapes a unit suite does not think to. Every figure on the page is independently verified (NPV/IRR/DDB recomputed in Python, pmt checked against Excel). CODE and OUTPUT proven red IN ISOLATION
./tests/run_odbc_cookbook.sh # docs/odbc_cookbook.md -- the ODBC tutorial on the same cannot-lie harness as the xlsx cookbook (tools/sync_odbc_cookbook.sh wraps the shared parameterised sync). RUN/CODE/OUTPUT/COVER tiers over examples/odbc_cookbook/*.bas. 8 recipes: connect/query, bound parameters (the recipe RUNS the same term pasted AND bound so the difference is a fact rather than an admonition), exec + rows_affected, the exact-number rule, NULL vs "" and date columns, transactions, telling an absent driver from a refused login, results straight into a frame. SKIPS TWO WAYS, deliberately distinguished: ODBC compiled out of the build, and ODBC present but no SQLite3 driver installed -- the second detected by the driver manager's own refusal text, since a bare "did it run" check would report a fact about the machine as a failure of the cookbook. CODE and OUTPUT proven red IN ISOLATION (a comment-only .bas edit leaves the output identical and must still fail; a perturbed page output block with .bas and .out untouched), plus COVER against an undocumented recipe
./tests/run_chart_cookbook.sh # docs/chart_cookbook.md -- the chart tutorial on the same cannot-lie harness (tools/sync_chart_cookbook.sh wraps the shared parameterised sync). RUN/CODE/OUTPUT/COVER tiers over examples/chart_cookbook/*.bas; never skips (pure-gBASIC chart library only). 10 recipes: first chart, frames/series/gaps, date axes, money at face value, grouped+stacked bars, histograms, pie, heatmap/correlations, sparklines, the spec layer + chart.page. Recipe outputs are FACTS (lengths, contains checks) rather than raw SVG, so the page stays readable while the goldens stay byte-exact; recipe 09 prints its whole sparkline because it fits. CODE and OUTPUT tiers proven red in isolation (a comment-only .bas edit; a perturbed page block)
./tests/run_datetime_cookbook.sh # docs/datetime_cookbook.md -- the datetime/duration tutorial on the same cannot-lie harness as the xlsx cookbook (SHARED sync tool: tools/sync_xlsx_cookbook.sh is parameterised and tools/sync_datetime_cookbook.sh calls it). RUN/CODE/OUTPUT/COVER tiers over examples/datetime_cookbook/*.bas; never skips (core + pure-gBASIC dates/schedule only). 10 recipes: precision, dot fields, duration algebra, deadlines/ages, business calendars, select/matches, series, dates.merge, schedule.layout, schedule.slots
./tests/run_lending_cookbook.sh # docs/lending_cookbook.md -- the lending tutorial on the shared cannot-lie harness (tools/sync_lending_cookbook.sh wraps the parameterised sync). RUN/CODE/OUTPUT/COVER over examples/lending_cookbook/*.bas; never skips, since `lending` and `accounting` are both pure gBASIC over core money. 7 recipes: declaring a loan's conventions, servicing as a fold with history, the two accrual bases, the two waterfalls, payoff and per-diem, underwriting, and posting a loan's whole life to a ledger. THE SHAPE IS THE ARGUMENT: `lending`'s subject is CONVENTIONS, and every one of them fails by producing an ordinary-looking BALANCE rather than an error, so the two convention recipes are written as DIFFERENCES -- the same loan and the same payments under both settings, with the answers parting company on the page. That is the only shape that distinguishes a library honouring a declaration from one ignoring it, and it is the shape that caught the library's own first defect (`_accrue` prorated the amortized basis by days, making the two bases algebraically identical and the declaration decorative). Recipe 4 asserts the waterfall TOTALS are EQUAL and only the components move, since a totals-only test passes on a library with no waterfall at all; recipe 7 proves the arithmetic rather than asserting it, by posting to a real ledger where an unbalanced entry or a phantom account is refused where it lands. CODE/OUTPUT/COVER proven red in isolation (comment-only .bas edit with stdout confirmed byte-identical first; perturbed page block with files untouched; an undocumented recipe)
./tests/run_deposits_cookbook.sh # docs/deposits_cookbook.md -- the deposits tutorial on the shared cannot-lie harness (tools/sync_deposits_cookbook.sh wraps the parameterised sync). RUN/CODE/OUTPUT/COVER over examples/deposits_cookbook/*.bas; never skips. 4 recipes: compounding versus crediting, the three balance methods, tiered rates, certificates. THE RECIPE WORTH NAMING IS 2, because it asserts the OPPOSITE of what a test suite usually does: `daily` and `minimum` give 28.77 and 4.11 on identical activity, and then `daily` and `average_daily` give THE SAME NUMBER, asserted as EQUAL and explained -- simple interest is LINEAR in the balance, so the mean balance earns exactly what each day's balance earns, and the two part company only under tiers. Claiming a difference there would claim something false, which matters because the neighbouring lending cookbook exists to catch exactly the opposite mistake. Recipe 4's maturity case is the CONTROL: without it a library that always penalised would satisfy every early-redemption check, and the early case has to show proceeds BELOW principal, since clamping the penalty at zero reports money the holder will not receive. CODE/OUTPUT/COVER proven red in isolation
./tests/run_credit_cookbook.sh # docs/credit_cookbook.md -- the credit-analytics tutorial on the shared cannot-lie harness (tools/sync_credit_cookbook.sh wraps the parameterised sync). RUN/CODE/OUTPUT/COVER over examples/credit_cookbook/*.bas; never skips. 6 recipes: the status table and the `observe` bridge, MBA versus OTS, migration counts and reconciliation, roll rates, vintage triangles, charge-offs and recoveries. EVERY DEFECT THIS LIBRARY PREVENTS IS AN ORDINARY-LOOKING PERCENTAGE, so the recipes put the WRONG answer beside the right one rather than merely leaving it out: recipe 4 prints BOTH denominators on the same data -- 100% "stayed current" counting only the loans still visible, 75% counting the whole starting bucket -- because the first is not bad arithmetic, it is the answer to a different question and nothing on a report distinguishes them; recipe 5's vintage table prints DASHES at ages a young cohort has not lived through, since a 0% there reads as "no losses" where the truth is "no data". Recipe 3 builds the attrition case BY HAND (deleting rows after a date) and says so, because `observe` never drops a loan -- which is also the honest way to say that a real servicer extract does. Recipe 6 likewise applies the charge-off decisions on top of the observed table, since a charge-off is a DECISION read from the record and never inferred from days past due. CODE/OUTPUT/COVER proven red in isolation
./tests/run_ari.sh         # ARI Phase 2 -- stdlib/ari.bas, the anchor-relative report parser (docs/text_design.md §4-§5, §13.H; syntax in docs/ari_spec_language.md). Tiers: GOLDEN over BOTH committed fixtures -- the hand-made one carries the irregularities that make report parsing hard (a recurring page header, summary fields in a DIFFERENT ORDER per teller, a heading four columns adrift of its data, a negative one column wider than the positives, `Teller #:` vs `Teller#:`, a malformed amount), the generated one carries form feeds, three branches, and a DIFFERENT MONEY DIALECT PER BRANCH that the spec never names; a second golden reaches what the first cannot (down/up line offsets, distance RANGES -- the gap after REMARKS: is 1, 2, 2 and 3 lines across four branches so no exact distance matches all of them -- flush, custom `type`, and the date contract: run undeclared the ambiguous minority must come back `unknown` WITH diagnostics rather than guessed, and run with `using date:` every date must settle AND the ones already settled must not move). GENERATOR DRIFT regenerates the committed sample and requires it byte-identical, because a golden testing a file nothing can reproduce is a golden testing itself. FURNITURE INDEPENDENCE parses the same report paginated three ways and requires the SAME answer -- the §13.H claim stated as a test, and the only tier that can catch furniture removal being entangled with section location, since any single pagination looks fine alone. VALGRIND, over the trace fixture as well as the golden, since the claims machinery is the only part of this library that allocates per FIELD PER ROW and the golden never calls it. LIMITATIONS, the register held to the truth by RUNNING it (docs/ari_limitations.md; every entry a negative control that goes red when the limitation is fixed, struck entries carrying the opposite check, plus a coverage tripwire both directions). TRACE -- `ari.trace`, which source spans the specification CLAIMED and what stayed UNCLAIMED, limitation C1 struck 2026-09-18 and with it the two §8 scoring measures `ari_discover` had reported `unknown` since Phase 1. THE GAP WAS NEVER THAT THE POSITIONS WERE HARD TO COMPUTE: `_locate_in_line` knew exactly where it cut and returned only the text, `_money_in` computed `beg`/`fin` for its own overlap rule and returned `best.val`, `_whole_match` held the match object and returned a number -- threading them out changed NO BEHAVIOUR ANYWHERE, which is what says the offsets were facts the library already had rather than a new inference about the report. `parse` and `trace` SHARE ONE WALK (tracing is a flag on the existing recursion, never a second walker, because a trace describing a different program is worse than no trace; asserted as identical value AND identical diagnostics). TWO DECISIONS THE MEASUREMENT FORCED AND READING COULD NOT: an ANCHOR IS A CLAIM -- a literal the spec names is the most spec-relevant text on the page, and left out it landed in the unclaimed list and depressed coverage by exactly the literals the author wrote; and a COLLISION NEEDS A VALUE ON AT LEAST ONE SIDE -- counted without that condition a section declared `starts(/^Branch: /)` beside a field read `right of "Branch:"`, THE COMMONEST CORRECT SHAPE IN THE LANGUAGE, reported three collisions on a specification with nothing wrong with it, and a number that fires on correct code is noise with a name. AND THE FIXTURE'S OWN FIRST SOURCE ORACLE WAS THE LIBRARY AGREEING WITH ITSELF: it compared `claim.text` against `mid(raw_line, start, length)`, and `text` is cut from the same row the offset indexes, so an offset one column out passes. That tier now establishes only the LINE NUMBER (that the furniture pass's physical line still addresses the same bytes) and the OFFSET is established twice independently -- against the SPECIFICATION, since an anchor claim must reproduce a literal written in its own rule, and against the VALUE, since re-reading the claimed span on its own must give what the whole parse gave. The perturbation that moves a locator one column is caught by the second and by NEITHER of the others. Blanks are excluded from both sides of the coverage fraction (a print image is largely column padding, and counting it puts every spec near 1.0), asserted EXACTLY rather than as a ratio: content + blank = the grid's own size. Coverage is asserted as a DIFFERENCE -- a one-field spec against a twenty-field one over the same report -- because a number that reads the same for both is measuring the report and not the specification. Seven perturbations proven red, each caught by the tier written for it. NOTE money is asserted as integer CENTS: `print` renders ~6 significant digits and would hide a lost cent above $9,999.99 (DOGFOOD 2026-08-01). Headless, no network, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_grid.sh        # L2 ARI-for-grids (stdlib/grid.bas over docs/xlsx_design.md §5 and §13.N): a MESSY worksheet turned into clean frames. Pure gBASIC because what remains after reading is POLICY -- which row is a header, which is a total -- which belongs where the report's owner can change it. Contract is "automatic when safe, spec when not, and it tells you which": `grid.tables` guesses and returns a CONFIDENCE plus its reasons, `grid.extract` does exactly what a spec record says (starts/ends anchor by CONTENT so inserting rows cannot break it, header_rows joins a two-row header, drop_totals, drop_matching, columns). Fixture messy.xlsx carries every irregularity §5 names -- title above the table, a two-row header whose parent is written only above the first child (how Excel stores a merged cell), an interleaved subtotal plus grand total, a trailing note after a blank row, a second table of different width, an empty spacer column mid-span -- so the correct answer is deliberately NOT what a top-left-to-bottom-right reader produces. The load-bearing assertion is ARITHMETIC not a golden: the extracted column must sum to the figure the SHEET'S OWN TOTAL ROW claims, checked against the sheet rather than a literal, because a golden would happily record a subtotal absorbed as data while the sum cannot. Also asserts the automatic path reports LOW confidence on the messy table and HIGH on the clean one (a detector confident about the messy case is worse than none), and that a spec matching nothing reports ok=false rather than an empty frame that reads as "no data". Needs GBASIC_PATH since grid.bas loads frame.bas, hence its own runner. Deferred: the ARI TEXT spec language over grids -- the spec is a record for now, which needs no parser
./tests/run_consolidate.sh # L3 consolidation (stdlib/consolidate.bas over docs/xlsx_design.md §6 and §13.O): many tapes with the same MEANING and a different SURFACE merged into one frame, the participation-loan/CECL problem. `consolidate.merge(sources, spec)` -> {ok,frame,accepted,rejected,notes}; a spec column is {from:[aliases], kind:"text"|"money"|"percent"|"number", required:, scale:}. Originally `names`/`kind`, because BOTH `from` and `as` were reserved words that could not be record keys at all; the 2026-08-13 parser change fixed that for `from`, which is now used, and `names` is still accepted with one call site deliberately left on it so the compatibility path stays exercised. The type field stays `kind` rather than `as`: at the time, a keyword could be a record LITERAL key but still could not follow a DOT (`rule.as` was a parse error, `{as:1}` was fine), so `as` would have forced `rule["as"]` throughout. THAT RESTRICTION IS GONE -- the keyword-fields work moved the fix into the LEXER, where after a `.` a word can only be a field, and all 16 keywords tried resolve after a dot (measured 2026-09-01, see run_keyword_fields). `kind` stays because it says more, not because `as` cannot be written. See DOGFOOD 2026-08-15. Aliasing is fuzzy first (names normalised to letters+digits, so "Rate (%)" ~ "rate"), money accepts the same union ARI does ($1,500.00 / (1,200.00) / 1,200.00- / 9,000, anything else unknown NEVER zero), a source missing a REQUIRED column is rejected BY NAME rather than emitted with unknowns (a tape short a balance column understates the pool and an understated pool looks like a small one), and every row carries its source because an untraceable consolidated figure is not auditable. THE TRAP is the percent scale: 5.25 and 0.0475 are the same thing 100x apart and nothing in the file says which -- a written % settles it outright, otherwise the judgement is per COLUMN from all values at once (a fraction cannot exceed 1) and a column entirely at or below 1 is reported AMBIGUOUS with `scale:` available to remove the guess. Assertions are ARITHMETIC not goldens: balances must sum exactly (which also proves cents survive; when written, print additionally truncated 23750.25 to 23750.2, fixed by PLAT-NUMFMT 2026-08-14) and every normalised rate must fall in a plausible band, because a mis-inferred scale is plausible in isolation and only a band check sees it. Needs GBASIC_PATH
./tests/run_dbframe.sh     # L4 easy half (stdlib/dbframe.bas over docs/xlsx_design.md §7 and §13.P): a frame becomes a database table, which §7 sequences BEFORE the formula compiler and calls valuable on its own. Runs the WHOLE pipeline end to end -- xlsx -> grid (L2) -> consolidate (L3) -> sqlite (L4a) -- then queries the result. A column TYPE is decided from every value (all-whole INTEGER, any fractional REAL, mixed TEXT, unknown NULL), never from the first, because coercing the rest is how "n/a" becomes 0 and a zero that should have been a gap changes a total. SQL is never built by pasting values: every value is BOUND, and identifiers -- which cannot be bound -- are validated and refused rather than escaped (an unsafe table name, and two headings collapsing to one identifier, both rejected by name). The INJECTION tier EXECUTES the claim instead of asserting it: a loan id of `'); drop table loans;--` is loaded, read back, compared to itself, and the other table counted to prove it still stands. Append vs replace has no silent default -- loading twice appends (create table IF NOT EXISTS; a real schema mismatch still fails loudly from the insert), and the bare `create` on that path was a genuine bug this test caught. Exactness is COMPARED not printed: the total must equal 265550.75; print rendered that as 265551 when this was written, so a printed check would have hidden the cents the pipeline exists to preserve (PLAT-NUMFMT fixed the formatter 2026-08-14; the comparison stands because it asserts the pipeline preserved the value, not that print can show it). Needs GBASIC_PATH; SKIPs when sqlite is not in the build
./tests/run_compile.sh     # L4 proper: the FORMULA COMPILER (xlsx.to_sql, docs/xlsx_design.md §7 and §13.Q). A column formula is one transformation over a column, so IF(C2>0.05,ROUND(B2*C2,2),0) lowers to CASE WHEN "rate" > 0.05 THEN ROUND("balance"*"rate",2) ELSE 0 END and the database runs it once over the table. Implemented as a SECOND PASS over the SAME lexer as the evaluator with the same precedence chain, emitting text instead of values -- sharing the lexer is the point, since a compiler parsing the dialect slightly differently would disagree with the interpreter on inputs neither test covers. THE ORACLE TIER is the one that matters: the same workbook is evaluated cell-by-cell by xlsx.evaluate AND loaded into SQLite and computed by the compiled SQL, compared ROW BY ROW -- the only check that catches a compiler which is self-consistently wrong, and it immediately caught the compiler lowering AND while the evaluator still reported AND unimplemented (AND/OR/NOT were then added, moving 410,121 corpus cells from unsupported to judged, 380,430 of them agreeing -- note the RATE dips 94.97%->94.92% because the newly-judged population agrees at ~93% vs the existing 95%, so quoting the rate alone would report a real gain as a regression). REFUSALS are the feature, each preventing a specific plausible wrong answer: a row-SPANNING range is an aggregate that changes cardinality, a reference to another row is a window function, VLOOKUP is a join, SUMIF a filtered aggregate, MIN with one argument is SQL's aggregate MIN not the scalar one, a volatile function has no set-based meaning. `_row` in the mapping pins the formula's row so a ref to a fixed cell ABOVE the data is caught rather than compiled to that row's column. Two bugs the tests caught: SUM(B2:D2) compiled to COALESCE(a,b,c,0) -- SQL first-non-null, not a sum -- and a bare defined name consumed following tokens as if it were a call. Also covers §13.R, the VECTORISED in-memory target `xlsx.apply(formula, mapping, frame)` -> one value per row: §7's other compile target, one call producing a whole column with no workbook, no snapshot and no per-cell dispatch. Honest about what it is NOT -- the formula is re-lexed per row since the evaluator has no retained AST -- so the only claim made is the measured one: ~70x faster than the loop a caller would otherwise write (xlsx.evaluate per row, which rebuilds the snapshot each call), asserted as a RATIO with the gate at 8x, never an absolute time. THREE-WAY tier: interpreter, SQL and the frame pass must produce the SAME column, because two agreeing could be two implementations of one misunderstanding while three agreeing across a cell-graph interpreter, a database and an in-memory pass is far stronger. It exposed that range expansion needed a snapshot, so SUM(B2:D2) silently returned only B in frame mode; a row-SPANNING range now yields #REF! there since a frame row cannot see its neighbours. Needs GBASIC_PATH and sqlite
./tests/run_market.sh      # stdlib/market.bas -- daily price history, the INPUT the finance stack never had. `stats.simple_returns`, `sharpe_ratio`, `max_drawdown`, `value_at_risk`, `capm` and `forensics.altman_classic(facts, prices)` all take prices as an ARGUMENT and nothing in the tree produced them (EDGAR serves filings, not quotes), so the whole return-based half of the library was unreachable without hand-assembling a series. Tested the way it is because BOTH failure modes are plausible NUMBERS rather than errors, which is exactly what a golden cannot catch: ORDER (providers disagree about oldest-first vs newest-first, and simple_returns on a reversed series returns the NEGATED sequence -- ordinary-looking data with every sign wrong -- so the fixture is SCRAMBLED ON PURPOSE and the test names all five dates in order plus the consequence, that the first return on this falling series must be NEGATIVE; proven red by removing the sort, which moves four dates AND flips the sign assertion), and ADJUSTMENT (a 2-for-1 split halves the raw close, so returns from unadjusted prices read as a -50% day -- `adjusted` reports what the PROVIDER supplies and is never assumed; both providers asserted). Failure is a VALUE, not a raise, since an unknown symbol and a dead network are ordinary outcomes here. NO NETWORK in either direction: fixtures are committed and replayed through market.offline, the seam llm.bas and edgar.bas already use, and a tripwire tier asserts the fixture holds no live provider handle. Never skips
./tests/run_otp.sh          # `otp` -- one-time passwords as a second factor (stdlib/otp.bas, docs/otp_design.md). RFC 4226 HOTP + RFC 6238 TOTP. gBASIC OWNS THE FACTOR rather than delegating: a capability that only works for Active Directory shops is not a platform capability, so the identity SOURCE stays a separate decision. SELF-CHECKING NOT GOLDEN AND HERE THAT IS FORCED -- every defect produces a PLAUSIBLE SIX-DIGIT NUMBER, so a golden would record a wrong code as expected and defend it forever, and nobody reviewing it could tell 081804 from 081805 by eye. THE ORACLE IS EXTERNAL AND PUBLISHED: RFC 4226 Appendix D and RFC 6238 Appendix B, computed in python3 BEFORE the library existed, so the fixture asserts what the RFCs say rather than what we emitted. THE VECTORS CARRY THEIR OWN TRAP and it is exercised -- the SHA256/SHA512 seeds are LONGER than SHA1's (32 and 64 bytes against 20) and reusing the SHA1 seed for all three yields wrong answers whose correction then gets applied TO THE CODE INSTEAD OF THE SEED (proven red). The t=20000000000 row is not decoration: the counter reaches 666666666, past what careless 32-bit packing holds. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS REPLAY, because a code stays valid for its whole 30-second step so being arithmetically correct is not enough to be accepted twice -- asserted as a DIFFERENCE with a CONTROL (the next step's code still works), since "accepted" alone passes on a library with no guard and "refused" alone on one that refuses everything. Replay is made STRUCTURAL rather than documented: `otp.check` REQUIRES the last counter the account accepted and RETURNS the one it matched, the rule reasoning.finding follows for `search.width` -- the argument easiest to forget is the one that must not be optional. RATE LIMITING IS THE ACTUAL BREAK (six digits is 10^6) and is the CALLER'S, because a library cannot hold per-account state across requests; the design says so rather than leaving it inferred. Skew bounded at 10 steps (each widens the brute-force window linearly). Refusals each beside their NEAREST LEGAL NEIGHBOUR, or a refusal suite is satisfied by refusing everything. Two successful logins in the SAME step is refused and that is RFC 6238 s5.2, not an accident. `base32_decode` answers UNKNOWN for non-base32, never "" -- different answers -- and accepts lower case and whitespace deliberately, since a secret is transcribed by hand and displayed in spaced groups so refusing that noise turns a usability problem into a lockout. `hmac_sha1` exists for TOTP AND NOTHING ELSE (apps ignore the algorithm parameter and compute SHA1); SHA-1 is broken for collisions, HMAC does not rest on that. Four perturbations proven red. Headless, no network, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_crypto.sh      # the libcrypto builtins and stdlib/crypto.bas: base64/hex (including base64url high bytes, the cross-alphabet decode, and empty input), SHA/MD5 digests, HMAC, constant-time compare, AES-256-GCM, Ed25519, and the Phase-3 compositions (signed cookies, CSRF, JWT/HS256). Two devices make crypto goldens possible at all: binary outputs are HEX-ENCODED before printing, so a golden is stable across architectures; and anything with a random input is asserted as a BOOLEAN rather than a value -- AES-GCM runs with a FIXED key and nonce so ciphertext||tag is deterministic, while Ed25519 keypairs are random, so those cases are round-trip and TAMPER-REJECTION checks instead. IMPORTANT: the external verification (python hashlib/hmac, the openssl CLI, pyca/cryptography, PyJWT) was done ONCE BY HAND and is recorded in the fixture headers -- this suite PINS those vectors, it does not re-derive them, so a rebaseline here is only meaningful if the same external check is repeated. Five negatives cover arity, type and range. Skips cleanly without libcrypto
./tests/run_gui_parse.sh    # parse-only smoke for examples/qt: run_qt_examples RUNS them but skips on a build without Qt, so syntax rot there is caught here (no display needed). The GTK and gi example directories it once parsed were removed with GTK and gi on 2026-10-22
./tests/run_doc_examples.sh # every ```basic block in the docs, CHECKED BY RUNNING IT. Exists because three documentation sweeps in one day each missed the same defect while READING: 163 lines used `#` as a comment, which gBASIC does not have -- every one of those samples dies on the first character of the comment if a reader copies it.
