The cleanup handler released the locks and then ended the process with `_exit(128 + signo)` -- which is the SHELL'S RENDERING of death by a signal and not the thing itself: a supervisor reading the real wait status saw `WIFSIGNALED=false, exit_code=143` where a program that had never taken a lock gives `WIFSIGNALED=true, signal=15`, so it could not tell a child that was KILLED from one that CHOSE to exit 143 -- and the handler is installed for the LIFE OF THE PROCESS, so releasing the lock did not restore it. WHY IT SURVIVED: bash reports `143` in `$?` for BOTH, so no shell-level check in this tree could see it, and the probe uses waitpid directly (tests/lock_signal_probe.py). THE FIX IS THREE LINES AND ONE IS LOAD-BEARING: a signal is BLOCKED while its own handler runs, so restoring SIG_DFL and calling `raise` merely marks it PENDING and the following `_exit` still wins -- it must be UNBLOCKED first, or the fix looks right and changes nothing, which is exactly what the first perturbation proves. PARITY is an EQUALITY, not a difference: a locked child and an unlocked one must die the SAME way, for all three signals the handler claims. DISTINCT is the control that stops the fix from being "call every death a signal" -- a program that really exits 143 must still report an exit -- and it SENDS NO SIGNAL, which was a CORRECTION rather than a choice: a fixture that exits by itself RACES the signal and reported `signal 15` under a perturbation, so it had been passing on timing rather than on the property it names. AND THE `released` TIER IS AN OUTCOME CHECK, NOT A CONTROL, which was MEASURED rather than assumed: with the three `signal()` installs deleted outright it STILL PASSES, because flock is released by the KERNEL when the process dies -- so the handler did nothing the kernel does not already do and its only observable effect was the wrong exit status. That redundancy is filed in DOGFOOD rather than acted on here. NO VALGRIND TIER and the suite says why: the process under test dies by a signal on purpose, so there is no clean exit for a report to be written at. Skips without python3, and says why -- a shell cannot tell a signal death from exit 143, which is the whole point
./tests/run_libcurl_floor.sh # gBASIC MUST COMPILE AGAINST THE libcurl OF THE PLATFORM IT SHIPS FOR. Three call sites used `CURLOPT_PROTOCOLS_STR`/`CURLOPT_REDIR_PROTOCOLS_STR`, which arrived in libcurl 7.85.0; ubuntu:22.04 ships 7.81.0 -- and that image is not an arbitrary old distribution, it is THE BASE IMAGE tools/build-release-tarball.sh builds in, chosen for its glibc floor. So on the exact platform the download targets, `make` FAILED with five errors, and nothing here could see it because this machine has 8.18.0 -- a development box is always the newest thing in the room. THE SHAPE IS WORSE THAN THE INSTANCE: an optional dependency that is MISSING degrades to a clean runtime error, which is the whole HAVE_* convention, while one that is PRESENT BUT OLDER took the ENTIRE BINARY down, every unrelated feature with it -- and the tarball only escaped because it builds LEAN and never links libcurl, so the one configuration nobody could ship was also the one nobody was testing. THE GUARD IS A SYMBOL COMPARISON RATHER THAN A BUILD, deliberately: the real check needs podman, a network and two minutes, which is a tier people run once, while comparing every libcurl identifier the sources use against a GENERATED list of what exists at the floor (tests/libcurl_floor/symbols.txt, 1,174 names from the 22.04 image, made by tools/make_libcurl_floor_symbols.sh -- generated, never hand-edited, because a fixture nothing can reproduce tests itself) catches the same class in milliseconds with nothing installed. It is GENERAL: it catches the NEXT post-floor symbol, not the five that were found. THE CONTROL IS WHAT KEEPS IT FROM BEING A COMMENT -- the scanner must FLAG a planted unguarded use and must LEAVE ALONE the same name once it sits inside a `#if LIBCURL_VERSION_NUM` guard, or the rule would forbid the very shim that fixes it; without the first half, "no offenders" is equally satisfied by a scanner that read nothing. TWO SCANNER DEFECTS FOUND BY RUNNING IT, both the false-positive direction: awk has no \b, so `GB_CURL_PROTOCOLS` read as `CURL_PROTOCOLS` and `HAVE_LIBCURL` as `LIBCURL`, reporting the shim as an instance of the defect; and smtp.c argues about dot-stuffing in a BLOCK COMMENT saying "LIBCURL'S", so a scanner that reads prose reports the ARGUMENT for a rule as a breach of it (`//` is stripped only when not preceded by `:`, so the one in "http://host" stays part of its string). THE GUARD TIER EXISTS BECAUSE THE CHEAPEST WAY TO CLEAR A FLOOR FAILURE IS TO DELETE THE OPTION, which trades a red build for a silently missing security setting -- so the restriction is required in BOTH branches of the shim AND the call sites are COUNTED (>=3 set, >=2 also on redirect), since the shim can be perfect while every call to it is gone. BUILD is the real thing and is OPT-IN (LIBCURL_FLOOR_BUILD=1), printing NOT RUN rather than nothing, because a tier silent about being absent reads exactly like one that passed; verified 2026-09-23, the full configuration compiles against 7.81.0. AND A MEASUREMENT THAT CHANGED WHAT WAS BUILT: the obvious test is BEHAVIOURAL -- redirect to file:///etc/passwd and require gBASIC not to follow -- and it PASSED against a binary with the redirect restriction deleted, twice over, because `webclient.get` and `http.start` each check the scheme in gBASIC before libcurl sees it AND libcurl's own default REDIR_PROTOCOLS has excluded file/scp since 7.19.4. So all three explicit settings are defence in depth that no gBASIC program can today distinguish from their absence, and that suite would have been a green line measuring nothing in a file whose subject is a security control. The fixtures were DELETED rather than shipped; the settings are KEPT, because both reasons they are redundant are facts about OTHER code that can change without anything here noticing. Headless, no network, never skips the hermetic tiers
./tests/run_dir_builtins.sh # `exists` on a DIRECTORY, and `make_dir(path, { parents: true })`. WHAT IT REPLACED: stdlib/persist.bas carried a 24-line `ensure_dir` that split a path and walked it segment by segment, and THE WALK WAS NOT A MATTER OF TASTE -- `make_dir` had no parents mode and `exists` would not take a directory reference, so each level had to be guarded through a FILE reference to a path that is not a file. The ledger carried it as a live limitation for months; it is ONE CALL now, and the `_last` helper that existed only to serve the walk went with it. WHAT DELIBERATELY DID NOT CHANGE, and it is ASSERTED rather than commented: `make_dir` was NOT made idempotent, which is the obvious repair and the wrong one -- a bare mkdir is ATOMIC, so "did I create it" is how a program takes a lock across processes without one, and an existing directory has to stay an error for that to keep working. The OTHER question got its own spelling instead, the same argument `accounting` made for refusing a second close. THE MUTEX TIER states that between PROCESSES, where it matters and where a fixture cannot reach: two contenders race for one name and EXACTLY ONE may win, with the CONTROL that under `parents` the same race has NO loser -- or the two spellings would be one spelling with a flag. THE UNREADABLE TIER IS THE ONE NOTHING ELSE CAN SEE: `exists` used to be `fopen(path,"rb") != NULL`, which answers a DIFFERENT question and agreed with the right one only by accident of glibc (fopen succeeds on a directory here, so `exists({file}"/etc")` was already true) -- and A FILE THAT EXISTS AND IS NOT READABLE WAS REPORTED ABSENT. It answers by `stat` now, and only a file with its permissions removed distinguishes the two implementations; the mode change is made from OUTSIDE because gBASIC has no chmod (a live ledger bullet), and the tier SKIPS WITH A REASON as root or where the chmod did not take, since a tier that cannot create its own premise must say so rather than pass. Its CONTROL is an absent file still reporting false, without which "true" is satisfied by an exists that never looks. Refusals each beside their nearest legal neighbour, including the NARROWING control -- `read` and `lock` still refuse a directory reference, because only `exists` asks about a PATH rather than about contents. Five perturbations proven red, each caught by the tier written for it; the fopen one by the UNREADABLE tier ALONE, since fopen agrees with stat on every other case in the file. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind and the root/permission guard)
./tests/run_path_split.sh # `stem(p)` -- the one third of path splitting that was really MISSING, and the interior-NUL door the same function left open for four of its six names. WHAT WAS ACTUALLY ABSENT: docs/etl_files_design.md §1 listed `basename`/`dirname`/`stem` among the gaps for the ETL book and MEASURED, two thirds had shipped months earlier under other names -- `file_name` is basename, `directory_name` is dirname, `extension` is the suffix, all three documented and all three taking a string or a file or directory reference alike. That is the SECOND of five items in that one list to turn out already present (`modified` is `file_mtime`), so 40% of what the document called absent was there, and BOTH WERE FOUND BY RUNNING A PROBE rather than by reading -- which is the argument for an executable gap list, the shape run_limitations.sh gives DOGFOOD's ledger. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS AN INVARIANT, NOT A LIST OF VALUES, because `stem` and `extension` are two halves of ONE cut: with an extension stem + "." + extension is file_name, without one stem IS file_name -- and that is what found the only real design question in it, since `extension` answers "" for TWO DIFFERENT REASONS (no dot at all, and a dot with nothing after it) and only the first means "nothing to strip". Sharing one scan between the two was necessary and NOT SUFFICIENT: before the trailing-dot rule `stem("a.")` answered "a" while `extension("a.")` answered "", and `stem("..")` answered "." -- both ORDINARY FILENAMES, which is why the fixture is self-checking and a golden would have recorded one as expected and defended it. `extension` is unchanged by the rule, having already answered "" in both cases, so NO GOLDEN MOVED. THE ORACLE IS python3's posixpath.splitext over the SAME battery -- read from the dump the FIXTURE writes, so there is one copy of it and the oracle cannot drift onto an easier set of names -- and 17 of 18 agree, with the one divergence PINNED BY NAME: python answers "a" for "a." because its extension keeps the dot where ours is documented as dropping it, so a change of convention has to come here and say so. THE NUL TIER IS PLAT-NUL'S STANDING LESSON APPLIED TO ONE FUNCTION: `real_path` and `file_type` refused an interior NUL and the other four branches of the same function did not, with the argument written three lines above them -- a path crosses into the system as a NUL-terminated C string, so one with a NUL inside would be CHECKED as one path and OPENED as another, which is about what a PATH IS and not about which call is holding one. Measured before widening it, with p = "/a/b" + chr(0) + "x/c.txt": `file_name(p)` answered "b" (the basename of `/a/b`), `directory_name(p)` answered "/a", and `join_path("/a" + chr(0) + "b", "c")` answered "/a/c" -- three plausible paths, none about the path the program held, nothing raised. All seven refuse now, BOTH arguments of join_path included, since a rule applied to the first alone is one a caller walks around by swapping them; the `{file}` and `{dir}` modifiers already refused one at CONSTRUCTION, which is why testing the string kind suffices and is asserted here rather than assumed. THE CONTROL outnumbers nothing and is still the load-bearing half of that tier: five path builtins are in the inner loop of every file walk, so a family that refuses everything is worse than the defect. MIGRATION: stdlib/edgar.bas hand-rolled `directory_name` as a 17-line segment walk -- the measurement §7 cited as evidence the gap existed -- and it is one call now with all 252 example goldens byte-identical; re-reading the other two sites that entry named, only one was a path split at all, since `web._segments` and `edgar._url_to_fixture` need the segment ARRAY (`contains(parts, "submissions")` asks whether a word appears anywhere in a URL). Five perturbations proven red, each caught by the tier written for it -- the NUL check narrowed to the first argument is caught by the right-hand join_path assertion and by NOTHING ELSE. NOTE a new builtin must also reach the VS Code grammar, which is GENERATED from src/builtins.c: run_editor_grammar.sh's drift tier caught the omission here, which is the one gate that notices. Headless, GI-independent, skips only the oracle without python3 -- and says it cross-checked NOTHING when it does.
./tests/run_intake.sh # `intake` INCREMENT 1 -- what arrived, what was taken (docs/intake_design.md). THE HALF OF THE ETL CYCLE THAT NEEDS STATE: everything else that brief asked for became a PRIMITIVE because it was a thing the language could not do, where "have I already imported this?" is different in kind -- a program could always answer it and had to write the answer itself, every time, differently. SELF-CHECKING NOT GOLDEN AND FORCED HARDER THAN USUAL: every defect this library can have produces AN ORDINARY-LOOKING SUCCESSFUL RUN -- a ledger that records nothing, an identity that always matches, a readiness rule that always says yes each print the transcript of a working import, and the thing being got wrong is precisely the thing nobody can see. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS A THREE-WAY DIFFERENCE over one re-dropped filename: with new contents AND a new size, "path" must skip while "stamp" and "content" take; with the SAME size and the SAME mtime -- a corrected file of the same length, which needs `touch -r` and is why it is in the runner -- "stamp" must skip while "content" still takes. Either case alone passes on a ledger that always answers one way. `identity:` AND `ready:` ARE BOTH REQUIRED WITH NO DEFAULT (the `ldap.security` rule) and each refusal NAMES ITS THREE CHOICES, so the error teaches the decision. THE CRASH TIER PERFORMS A DEATH, because durability is a claim about what survives one: SIGKILL mid-import behind a gate file, and a second open must report EXACTLY the files that completed -- not fewer (the journal was not durable) and not more (it recorded work that never ran). THE LEDGER IS A SNAPSHOT PLUS AN APPEND-ONLY JOURNAL and the replay is `for each line in f`, so this library's recovery path is the streaming increment it was built on. THE SHAPE TIER'S FIRST TWO DRAFTS EACH ASSERTED SOMETHING FALSE, which is the lesson: draft 1 timed whole runs and reported 4.6x over a 10x ledger, true and not the claim (`open` parses the snapshot and `close` writes it, so the per-RUN cost is legitimately linear); draft 2 subtracted that baseline and reported 10.9x, so the per-FILE cost is linear too and the design doc's "it costs nothing" was WRONG -- isolated with no intake involved, 120 record-field assignments THROUGH A FUNCTION cost ~0s at 2,000 fields and ~1s at 20,000, because a record is a VALUE and writing one field of a copy detaches the array, and the alternative that would make it flat DOES NOT EXIST IN THE LANGUAGE (library-level state is `undefined variable` from a sibling function). So it asserts a DIFFERENCE BETWEEN TWO IMPLEMENTATIONS at one ledger size, the rewrite written into the tier: 322ms against 2041ms, 13 KB written against 266 MB. THE ORDERING TIER EXISTS BECAUSE A PERTURBATION SWAPPING TWO LINES LEFT EVERYTHING GREEN -- `compact` writes the snapshot and THEN removes the journal, and that claim is only testable if a durable write can be made to FAIL, which is a directory put where the snapshot has to go AFTER open. TWO DEFECTS FOUND IN THINGS THIS LIBRARY ONLY USES: `read` on a DIRECTORY aborted the interpreter (fopen succeeds, ftell answers LONG_MAX, malloc fails, abort -- SIGABRT and a core dump, which takes block-buffered stdout with it so a program tracing its progress printed NOTHING), and `persist.read_status` then RAISED out of the one function whose whole contract is that it reports missing/corrupt/loaded as a VALUE, because `exists` answers true for a directory. Both fixed, both with controls that an ordinary file still reads. A VANISHED FILE IS A DANGLING SYMLINK, which is that case FROZEN -- the only deterministic way to write it, since a real mid-scan deletion is a race -- and the tier asserts its own PREMISE (the listing reports the entry, `exists` denies it), which is why the check is a question rather than a caught raise. CONCURRENCY MEASURES THE WAIT rather than asserting a refusal there is no way to make, `lock` having no non-blocking form. INCREMENT 2 (`archive`/`purge`) SHIPPED 2026-10-16: archive mirrors the source tree (which is what `relative` is for -- two quarters' files share a `name`), and the two cases are told apart BEFORE acting, because `atomic_replace` is same-filesystem ONLY and says so by name -- a rename within one filesystem, ASSERTED BY INODE rather than by a stopwatch (the first draft timed 64 MB and reported 1.3x, because the second filesystem is tmpfs and copying into RAM from page cache is barely slower than a rename), and copy-CHECK-THE-BYTE-COUNT-delete across two. `move` is REFUSED BY A SOURCE TRIPWIRE and that claim was PROVEN UNTESTABLE first: a perturbation replacing the whole dance with one `move` left EVERY OTHER TIER GREEN, including the inode check, since `move` renames on one filesystem too and is right whenever nothing goes wrong -- what the split buys appears only on a filesystem that reports a successful `fclose` without writing the bytes. The tripwire carries TWO controls: it must flag a planted `move(` and must NOT flag this library's own comments about `move`, which discuss it at length. PURGE REPORTS BY DEFAULT (deleting is the one irreversible act here) and its report CARRIES THE NEW LEDGER -- a correction found by making the mistake in the design's own worked example, since a record is a VALUE and `purge` cannot return two things; `close` REPLAYS THE JOURNAL BEFORE COMPACTING now, which came out of that bug and makes the journal the source of truth, so a dropped ledger is wasteful rather than destructive (asserted across two processes). AND A REFUSAL THAT ACTED FIRST: `keep: 6 months` is refused because months have no fixed length, and that check lived in the seconds conversion AT THE END -- so the call archived the file and THEN raised, leaving the file in the archive, no ledger record of it, and the next run able to find neither; the fixture now asserts a refused archive moved nothing, which nothing had. THE DST TIER STATES ITS OWN TIMEZONE because this suite pins TZ=UTC: the conversion was `epoch(from_epoch(0) + d)`, local-time arithmetic, which across a DST boundary made 180 days 15548400 seconds -- an hour short and a plausible number -- and under UTC the wrong arithmetic gives the right answer, so no other tier could see it. Seventeen perturbations proven red across both increments, each caught by the tier written for it. NOTE the sixth instance of the `set -e` trap in this tree landed in THIS suite: a tier captured `$(./gbasic ...)` from a program a perturbation makes RAISE, so `set -e` ended the run SILENTLY mid-tier with no FAIL line -- captured with `set +e` and the exit code checked first now. NOTE two of this suite's own tiers were wrong first and BOTH were my runner conflating a ledger's NAME with its identity RULE -- and the concurrency one DISCARDED THE SCANNER'S OUTPUT with `|| true`, so a refusal read as "the lock does not block" and reported a missing lock over a lock that works. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_read_at.sh # `read_at(f, position, count)` -- THE OTHER HALF OF A DOOR THAT ONLY OPENED ONE WAY. `overwrite(f, text, position)` has written at a byte offset since it shipped and NOTHING COULD READ AT ONE, so a program could rewrite the middle of a file it had no way to inspect and the only reader was whole-file. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS THEREFORE A ROUND TRIP THROUGH BOTH -- one offset space, two directions, at all ten positions of a ten-byte file, which is the only check that fails if they disagree about where position 3 is, since an off-by-one returns four PERFECTLY REAL BYTES from the wrong place. It is also the gap that made BINARY INPUT UNSTREAMABLE (`for each line` is the only other bounded reader and a line is unbounded), asserted as a DIFFERENCE on one 50 MB file with no newline in it: 14.8 MB resident chunked against 115 MB by line, with a SHAPE tier requiring the chunked figure to stay flat across a 4x change in file size -- the only way to tell a bounded reader from one that happens to fit. THE TRAP IS THE SHARPEST THING HERE AND THE SUITE FOUND IT RATHER THAN THE DESIGN: a position is BYTES and `len` is CODEPOINTS, so the obvious `pos = pos + len(chunk)` loop advances SHORT on anything but text, re-reads what it had, and reassembles a LONGER CORRUPTED FILE in silence -- measured, a 65,536-byte chunk of random bytes has len 62,800. The 50 MB zeros tier could NOT see it (NUL is a single-byte codepoint, so the two agree) and the 3 MB RANDOM reassembly tier did: COMFORTABLE TEST DATA HID IT, the shape recipe 7's hash bug already has a name for. Asserted deterministically with a two-byte character and as a DIFFERENCE between the same loop written both ways. TWO PERTURBATIONS WENT GREEN FIRST AND BOTH TAUGHT SOMETHING: removing the count CLAMP leaves every ANSWER correct (fread reads only what is there) and changes only how much was allocated, so it is caught by asking for nine quintillion bytes of a ten-byte file -- unclamped that is a failed malloc, which in this interpreter is `abort()`, the SIGABRT-and-core-dump defect the whole-file readers were fixed for a day earlier. And the DIRECTORY guard is redundant for the MESSAGE (fread on a directory fails with EISDIR and the error path reports it, measured both ways) while being load-bearing for the ALLOCATION -- a directory's `ftell` answers LONG_MAX, so the clamp has nothing to clamp against. THE TIER FOR IT MUST USE A DIRECTORY ON THE REPOSITORY'S FILESYSTEM, which is why the fixture takes a second argument: what `fseek`/`ftell` answer for a directory is a fact about the FILESYSTEM, LONG_MAX on ext4 and an outright `fseek` failure under /tmp's tmpfs, so pointed at the `mktemp -d` work directory the tier stayed GREEN against a build that core-dumps by hand on the same machine. Five perturbations proven red. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_metadata.sh # METADATA: what a file says about itself, and changing it -- `permissions`, `chmod`, `set_mtime`, `is_symlink`, `symlink`. `chmod` was the LAST OUTSTANDING ITEM of the five docs/etl_files_design.md §1 called absent, and MEASURING AROUND IT FOUND THREE DEFECTS RATHER THAN ONE GAP. (1) `file_type` FOLLOWS a symlink -- measured, a link to a file answers "file" and a link to a directory "folder" -- so nothing could ask whether a path IS one, while `gb_is_symlink` had been in the platform layer since the glob needed a cycle guard, exposed by nothing. (2) NOTHING COULD CREATE ONE: three runners build links with `ln -s` and run_scratch.sh states in as many words that its load-bearing tier lives in bash for want of this. (3) THE LISTING DISAGREED WITH `file_type` about a dangling link, falling back to "file" for anything that was not a directory -- so a dangling symlink, a FIFO, a socket and a device all arrived labelled `file` while `file_type` said `unknown` or `other`, two routes to one question disagreeing and the listing holding the WRONG one, there being no file there: `if e.type = "file" then read(e.path)` raises on the first and BLOCKS FOREVER on the second, and `files(d, "**/*")` shares the code. `permissions` ANSWERS TWO DIFFERENT QUESTIONS ON PURPOSE and the field names say which: `mode` is what the FILE says, `readable`/`writable`/`executable` are what THIS PROCESS can do (access(2)) -- root reads a 0000 file and a 0644 file on a read-only mount is not writable, so conflating them is the classic bug. THE MODE IS TEXT AND THAT IS FORCED rather than chosen: gBASIC has no octal literal, measured -- `0o600` is a parse error (the lexer reads it as a duration unit) and `0600` is the number six hundred -- so `chmod` REFUSES A NUMBER BY NAME with the arithmetic in the message, six hundred being a perfectly good mode_t that would set bits nobody asked for AND SUCCEED. THE ORACLE TIER IS WHAT THE FIXTURE CANNOT BE: `chmod` then `permissions` is two calls into ONE implementation and passes on any self-consistently wrong rendering (the decimal 420 where 0644 belongs reads as an answer and agrees with itself), so `stat -c %a` judges five modes from outside. THE ACCESS TIER IS INFORMATIVE EITHER WAY ROUND rather than skipping: as an ordinary user a 0000 file is unreadable and the two fields agree, and AS ROOT it is readable while the mode still says 0000, which IS the divergence and is why both fields exist. THE FIFO IS THE ONE FILE KIND gBASIC CANNOT CREATE and the one that makes the old "file" label dangerous rather than merely wrong, so it is the runner that asserts both routes answer `other`. WARNING 2114 (chmod could not express every bit) IS UNREACHABLE ON POSIX, where every bit is honoured, so it is carried by a SOURCE TRIPWIRE with its own vacuity control and by Windows, which is the only place it can fire -- and if it does not fire there, the `dropped` out-parameter is wrong rather than the warning. Seven perturbations proven red, and ONE OF THEM FOUND TWO CHECKS OF MINE THAT COULD NEVER HAVE FAILED: passing `error` to the `refused()` helper CLAIMS it, so the `if error then` immediately after was false and the two message assertions inside it never ran -- caught by disabling the numeric-mode refusal and watching the file stay green, since falling through to the ordinary type check still refuses the call while naming nothing a reader can act on. THE LISTING CORRECTION BROKE `intake` AND THAT IS HOW IT WAS CAUGHT: `files` filtered on `type = "file"` EXACTLY, so relabelling a dangling link `unknown` silently DROPPED it from the listing and intake's withdrawn-file report became unreachable -- the silence that tier exists to prevent, back again. `files` excludes FOLDERS now, and the fixture asserts a dangling link is still returned with two controls. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind and mkfifo)
./tests/run_scratch.sh # SCRATCH SPACE: `temp_file`, `temp_dir` and `remove_tree` -- make somewhere to work, and get rid of it afterwards. BEFORE THEM A PROGRAM HAD NEITHER END: there was no way to ask for a unique name, so `persist.bas` writes `path + ".tmp"` beside its destination and two writers of one file share one temp name; and `remove_dir` removes only an EMPTY directory, so nothing in the language could remove a tree it had just built. `remove_tree` gets its OWN NAME rather than an option on `remove_dir`, which was already decided in the comment above `remove_dir` -- the option it would be offered is a recursive delete and that "belongs to a decision nobody should make by passing a flag". SELF-CHECKING NOT GOLDEN AND FORCED: every defect in this family produces AN ORDINARY-LOOKING SUCCESS -- a `temp_file` that answers a name without creating the file looks identical until two programs collide, a `remove_tree` that FOLLOWED a symlink reports the same `true` while removing somebody else's data, and a prefix that escapes its directory puts the file somewhere the caller did not name and says nothing. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS THE SYMLINK AND IT IS THE REASON THE RUNNER EXISTS: gBASIC cannot create a symlink, so the one behaviour separating a correct recursive delete from a destructive one cannot be written in the language it is a property of -- the tier builds a tree holding a link to a directory OUTSIDE it and requires the target to survive WITH ITS CONTENT (a walk that truncated it would leave the file there), with the companion refusal that a link given as the TOP path is refused by name, since `rm -rf` on a link removes the link and stops and a function called `remove_tree` answering "done" having removed no tree is worse than a refusal. `in:` EXISTS BECAUSE OF A MEASUREMENT: the brief for a temp file is the safe-write pattern and written the obvious way IT DOES NOT WORK -- `atomic_replace` is same-filesystem only and the system temp directory is routinely on another filesystem, measured here /tmp being tmpfs against the repository's ext4, so a temp file in /tmp CANNOT be swapped onto a destination beside the program's own data. That tier needs a directory on the REPOSITORY's filesystem and the first draft used the work directory, which is a `mktemp -d` IN the system temp directory -- comparing one filesystem with itself, so the difference could not be produced; it SKIPS WITH ITS REASON where the two really are one filesystem, because a tier that cannot establish its own premise must say so rather than pass (run_read_at's directory tier, from the other direction). THE MODE IS READ WITH `stat`, from outside, since gBASIC cannot read a file's permissions: 0600 for a file and 0700 for a directory, which is what lets a program hold a secret in one BEFORE `chmod` exists in this language -- and on Windows it inherits the directory's ACL and that promise CANNOT be made, the divergence being the mode and not the uniqueness. DEPTH IS BOUNDED AT 256, the glob's bound, and the refusal names the TOP of the tree: the first version named the path 256 levels down, 512 bytes of separators that did not fit in the message, so it reported a truncated path with its reason cut off the end -- with the CONTROL that an ordinary four-level tree is still removed, or "it refuses deep trees" is satisfied by refusing every tree. NOTHING IS REMOVED AUTOMATICALLY and the fixture asserts it, deliberately: in the safe-write pattern the temp file is MEANT to outlive the call that made it, it becomes the destination, so an interpreter that swept temp files at exit would break the main use -- and the fixture therefore cleans up after ITSELF, which is not housekeeping but the rule stated, the shape run_repl.sh had to fix after greeting somebody with "5721 previous sessions ended without saving". Seven perturbations proven red, each caught by the tier written for it. TWO DEFECTS IN THIS RUNNER FOUND BY THOSE PERTURBATIONS, both the "a red tier that reports the wrong thing" class: a fixture that DIED reported only the death and swallowed the MISMATCH it had already printed (the perturbation answering a name without creating the file mismatches on line 1 and then raises at `file_size`), and `fmode=$(stat ...)` on a file a perturbation never created ended the suite SILENTLY under `set -e` -- the seventh instance of that trap in this tree, in the one tier whose whole job is to notice a missing file. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind and the one-filesystem case)
./tests/run_inline_modifier.sh # `{file}path` -- the assignment clause, INLINE, wherever an expression may go. A modifier could only be applied by an ASSIGNMENT, so a value wanted once had to be given a NAME AND A LINE to be given it on: MEASURED 2026-09-22 across stdlib, examples and tests, 1,034 one-word clauses of which **510 bind a name that is read exactly once** -- a value named only so it could be passed. IT LIVES IN THE LEXER AND THAT IS THE WHOLE REASON IT WORKS: `{a}` and `{a: 1}` differ at their THIRD token and LALR(1) cannot see that far from the brace, so `LBRACE IDENT RBRACE unary_expression` in the grammar costs ONE shift/reduce conflict against a standard of zero, and the fuller rule that would also admit `{split ","}` inline costs **NINETEEN** -- both measured, both refused, this project having rejected `IDENT expression` as a statement form over FOUR. The lexer may look as far ahead as it likes, so recognising the exact shape `{ IDENT }` there costs none; same technique and same argument as the keyword-field fix. SAFE BECAUSE `{IDENT}` MEANT NOTHING BEFORE (`r = {x}` is a parse error), so nothing can change meaning -- but it also claims the COMMONEST ASSIGNMENT CLAUSE, since `x {date}= s` is that exact shape, so `comparison_lens` accepts the new token as well and every existing clause arrives through it. THE PRICE WAS STATED AND IS NOW PAID OFF: the first increment took a ONE-WORD name only, so `{end of month}`/`{split ","}` still needed the clause -- CLOSED the same day (3534f8f), because the 19-conflict measurement was right and about the WRONG ROUTE. It priced putting the production into `unary_expression`; the fix is in the LEXER, which may look as far ahead as it likes and costs ZERO, with the discriminator ONE TOKEN past the leading identifier -- a record literal always has `:`, `=` or `(` there and a modifier never does, a follow-set DERIVED from `record_field_list` in `src/parser.y` rather than surveyed. Every shape is an expression now: an argument, a multi-word name, a library qualifier and a `;` chain. THE CLAUSE IS NOT REDUNDANT and the division of labour is unchanged, because the two apply to different things -- the clause takes the WHOLE right-hand side while inline binds like unary minus, so `b {number}= "10" + "5"` is the NUMBER 105 and `h = {number}"10" + "5"` is the STRING "105"; a compound assignment (`n {number}+= "5"`) has no inline spelling at all. This sentence claimed the limitation was LIVE, with "a live DOGFOOD bullet with its own probe", for as long as it took somebody to read it -- the bullet was struck the same day and `run_limitations.sh` carries the control BOTH ways. Caught 2026-10-03 while checking what a core-language book would have to teach, which is the only reason anybody looked: this file is read by every session and NO GATE READS IT (run_docs_gate reads docs/, run_limitations reads DOGFOOD.md, and nothing reads CLAUDE.md), so its claims rot in exactly the way the gates exist to prevent. THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS PARITY over ten modifier kinds, and it had to be MADE load-bearing: with the inline path deliberately broken so it returns its subject untouched, SEVEN of the ten pairs still passed a `string(got) = string(want)` check, because `string(a datetime)` and the text it was parsed from are THE SAME CHARACTERS -- the finio OFX defect exactly. Parity is asserted on the TYPE, on `=`, and on the rendering. PRECEDENCE is a DIFFERENCE with a discriminating case (`{number}"12" + 1` is 13 inline and 121 if the modifier swallowed the sum, both ordinary numbers). The TOKEN tier is what the GRAMMAR tier cannot stand in for -- `--tokens` must show ONE MODIFIER_PREFIX carrying the NAME, with the CONTROL that a record literal still lexes as LBRACE, or "it is one token" is satisfied by a lexer that folded every brace. A failing SUBJECT reports the SUBJECT's cause: without the guard `print {USD}undefined_name` says "USD modifier expects a number or decimal text", the reports-the-wrong-cause class again. Six perturbations proven red, each caught by the tier written for it and the unfreed name by valgrind ALONE. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_optparams.sh   # PLAT-OPTPARAM literal default parameter values -- `function f(a, b = 10)`. gBASIC had STRICT ARITY and no way to express an optional argument, so every library wanting one paid in a second function name or an options record, and docs/finance_design.md was written AROUND the absence (its §6 is now marked superseded, which is the point: the limitation was removed rather than worked around, one day before Phase 1 would have shipped the workaround). LITERALS ONLY, and that is the design rather than a shortcut: an arbitrary default expression must be evaluated in SOME scope, and gBASIC has NO CLOSURES -- a default reading an enclosing variable would inherit the read-then-shadow rule run_core.sh exists for, and one reading an earlier parameter would need a defined evaluation order. A literal has nothing to see. It also disposes of the MUTABLE-DEFAULT bug by construction: an array or record default is a PARSE ERROR and gBASIC strings are immutable, so no default is a value that could be mutated into the next call -- a tier written to test that turned out to be untestable, which is the stronger result. Tiers: SEMANTICS (self-checking, because a default that silently fails to apply yields `nothing`, which prints as an ordinary word and which a golden would enshrine); REFUSALS (9 -- a gap before a required parameter, four non-literal default forms, and the three constructs that share `parameter_list` but cannot honour a default, since a modifier's parameters come from the invoking clause, a program block's from argv and a server handler's from the URL pattern; each asserts a nonzero exit, the message, AND that NOTHING RAN); ACTOR (the child is a fork+exec that never saw the call site -- the parent sends only the supplied arguments and the child fills the tail, rather than materializing defaults onto the wire where a child built from different source could disagree); GRAMMAR (bison must still report ZERO conflicts -- not decoration, since the project rejected `IDENT expression` as a statement form over 4 MEASURED conflicts, and `IDENT OP_EQ` inside a list of bare identifiers is exactly the shape that could collide with assignment); VALGRIND. THE LEAK IS THE LESSON: the new per-parameter-list `defaults` array must be freed in SIX places (function, modifier, program, watch, server item, modifier signature) and missing three of them turned 22 SUITES RED on their own valgrind tiers -- a few bytes per parse, invisible to every functional test. Two of the six were only found by valgrind naming the exact grammar line. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_continuation.sh # PLAT-CONT: a line break inside an unclosed `(`, `[` or `{` continues the statement. gBASIC had NO line continuation in any form, so every long expression was assembled -- `join([...], " ")` is the shape that showed up in the wild, once per `create table`. The mechanism needs no new syntax and no trailing marker to forget, because the brackets the author already wrote say where the statement ends; it is a LEXER change only and the grammar is UNTOUCHED (zero conflicts before and after). The load-bearing pair is EQUIVALENCE and TERMINATOR. EQUIVALENCE states transparency as a FACT rather than a claim: the same program written on one line and across many must produce a BYTE-IDENTICAL TOKEN STREAM with positions stripped, and identical stdout -- structural, so it cannot drift the way a golden of the multi-line form would, with a coverage floor since two EMPTY streams are also identical. TERMINATOR is THE CONTROL, and every other tier is satisfied without it by a lexer that suppressed newlines UNCONDITIONALLY: outside brackets a newline still terminates, the depth returns to zero when a bracket closes, and a bracket inside a STRING or a COMMENT is not depth at all -- the two places a character-counting implementation goes wrong and swallows the rest of the file. Other tiers: FORMS (every bracket kind in every position the grammar admits one, incl. blank lines and per-line trailing comments inside an argument list), MODES (the two STATEFUL lexer modes -- brace-modifier content, and `consider`, which recognises its branches by COLUMN -- since nothing in the continuation tiers writes either and neither would be noticed breaking), REFUSAL (an unclosed bracket at EOF NAMES the opener and its line, because the parser's own report blames "unexpected end of file" and that is the WRONG END of the file -- the mistake is where the bracket opened; one that runs into a following statement is reported there instead, which is the more common typo and already reads well), DEPTH (200 levels each on its own line, past the fixed-size 64-entry opener stack, under valgrind -- the stack is fixed by design since nesting that deep is pathological, so what matters is that going past it does not write out of bounds). Red-proofed: FORMS, EQUIVALENCE, MODES and REFUSAL all fail against the pre-change binary; TERMINATOR correctly PASSES there, being the control, and was proven red separately against a copy whose depth never decrements. Headless, GI-independent, never skips (bar valgrind)
./tests/run_ldap.sh       # the `ldap` module (docs/ldap_design.md) -- bind and search, and NOTHING else. Raised as a CROSS-REPO ASK by gdash, whose identity tier 2 is LDAP(S) bind against AD and which had no way to reach a directory at all (verified, not taken on trust: no ldap anywhere in the tree, no raw TCP client, no ldapsearch on the host). Built on libldap, so it is the relationship `smtp` has with libcurl rather than a from-scratch ASN.1 BER client -- that was the open feasibility question and checking it first is what made the ask answerable. THIS IS AN AUTHENTICATION PATH AND THE FAILURE THAT MATTERS IS NOT A CRASH: it is a bind that says the wrong thing. A directory that is DOWN reported as a bad password locks every user out while telling the operator nothing; a bad password reported as unreachable is worse. So THE LOAD-BEARING TIER IS DISTINGUISHABILITY -- invalid_credentials, unreachable and tls_failed must be three different answers, asserted as DIFFERENCES rather than values since any one alone is satisfied by a module that always says it. That is also why `bind` returns a VALUE rather than raising: a caller has to read `reason` to learn anything, so the two cannot be conflated by accident. TLS FAILURE AND NETWORK FAILURE ARE THE SAME RESULT CODE (-1, "Can't contact LDAP server") and the ONLY thing separating them is the DIAGNOSTIC, which carries the OpenSSL error for TLS and is NULL for a refused connection -- measured both ways before the mapping was written, and the detection is therefore a string test whose worst case is a TLS problem reported as `unreachable`, never a failure reported as success. AN EMPTY PASSWORD IS REFUSED rather than sent: LDAP treats it as an UNAUTHENTICATED BIND, the directory answers SUCCESS, and a caller that did not know would have logged the user in -- the classic way an LDAP login accepts everybody. `security` is REQUIRED with no default so a cleartext bind is a word somebody TYPED; referral chasing is OFF and NOT CONFIGURABLE, since on this path a referral is an instruction to send credentials to a server the operator never named. TESTED AGAINST A MOCK (tests/ldap/mock_ldap.py) that speaks genuine BER over a genuine socket -- libldap encodes and decodes against it exactly as against a directory, and the mock must parse real BindRequest/SearchRequest to answer; it HONOURS THE REQUESTED ATTRIBUTE LIST, which is not decoration, because without it asking for two attributes and asking for none look identical from the server side and the whole feature goes untested. THE LIMIT IS REAL AND STATED IN §8: this module has NEVER MET Active Directory or OpenLDAP, no such server is reachable from here, and a mock has no referrals, aliases, controls or size limits. A tier also asserts THE PASSWORD NEVER APPEARS on stderr or in any reported message. VALGRIND FOUND A REAL LEAK reading alone would not have: `append_to_array_ref` mutates in place AND returns an OWNED copy, so assigning its return reads like a fluent API and leaks a reference per element. Skips cleanly without libldap in the build, or without python3 or openssl -- AND THAT SKIP HAD NEVER ONCE FIRED (fixed 2026-10-09). It was `if ./gbasic probe.bas 2>&1 | grep -q "not available in this build"`, and under this suite's own `pipefail` A BUILD REFUSAL MAKES gbasic EXIT 1, so the pipeline reported 1 EVEN WHEN grep MATCHED and the condition could not become true. Measured on a build made without libldap: 10 of 11 checks FAILED, a red gate describing a correct build, which is what CI had been showing on every push since the suite shipped. It asks `tests/build_has.sh` now, which CAPTURES into a variable so there is no pipeline to mislead it. A SKIP PROBE IS THE WORST PLACE FOR THIS TRAP, being the one branch nobody ever watches work -- and sweeping for the shape afterwards found it a SECOND time, in run_web_tls.sh. AND MAKING THE SUITE RUN IN CI FOR THE FIRST TIME IMMEDIATELY FOUND A REAL DEFECT, which is the argument for the whole exercise: `tls_failed` was separated from `unreachable` by looking for SSL/TLS/certificate IN libldap's DIAGNOSTIC, and that text belongs to whichever TLS library the DISTRIBUTION built OpenLDAP against -- Ubuntu 24.04 ships a GnuTLS build, newer releases are back on OpenSSL now its licence is Apache-2.0. So the module's central claim, that an operator can tell a certificate problem from a restart, was TRUE on the developer machine and FALSE on 24.04, which is the worst shape a portability defect takes: invisible wherever it was written. It asks the NETWORK now -- a bounded TCP connect to the same host and port, only when TLS was wanted and the text said nothing, so if something accepts then the host is up and a SERVER_DOWN came from above the transport -- a fact about the world rather than a library's phrasing. MY FIRST DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG and is worth recording: I reproduced CI's two mismatches locally by killing the mock and concluded the FIXTURE was at fault, which proved only that it COULD produce that symptom and not that it did. The discriminator was in the same output all along -- `naming the CA makes the same certificate acceptable` PASSED, which can only happen if the TLS listener is live. The fixture fixes were real and are kept (both mocks required ready and NAMED SEPARATELY with the failing one's stderr printed, and OS-ASSIGNED ports, since the fixed 13911/13912/13999 sat under a comment promising to `fail loudly if busy` that nothing implemented); they were simply not the cause. THE CONTROL IS A DEAD HOST OVER TLS, added because the pre-existing unreachable case declares `plain` and so never exercised the probe at all -- a probe that always answered `accepted` would report every outage as a certificate problem, sending an operator to look at certificates during a plain outage, and every other check in the file would still pass. Four perturbations proven red
./tests/run_smtp.sh # PLAT-MAIL: composing and sending email (docs/mail_design.md). gBASIC could not send mail AT ALL, so a program needing a notification shelled out to `sendmail` -- a hard dependency on a local MTA, no way to reach an authenticated relay, and no error the program could read. THE SPLIT IS WHAT MAKES THE SUITE THOROUGH: composition is `stdlib/mail.bas`, pure gBASIC, so the whole of it is checkable WITH NO NETWORK (the run_web_routes lesson); the native `smtp` module owns only the wire. Each layer refuses at the boundary it can SEE -- headers there, SMTP commands here -- because a rule enforced in the wrong layer can be walked around. Tiers: COMPOSE (SELF-CHECKING not golden, 23 assertions each stating its own expected answer, because every defect here -- a body sent 7bit that needed base64, a Bcc reaching the headers, a boundary occurring inside a part -- produces a message that STILL LOOKS LIKE A MESSAGE and a golden would record it as expected); GOLDEN (byte-exact, with only the Message-ID and boundaries masked, Date pinned by the fixture and TZ=UTC by the runner); ORACLE, THE STRONGEST TIER (every composed message parsed by python3's `email` module, an INDEPENDENT implementation, asserted on what a mail CLIENT sees -- encoded-words decoding back to the author's text, a Date parsing to the right instant, attachment bytes surviving base64; validating with anything of ours would only prove we agree with ourselves, and the Date formatter is separately byte-identical to python's email.utils across all seven weekdays); REFUSAL (14 compose refusals, each beside its NEAREST LEGAL NEIGHBOUR, since a refusal suite with no control is satisfied by refusing everything); SEND-REFUSAL (15 envelope/configuration refusals needing no relay, plus a control that must get PAST validation and fail at the SOCKET); WIRE (through a sink recording exact bytes -- three things no functional test can see from outside: CRLF framing, DOT-STUFFING, and Bcc in RCPT TO but not in DATA); AUTH; REJECT; TLS; VALGRIND. THREE DEFECTS THE SUITE FOUND, none of which would have raised anything. (1) LIBCURL ALREADY DOT-STUFFS -- stuffing in smtp.c as well put THREE dots on the wire where the body had one, which the receiver un-stuffs to two: a corrupted message that is delivered, accepted and plausible. Found by reading the wire capture, not the docs. The tier therefore asserts the OUTCOME (the delivered body is byte-identical to what the program wrote) rather than who did the stuffing, and the fixture body is ASCII ON PURPOSE, since a non-ASCII body goes base64 and has NO line starting with a dot -- a UTF-8 fixture cannot exercise dot-stuffing at all and would pass on a build that never stuffed anything. (2) THE TRAILING NEWLINE is transport framing for 7bit and CONTENT for base64, so the same body decoded WITH a trailing newline in one encoding and WITHOUT it in the other -- a difference decided by a rule the author never sees. (3) `weekday` is ISO 1-7 (Mon..Sun), and a Sunday-first day table read with it is right FOR SATURDAY ALONE -- which is the day the first hand-check happened to fall on. Also added `zone_offset(dt)`, the one-argument form: a datetime carries no zone, so without it a program can read the wall clock and cannot say what it is offset from, making an RFC 5322 Date unwritable. Skips without python3 (the sink and the oracle) or without libcurl in the build; the TLS tiers skip without openssl. All loopback; nothing leaves the machine
./tests/run_parse_exit.sh   # a REPORTED parse error must fail the parse (DOGFOOD item 6). `yylex` signals a token the grammar cannot map by returning 0 -- end of file -- and bison cannot tell that from a real one. Inside a block the synthetic EOF lands where the grammar refuses it, which is the ONLY reason this looked like the exit-code inconsistency it was filed as; at TOP LEVEL the grammar accepts, so the file parsed as whatever preceded the bad token: the statements before it RAN, everything after it disappeared, and the exit was 0. Not `dim`-specific (any unlexable byte did it) and not cosmetic (the bare stderr line bypassed the diagnostics sink, so --json-diagnostics emitted a non-JSON line INTO a JSON stream -- Studio's own consumer). Four claims: LOCATED (every stderr line matches the located form, which is how a sink-bypassing line is caught generically rather than by naming the one we knew about), NONZERO, NOTHING_RAN (stdout empty -- the truncation is what made this dangerous, not the exit code), ONE_CAUSE (no follow-on blaming "unexpected end of file", which is a fiction the synthetic EOF invented). Plus a source tripwire that the raw fprintf is gone. Headless, never skips
./tests/run_process_lifetime.sh # "nothing this interpreter started outlives it" -- docs/reference.md says it in bold, and it was enforced by a teardown pass at the end of eval_program, which a SIGKILL never reaches (DOGFOOD item 4: four gBASIC children found sleeping two days after the runs that started them, three with their working directory already deleted). A promise about surviving a kill can only be kept by the KERNEL, so all three fork sites now arm PR_SET_PDEATHSIG between fork and exec, as the actor path has since it was written. Written from the observable side because there is no other honest way: start a child, kill -9 the parent, look for the child. Tiers: START, ABANDONED (the handle dropped, which is deliberately NOT lethal, so the child is running with nothing holding it -- the exact state the userspace sweep exists for and cannot reach under SIGKILL), RUN (killed mid-synchronous-child), CLEAN_EXIT (the control -- a normal exit still reaps and the program still works), EVERY_FORK_ARMS (counted against the fork sites, not grepped for the symbol: the failure this guards is a NEW fork site that forgets, which "is PR_SET_PDEATHSIG mentioned somewhere" cannot see). Each child carries a per-run marker in its argv so pgrep cannot match a stray from a concurrent suite, and the suite waits for the child to EXIST before killing anything -- a child that never started is trivially "gone" and would pass vacuously. NOTE the design doc had said process-lifecycle behaviour "does not fit a stdout golden test" and left it hand-verified, which is how the other two fork sites went unnoticed for two months: that was a statement about goldens, not about testability. Headless, never skips
./tests/run_web_handler_errors.sh # what a `server` block does when a HANDLER is wrong, which is the surface every other web suite is blind to: their fixtures are written by someone who knows the right answer, so none of them ever returns `{ body: 42 }`. `webserver_validate_response_value` has eighteen refusals, and until 2026-08-23 every one of them RAISED. That was correct for the path they were written for -- `append(server.responses, {...})`, where the program made the mistake in its own frame and the error names that line, which is what the seven `negative_webserver_response_*` cases pin -- but PLAT-WEB-5 pointed the same validator at a new caller without re-asking what its raises now MEAN. From a handler there is no such frame: the raise surfaced as `file:0:0` and ended a listener that docs/reference.md promises keeps serving, on the DEFAULT `workers: 1` where nothing restarts it. Each of the 17 response cases therefore asserts three things and the third is the one no existing test made: 500 on the wire, the reason on stderr, AND THE NEXT REQUEST ANSWERED -- a failure of the third names the malformed response that killed the listener. Two crossings beside them, because the other fixtures each test one facet alone: ECHO_HEADER (a query parameter reflected into a response header -- ordinary handler code, and the CRLF refusal that stops response splitting was, while fatal, a REMOTE kill switch: one request carrying %0d%0a and the server was gone; asserts the split never reaches the wire AND that refusing it is survivable) and CRASH_POOL (a raise inside a handler under `workers: 2`, the let-it-crash model §7b describes and which nothing exercised, since every other fixture runs unsupervised at workers: 1 -- it crashes MORE workers than the pool holds, because killing one proves nothing when the survivor answers instantly and a supervisor that respawns nothing looks identical). Both fixes were proven red by reverting them.
./tests/run_insight_calibration.sh # DOES THE THRESHOLD MEAN WHAT THE FINDING SAYS IT MEANS? A MANUAL tier, named when excluded, because CHECKING a calibration rather than trusting its formula costs ~250 searches over freshly generated null data and about five minutes -- which is exactly why nobody had done it. Every Finding used to assert `alpha: 0.05` and R1/R11/R12 and every `clears` verdict rest on it; MEASURED, the answer was NO -- 0.090 at 12 cells, 0.110 at 20, 0.143 at 40 against a requested 0.05, WORSENING as the search widened, the opposite of what a family-wise correction is for. Cause is TAIL WEIGHT, not skew (a change is a difference of two independent sums so skew cancels); the control is uniform data landing at 0.047, exactly alpha. The field is `alpha_requested` now -- the rename IS the retraction, since a Finding may state what was ASKED FOR and must not imply it was DELIVERED -- and `null.calibration` carries method/assumed/measured. THREE TIERS AND EACH NEEDS THE OTHERS: light-tailed through the t threshold lands at alpha (0.040), without which the next tier reads as "the correction is broken" rather than "its assumption is unmet"; heavy-tailed through the same threshold does not (0.093), which puts the DOCUMENTED LIMITATION ITSELF under test; and the permuted null on the same data fires LESS (0.080), asserted as a DIFFERENCE against the t null rather than as a small number -- because "the rate is small" also passes on a null that NEVER FIRES, which is the bug the first attempt shipped (sign-flipping leaves every |deviation| intact, so the threshold landed ABOVE the statistic it judged, 3.724 vs 3.309, and it fired 0 times in 200 trials). Hence the last tier: it still fires, it is a threshold and not a mute button. A FOURTH TIER ADDED 2026-09-04 MEASURES A DIFFERENT AXIS: every tier above it measures ONE search, and the correction is family-wise over the CELLS of one search, so a monthly process runs twelve families and pays for one. Measured 0.725 that an ordinary year raises a finding, 0.175 with `repetitions: 12` declared, and the tier asserts BOTH plus the honest third -- that it does NOT reach the requested 0.05. Adds ~50s. INSIGHT_CALIBRATION_FULL=1 raises the counts for a real measurement rather than a regression check
./tests/run_xml_bigfile.sh # WP-XML-6, a MANUAL tier deliberately outside the routine suites: it generates a >=100 MB document and takes tens of seconds. It proves the streaming CLAIM rather than the streaming API -- windowing a huge file with skip_to/subtree while sampling /proc/<pid>/status VmHWM from outside, and requiring the peak to stay under a fixed ceiling (64 MiB by default; BOUND_KB and TARGET_BYTES override). The loop sums a <value> out of every record so the whole file is genuinely walked and cannot be short-circuited, and a run that never managed to sample VmHWM FAILS rather than passing vacuously. What it catches is the one bug the functional xml suites cannot see: a reader that buffers the whole document and returns exactly the right answers. Invoke directly: ./tests/run_xml_bigfile.sh
GBASIC_POSTGRES_TEST=1 PGDATABASE=gbasic_test ./tests/run_postgres.sh   # opt-in. `sudo tools/setup_postgres_dev.sh` provisions the role and databases once (peer auth, no pg_hba edits). Carries postgres_arrays.bas since 2026-09-05: NATIVE POSTGRES ARRAYS BOTH WAYS, 50 checks WITH POSTGRES AS THE ORACLE -- an array parameter is read back through array_length/unnest/array_dims, so what is asserted is what the SERVER parsed rather than what our reader makes of our writer, and a round trip alone passes on a matched pair of bugs. THE AMBIGUITY THE DESIGN TURNS ON: `[["a","b"]]` is JSON for `$1::jsonb` and a literal for `acl && $1`, different wire text, and nothing in the call says which -- so a statement carrying an array parameter is PREPARED AND DESCRIBED and each array rendered by the type Postgres inferred for its position; every jsonb call that worked before still gets JSON (the CONTROL tier), untyped `$1` is still JSON text, records always JSON. 1.3x a bare query, only when an array is passed, gated at 5x. Elements convert by their own type (int8[]/numeric[] elements are STRINGS -- the exactness rule per element), NULL is nothing, 2-D is nested not flattened, and the quoted TEXT "NULL" is a string while bare NULL is not -- only quoting every element keeps those apart, which is why the writer always quotes. PROVEN RED on the pair that matters: backslash unescaped makes `back\\slash` arrive as `backslash`, a plausible string only the oracle and the round trip can see; also on arrays-as-JSON-regardless (the old behaviour) and a bare NULL read as text. THE FILE CHANGED JOBS THE SAME DAY: it was written in the morning as the NEGATIVE CONTROL for the limitation (an array result raised, a parameter went out as JSON, documented nowhere -- found checking the AI reference proposal against the tree, and the reasoned finding REVERSED when run: the jsonb/vector schema worked all along), went red the moment arrays landed that afternoon, and became the positive suite. That is the control doing exactly what it is for. Provisioning the database also found run_gbasic_site_postgres.sh had been RED since PLAT-WARN shipped (a discarded boolean from record_post_event tripped unused-result and the clean-stderr check) -- opt-in and unreachable, so nothing could see it
GBASIC_SITE_POSTGRES_TEST=1 PGDATABASE=... PGUSER=... ./tests/run_gbasic_site_postgres.sh  # opt-in: examples/gbasic_site/site_postgres.bas against a REAL PostgreSQL, and the only suite that touches the site's DATA layer at all -- run_gbasic_site.sh drives site.bas, which is the static-page prototype and holds no database, so every schema, migration and query defect is visible here or nowhere. It provisions the database from examples/gbasic_site/setup.bas, checks the seeded rows directly through `pg` (pages, categories, topics -- so a broken migration fails HERE rather than as a puzzling 404 later), then runs the site end to end through a python3 client three times over: the main suite, a per-IP anonymous-posting RATE LIMIT (a small limit set only for that run, which is why the main suite is unaffected -- the default leaves posting unlimited), and CSRF. Sessions are covered by SEEDING AN ALREADY-EXPIRED one rather than waiting for a clock, which is what makes expiry testable at all. Each case starts the server, waits for it to publish an EPHEMERAL port, drives it, has the client stop it via /shutdown, requires clean stderr, and diffs against a golden. NOTE it backs up and restores examples/gbasic_site/server_port.txt, because a developer's own checkout may have one. Skips unless GBASIC_SITE_POSTGRES_TEST=1 and PGDATABASE/PGUSER are set
```

`gbasic-lsp` is deliberately kept out of the default `all` target, so a routine
test pass must build it explicitly or it will silently rot. Use `make dev`
(builds every binary: `gbasic`, `libgbasic.a`, and `gbasic-lsp`) as the developer/
CI entry point; `tests/lsp/run_lsp.sh` also runs `make gbasic-lsp` itself.

`run_examples.sh` runs a hardcoded list of cases (it does not auto-discover), so
when adding an example test, add its filename to that list as well as creating
the `.out`. To run one case by hand: `./gbasic examples/foo_test.bas` and diff
against `examples/foo_test.out`. Module runners skip rather than fail when their
native dependency is missing. GUI testing is manual (needs a display).

## Conventions

- Optional-dependency code must stay behind its `#if HAVE_*` guard and degrade to
  a runtime error when the feature is compiled out. **And the inverse, which is
  easier to get wrong because the ordinary build hides it**: code that is NOT
  optional must stay OUT of those guards. `with principal` was written next to
  `http_multi`, which is inside `#if HAVE_LIBCURL`, so the whole feature vanished
  from a build without libcurl — caught only by `run_packaging.sh`, which builds
  lean. `make LIBCURL_AVAILABLE=0` (or the matching `HAVE_*` variable) is the
  cheap check before trusting a green gate — **and it no longer needs
  `make clean` in front of it**, which that advice used to carry. No object
  depended on the switch values, so changing one rebuilt NOTHING; a
  `.buildswitch-stamp` is a prerequisite of every object compiled with CFLAGS
  now. That mattered more from 2026-10-11, when `src/builtins.c`'s registry
  became conditional so `has_builtin` would answer for THIS build: a stale
  `builtins.o` made it LIE ABOUT THE BINARY IT WAS IN, which is the defect that
  change was made to remove. Measured — after `make LIBCRYPTO_AVAILABLE=0
  ZLIB_AVAILABLE=0` with no clean, `has_builtin("sha256")` still answered true.
- Design docs in `docs/` may describe unimplemented future work; check the
  implementation-status documents (and `eval.c`) before treating a proposal as a
  shipped feature. `docs/historical_development_archive.md` records completed
  phases.
- The `use`/`--add-uses` syntax is legacy and retained only for compatibility;
  prefer `load`/`--add-loads`.

## House rules

Identical to `AGENTS.md` — the two entry files carry the same rules:

- **Before writing gBASIC code**, read `docs/ai/START-HERE.md` and follow it
  (`UNLEARN.md` first). gBASIC diverges from QBasic/VB intuition in ways that fail
  silently.
- **When you work around a gBASIC limitation or surprise**, append an entry to
  `/DOGFOOD.md` using its template *before continuing*.
- **Evidence standards:** tests-first where feasible; keep goldens byte-exact (a
  behavioral change that moves a golden is a deliberate, listed rebaseline);
  measure, don't assume; report what you could not verify. Never mark anything
  "verified".

## gBASIC Studio

Studio lives in its own project: `~/development/gbasic-studio`. It is not built
here and its libraries are not in this `stdlib`. What DID stay is everything that
was general rather than Studio-specific — `persist` (crash-safe versioned
persistence) and `filetree` (a directory as a value tree), both extracted from
Studio and documented in the cookbook. Studio consumes this repo's platform
surface (`source_outline`, `try_decode`, `process.*`, `--line-buffered`,
`print to error`, `atomic_replace`, the `qt` module and its `window` declarations) and
finds it via `GBASIC_PATH`; nothing here depends on Studio.

## EDGAR suite work
Governed by docs/edgar_suite_development_plan.md. One WP per session,
assigned by Matthew. Follow its §1 session protocol exactly: read only
the WP's Read list, stop at the WP boundary, record verbatim evidence in
docs/PROGRESS.md. Never mark anything `verified`. No network in tests.
